them; `validate_document` returns ids marked *not yet issued*, and `explain` says so for an id
that was never issued. Ids are designed to be citable, but v1 promises no availability; the ids
of a withdrawn release resolve to *withdrawn*, and erased derivations to *erased* (§12.2).

### 7.7 Readback

The server renders a deterministic, plain-language readback of every canonical cohort and view
from templates, as segments (§8.1): template text, and data tokens for labels, values and units.
A readback states every path step and step condition, quantifier and lift rule, the units of
every numeric constant, every record filter and parent scope, every negation, and what is
excluded. For example: *"Patients in GBM (TCGA PanCan) @3 with a TP53 mutation in some tumour
sample. Patients with no TP53 mutation in any assessed tumour sample, and some tumour sample not
assessed for TP53, are unknown and not counted."* Pack summaries (§7.3) follow the readback,
labelled as such. Readbacks are returned with every result and cohort count and shown next to
every figure.

A readback is a function of the canonical form and the release's descriptors alone, rendered in
the canonical order: two documents with one canonical form have one readback, but for the words of
pack summaries, which packs render from their leaves as written. Every label, id, constant, unit,
key (each part of a composite one) and the release's label is a data token; the template text
holds nothing from data or the document. For each down step it states the coverage the question
relies on (every row's children recorded, recorded only for the listed rows and by which scope
columns, or undeclared, and whether it is only proposed), its record filter and its parent scope;
it ends by saying that a unit whose answer cannot be decided is unknown and not counted, and pack
summaries follow, each labelled as the pack's summary of its leaf in the conditions whose leaf keys
it names, and ordered by them, never by the leaf's pointer, which holds the cohort's name. Lists
are cut: at most 64 constants of a `values` list, keys of an `ids` leaf or values of a scope, then
how many more there are (D296).

---

## 8. Result contract

### 8.1 Envelope

```jsonc
{
  "derivation": {
    "id": "drv:…",
    "document": { /* canonical form */ },
    "analysis": { "id": "survival.km", "version": "1.0.0" },
    "releases": [ { "dataset": "trial_xyz", "label": 3, "manifest": "sha256:…", "status": "published | draft" } ],
    "packs": { "onco": { "version": "1.2.0", "results_version": 3 } },
    "semantics_version": 1,
    "disclosure": { "min_cell_count": 5 },       // effective setting (§8.4)
    "engine": "aibi 0.8.0"
  },
  "issuance": { "id": "iss:…", "cache_hit": false, "values_from": "iss:…" },
  "source": { "document": { /* as written */ },
              "leaves": { "<JSON Pointer into the document as written>": ["leaf:…", …] },
              "params": { /* echoed */ } },
  "digest": "sha256:…",
  "cohorts": [ { "position": 0, "id": "drv:…", "reference": true }, … ],   // view order
  "population": [ { "n_true": 0, "n_false": 0, "n_unknown": 0, "unknown_by_reason": { … },
                    "unknown_by_leaf": { "leaf:…": 0 }, "lift_differs": 0,
                    "suppressed": [ "<JSON Pointer>", … ] } ],                           // by position
  "analysed":   [ { "n": 0, "excluded": { "NOT_COVERED": 0, … }, "excluded_units": 0 } ], // by position
  "values": { "positions": [ { … } ], "view": { … } },
  "caveats": [ Caveat ],
  "readback": { "cohorts": [ [Segment, …] ], "view": [Segment, …] },
  "labels": [ { "data": "<cohort name>" }, … ],
  "charts": [ /* Vega-Lite specifications, §8.5 */ ]
}
```

- `values.positions` holds per-cohort values in view order; `values.view` holds values that
  belong to the view as a whole (omnibus tests, q-values, effect sizes).
- `analysed` holds, per position, `{n, excluded, excluded_units}` for the analysis's unit set
  (complete cases in `survival.cox`, which has no `variables` and gives each covariate's, the
  stratum's and the endpoint's counts in its values, D363; units with valid endpoint data in
  `survival.km`). For views
  over several columns or predicates, it counts the units analysed for at least one of them and
  adds `variables`: one `{n, excluded, excluded_units}` per column or predicate, in parameter
  order (two or more); no variable's `n` exceeds the overall `n`. `excluded` counts a unit under each of its
  reasons and lists every exclusion reason, zeros included; `excluded_units` counts each excluded
  unit once, so `n` + `excluded_units` is the position's `n_true`. A variable that counts rows
  (`count: "rows"`, §9.2) counts units here too: `n` the units whose rows it pooled, those that
  reached none included, and `excluded` those its pooling excluded; the rows it leaves out are
  counted by reason in its values (`excluded_rows`, D378). A variable's memberships (`each`,
  §9.2) count a unit in `n` where its answer for some category its cohort lists is known, and
  exclude it otherwise under the reasons of all its answers, so its `analysed` depends on the
  categories listed: a unit whose default is known but whose UNKNOWN answers are for every
  category its cohort lists is excluded at that position and known at one that lists another,
  for which it is FALSE (D382); each category's UNKNOWN units are counted by reason in its
  proportion's `excluded`. Under *k* the memberships' `n`, `excluded_units` and `excluded`, and
  their entry of `variables` beside other variables, are `null` whatever the counts, since they
  combine the categories, and each category's `excluded` is `null` too (§8.4, D383). A suppressed
  count in
  `analysed` is `null` with `not_estimable` reason `suppressed`; in `population`, `suppressed`
  lists the null members by JSON Pointers relative to the entry (e.g. `"/n_false"`).
- A result has one to six cohorts. `reference` is true for the reference position of an analysis
  that declares `uses_reference`, and false everywhere else.
- A release's `label` is its number, or `"draft"` exactly when its `status` is `draft`; `releases`
  lists each dataset once, by dataset id. An issuance's `values_from` is its own id exactly when
  it is not a cache hit; an output over a draft is never a cache hit (§12.3).
- **Caveats in outputs** affect the digested parts only: `/cohorts`, `/population`, `/analysed` and
  `/values`, and, for `DRAFT_RELEASE`, `/derivation/releases`. The checks an output makes of its
  caveats read those parts alone, never the documents or parameters echoed beside them. Caveats are
  sorted by code, affected paths, severity and message (each segment by kind, data before text, then
  its string, then truncation), strings compared as UTF-16 code units, and exact duplicates are
  dropped; `affects` and `suppressed` are sorted the same way. An absent optional member of an
  output is omitted, never written as `null`.
- **Segments.** A `Segment` is `{"text": "<server-written text>"}` or `{"data": "<text from data
  or a document>", "truncated": true?}`. Values, numbers included, appear in readbacks and
  messages as data tokens holding strings; a data token is cut at 200 characters and then marked
  `truncated`. A data token writes each lone surrogate and noncharacter its string holds as its
  `\uXXXX` (or `\UXXXXXXXX`) escape and is cut only between escapes, so every token is Unicode
  text whatever it was given; the escapes are for display, never parsed back (D398). Quoting and
  escaping are applied when rendering, never stored. A `text` segment
  holds only the server's own words: its literals and constants, numbers, and enumeration and
  `Literal` values; everything else is a data token, one value or id to a token (§14, D397), except
  the sites D397 lists as known exceptions until M4.0f-A2b. A `text` segment is made only by
  `text()`, passes only as the exact instance it is, and is rebuilt from bytes only at a listed
  boundary; the other ways in that Pydantic offers are refused by their own validator, and a dump
  validates what it writes again, so that a mapping put in a segment's place after an output was
  built is refused too. This holds against data and against a mistake in the server's own code,
  not against hostile code in the tree (D399).
- **Data wrapper.** Outside segments, every string in an output that comes from data or from a
  document (labels, values, names, notes) is carried as `{"data": "<text>"}` and marked
  `"x-aibi-data": true` in the JSON Schema. The embedded documents (`derivation.document`,
  `source.document`) are carried verbatim, and their whole schema nodes are marked
  `x-aibi-data` (A6).
- **Cache and issuances.** The result cache stores only the digested content, keyed by result id.
  `source`, readbacks, labels, pack summaries and charts are rendered for each issuance. An
  issuance served from the cache says so and names the issuance whose SQL produced the values.
  `explain` returns the SQL recorded for an issuance (§12.2); it is not part of `run_analysis`
  responses.
- **Cohort counts** (`count_cohort`, §11.1) are `{id, digest, population, size, disclosure,
  readback, caveats, releases, issuance}` per cohort. `size` is the cohort's size as a proportion
  of the unit table (§8.2), with `denominator_definition` `{position: null, predicate: null,
  counts: "unit_table"}` and no `excluded`, since nothing is excluded from the unit table; its
  `denominator`, the unit table's size, is suppressed only when that is from 1 to *k* − 1 (D297).
  `disclosure` is the effective setting, `{min_cell_count}` (§8.4). Caveats of a cohort count have
  `affects: ["/population"]`, and never concern analyses or several cohorts (`COHORTS_OVERLAP`,
  `CONFOUNDED_WITH_DATASET`, `INVALID_EXCLUDED`). A count over an empty unit table carries
  `NOT_ESTIMABLE` for its size (D297). `count_cohort` returns one per cohort, by cohort name,
  each beside its name (as data) and the map from its leaves as written to its leaf keys, with
  the views checked as `validate_document` checks them and the parameters used (D300, D317).
- **Digests are checked.** A result envelope or a cohort count whose `digest` is not the digest of
  the digested members it carries cannot be built (D298).
- **Catalogue statistics** (row counts, value distributions, observation-state counts, and the
  counts in the curation queue) carry a release-scoped reference
  `stat:<manifest hash>/<descriptor id><JSON Pointer>` (the pointer supplies the `/`), with
  `?floor=<n>` (2 ≤ n ≤ 2^53 − 1) appended when a deployment floor (§8.4) applies. The pointer is
  written in its URI fragment form (RFC 6901 §6): percent-encoded from UTF-8 with upper-case hex,
  `?`, `#` and `%` included, so a reference has exactly one spelling and is compared as a string.
  The assistant cites it like a derivation id. The pointer addresses the descriptor's statistics
  as disclosed (`/n_rows` of a table; `/states/<STATE>`, `/categories/<value>`, `/pooled`,
  `/bins/<i>`, `/min` and `/max` of a column; `/report/<i>` of the dataset for the *i*-th note of
  the import report that the curation queue counts), and the floor is appended whenever the
  deployment sets one (D272).

### 8.2 Numbers, proportions and effect sizes

- Results contain only what JSON text carries unchanged: finite numbers within ±(2^53 − 1), and
  Unicode text; a statistic beyond that range is refused or reported in other units, and an
  integer beyond it inside `values` is a decimal string (§5.1). A number that cannot be computed
  is `null`, with its
  reason in the enclosing object's `not_estimable` map, keyed by a JSON Pointer relative to that
  object, e.g. `{"estimate": 29.0, "ci": {"low": 21.4, "high": null}, "not_estimable":
  {"/ci/high": "not_reached"}}`; the map is omitted when nothing is missing. Any number may be
  not estimable. The reasons are an enum:
  `no_units`, `no_events`, `zero_denominator`, `zero_variance`, `not_reached`,
  `beyond_follow_up`, `separation`, `not_converged`, `degenerate_table`, `overlapping_cohorts`,
  `confounded_with_dataset`, `suppressed`.
- Every proportion is an object, never a bare number:

```jsonc
{
  "estimate": 0.4119601328903654,
  "numerator": 124,
  "denominator": 301,
  "denominator_definition": { "position": 0, "predicate": "leaf:…", "counts": "known" },   // counts: "known" | "unit_table" | "rows"
  "denominator_text": [ /* segments; outside the digest */ ],
  "excluded": { "NOT_COVERED": 17, "NO_INFORMATION": 3, "NOT_ASSESSED": 0, … },   // every reason
  "ci": { "method": "wilson", "level": 0.95, "low": 0.358, "high": 0.468 }
}
```

  Survival-function estimates are not proportions and are exempt. The estimate is the numerator
  over the denominator, as a double; rounding happens only before hashing (§9.3). A count is
  `null` only when suppressed, and the estimate and its interval are suppressed exactly when a
  count is. Over a zero denominator the estimate is not estimable: `no_units` where no unit was
  counted, as §9.5 orders the reasons, else `zero_denominator`. `excluded`, where a proportion
  has one, lists every exclusion reason, and a suppressed one is `null`. An interval's `level` is
  between 0 and 1, exclusive, and its `method` is an identifier, as the analysis entry names its
  methods.
- Every effect size names its measure (an enum: `risk_difference`, `risk_ratio`,
  `proportion_difference`, `mean_difference`, `median_difference`, `hazard_ratio`), its position
  and its reference position. Differences are position minus reference; ratios are position over
  reference:

```jsonc
{ "measure": "hazard_ratio", "position": 1, "versus": 0, "estimate": 1.8,
  "ci": { "method": "wald", "level": 0.95, "low": 1.3, "high": 2.5 } }
```

- Whenever a number other than a suppressed one is not estimable, the result carries
  `NOT_ESTIMABLE`. Nothing is extrapolated.

### 8.3 Caveats

`Caveat = {code, severity: "info" | "warn" | "block", message: [Segment, …], affects: [JSON
Pointers relative to the output's root]}`. Codes are a stable, documented enum; core codes are
unprefixed and pack codes are namespaced (`onco.DRIVER_ANNOTATION_PIN`). Every code, core or
pack, declares its severity. The core set:

| Code | Severity | Raised when |
|---|---|---|
| `UNKNOWN_EXCLUDED` | warn | A cohort, denominator or analysis excluded units or rows because they were UNKNOWN |
| `INVALID_EXCLUDED` | warn | An analysis excluded rows or units with invalid values (§5.8) |
| `SCOPE_PARTIAL` | warn | A value depended on closedness restricted to listed scope tuples (flag, §6.3, §6.5) |
| `COVERAGE_PROPOSED` | warn | A value depended on proposed coverage (flag, §6.3, §6.5); the message names the relationship |
| `UNCONFIRMED_SEMANTICS` | warn | A descriptor field that canonicalisation or evaluation read is `imported_default`, `proposed` or `undeclared` (§5.1); the message lists the fields |
| `UNMAPPED_COMPARISON` | warn | A cross-dataset query matched references by name under `unmapped: "allow"` |
| `TIME_ORIGIN_MISMATCH` | **block** | Time-based values were compared across datasets whose time origins are undeclared or differ |
| `CONFOUNDED_WITH_DATASET` | warn | Some between-cohort contrasts could not be estimated because cohort membership coincides with dataset (§7.5) |
| `COHORTS_OVERLAP` | warn | Cohorts in a view share units; no between-cohort value was computed (§7.4) |
| `SMALL_N` | warn | Units analysed or events fell below the analysis's minimums, events per parameter below 10, or expected counts below 5 in a table tested with chi-squared |
| `PH_VIOLATED` | warn | The proportional-hazards test failed; the hazard ratio is an average over time |
| `DRAFT_RELEASE` | warn | The output was computed against a curation session's draft release |
| `SUPPRESSED` | info | Values were suppressed by the disclosure settings (§8.4) |
| `NOT_ESTIMABLE` | info | A number other than a suppressed one could not be estimated (§8.2) |
| `LIFT_DIFFERS` | info | The other lift rule would change some units' results, of a cohort or of a predicate a view's parameters ask (D323); the message gives the count (subject to §8.4), in the pack's wording where a pack provides one |
| `POOLED_ACROSS_DATASETS` | info | The output reports a value pooled across datasets |

MCP tool descriptions MUST tell clients that `warn` and `block` caveats have to be shown to the
user. A `block` caveat means the output is returned for inspection but MUST NOT be presented as
an answer.

### 8.4 Disclosure settings

- Each dataset descriptor has `disclosure: {min_cell_count, allow_row_ids}`: `min_cell_count`
  (*k*) is `null` (off) by default and at least 2 when set; `allow_row_ids` defaults to true. Being
  part of the descriptor, the settings are part of the release. A deployment may set a floor for
  `min_cell_count` (in its configuration, D253). The effective *k* of an output is the largest of the floor and the settings of
  every dataset the output draws on; it is part of the ids and references of §7.6 and §8.1, and
  results and cohort counts state it.
- **Disclosure classes** (D353). Every analysis is in one of two classes, which the core states for
  it (a pack never states its own; any later way for a pack's values to be disclosed is one the core
  verifies): **disclosed**, whose output the pass below protects by the rule it cites
  (`compare.existence` by D320, `summary.distribution` by D329, `compare.columns` by D337), so its
  view runs under *k* and the pass applies; and **refused**, whose output is nothing the pass can
  protect: a list of units (`summary.members`, D332), what a pack computes from its members' values
  (every pack's analysis, D344), or values that give counts no rule of this section protects
  (`survival.km`, and `survival.cox` when it comes, D351). Under any effective *k*, a deployment's
  floor included, whatever its cohorts' sizes, a view of a refused analysis is refused in phase 2
  (D317) with `WITHHELD_UNDER_K` at `/views/<i>/analysis`, before anything else phase 2 checks of it
  (a view whose parameters or cohorts phase 1 refuses reports those first), so that
  `validate_document` lists it, `count_cohort` refuses it, no query runs and the analysis's pack
  code (its requirement predicates and `run`) is not called; the refusal names the analysis, the
  setting that binds (the dataset's `min_cell_count`, a draft's latest published release's (D275) or
  the deployment's floor) and why, never a value, and offers `count_cohort` and the disclosed core
  analyses; applicability calls it `unavailable`, naming `min_cell_count` (§9.4). Apart from *k*, an
  analysis that lists or hands each member's values in the keys' order (`summary.members`, every
  pack's) is refused where its dataset allows no row ids (`ROW_IDS_NOT_ALLOWED` there, checked
  first) and `unavailable` naming `allow_row_ids`. An analysis leaves the refused class only with a
  rule in this section that names what it shows under *k* and, in the same change, a brute force
  whose bounds its decision states, of the form of D329's, D337's and D351's (every way small
  cohorts of the analysis's shape split, for *k* from 2 to 5), that nothing it shows pins a count
  from 1 to *k* − 1 beyond what the Limits below disclaim; a new analysis states its class when it
  is planned. A disclosed analysis may withhold a form of its parameters under any *k*, at that
  form's member (D379): `summary.distribution` withholds a variable's `count: "rows"`, since a row
  total sums each unit's number of rows, a statistic of values (D329), and a row count of *k* or
  more can be one unit's. A variable's memberships (`each: "category"`) are disclosed by the rule
  below (*Memberships*, D383), but where the descriptors may bound the rows each unit reaches,
  which they decide rather than a member's path, they are withheld: a column that is not a list
  runs under *k* only where its path is proven open per unit: the path is one down step from
  the unit table (so that the step's parent is the unit), not a relationship of the unit table
  to itself (whose child rows are the units, so the view's own population bounds them), and
  that step is open, every set of the child table's columns that the gate keeps unique (its
  primary key, the parent columns of a relationship into it and the child columns of a
  one-to-one relationship from it, D230) holding a free column: one stored, of datatype
  `number`, `integer`, `string`, `category`, `date`, `datetime` or `time_offset`, that no check
  of the gate restricts (no relationship's child column, in no record filter on its table) and
  that no derivation the gate checks reads (one in such a set, a foreign key or a record filter),
  so that the unit's rows may differ in it without end. Every other path is taken as bounded, a
  longer one included, since a later child's unique set may lie within columns each unit fixes
  along the path (the unit's key carried down a composite foreign key) whatever an earlier step
  allows; a per-unit proof over several steps is #75's. There the categories' TRUE counts may sum
  to at most some number of times the units, which pins one the rule hides, so
  `summary.distribution` withholds them, by a check of its own (`distribution.withheld_under_k`).
  Under any effective *k*, whatever its cohorts' sizes, a view that gives one is refused in phase
  2 with `WITHHELD_UNDER_K` at the first such member in the columns' order
  (`/views/<i>/params/columns/<j>/count` or `…/each`), before anything else phase 2 checks of its
  variables, naming the setting that binds and why, never a value, and offering the forms that run
  under *k* in its place: for `count: "rows"`, the aggregates its column takes, `count` (each
  unit's rows as its value) with `bins`, a number's `max`, `min` and `mean` with them where the
  column declares no range, an ordered category's `max` and `min`, and `some` and `every` with
  `values`; for `each`, `some` with `values`, and `every` with them where the path allows it (not
  of a scope column of the last step's child row, which `every` may not mention, §6.5), as
  resolution offers them, each a question whose split the pass protects as a variable's (D329),
  one at a time; never the view without `count` or `each`, whose column is multi-valued and takes
  an aggregate, nor `count: "rows"`, nor `each` where it is withheld, which no refusal under *k*
  offers. The analysis stays disclosed, and `available` (§9.4). What the pass guarantees is
  bounded by those Limits: nothing in one output, beside a cohort's `not`, `known` and `unknown`
  in one call, pins a suppressed count to one value (D297, D320), but under *k* = 2.
- With *k* set, a disclosure pass runs on every output after it is computed. A suppressed value
  becomes `null` with `not_estimable` reason `suppressed` (in `population`, its pointer is listed
  in `suppressed`), and the output carries `SUPPRESSED`. The pass applies these rules:
  - **Linked counts.** In each linked set, a count from 1 to *k* − 1 is suppressed, and if exactly
    one count of the set is suppressed, the smallest non-zero other count (the first in the set's
    listed order on a tie) is suppressed too. The linked sets are: `n_true`, `n_false` and
    `n_unknown`, whose total, the size of the unit table, is a member (cohort counts, below);
    each predicate's split of a cohort's units, with `analysed` (predicates of a result, below);
    a column's observation-state counts (with `n_true`, or with `n_rows` in
    catalogue statistics); a column's histogram bins or category counts (with its PRESENT count);
    and `lift_differs` alone, which is always suppressed, a 0 included: the units it counts lie
    on one side of `n_true`, `n_false` and `n_unknown`, which `not`, `known` or `unknown` of the
    cohort counts beside the rest, so shown beside any counts it could bound a suppressed one. A
    count of 0 is otherwise shown. The pass runs after categories are pooled and
    histogram bins are merged (below), covers the whole output and repeats until nothing changes: a
    count suppressed in one place is suppressed wherever the same count appears (e.g. `n_true` and
    `size.numerator`); a suppressed total of a linked set counts as a suppressed member of that set;
    and a breakdown is `null` whenever its total is suppressed (`unknown_by_reason` and
    `unknown_by_leaf` with `n_unknown`, `analysed.excluded` with `excluded_units`, observation-state
    counts with `n_true` or `n_rows`). A value that is not estimable keeps its reason: the pass
    suppresses only values that were computed; but a value whose counts are suppressed is
    `suppressed` whatever else would have made it not estimable, since `no_units` or
    `zero_denominator` beside it would give a suppressed count away as 0 (D320).
  - **Breakdowns.** A per-reason or per-leaf map with any count from 1 to *k* − 1 is replaced by
    `null` as a whole. In catalogue statistics, categories with any cell from 1 to *k* − 1 are
    pooled into one *suppressed categories* row; if that row still has such a cell, the
    distribution is suppressed (D271). In a result, a variable's categories are merged in their
    listed order as histogram bins are (below), so that a merged row's count is never read off
    as a pooled row's can be, and a category column's values that it does not declare are one
    row, after the declared ones, that names none of them, since a value held by few units is
    itself what they hold (D329).
  - **Derived statistics** (proportions, intervals, effect sizes, tests; survival analyses are
    refused, below) are suppressed with any count they are computed from. Suppressed tests leave the multiple-testing family, and the
    result says how many did.
  - **Distributions.** Histograms take their bin edges from the analysis parameters, or divide
    the column's declared `range` (§5.4) into *B* equal-width bins (*B* set by the analysis
    version; 10 in catalogue statistics); never from the data. Without either they are refused.
    Bins are [eᵢ, eᵢ₊₁), the last closed on the right; values outside the edges fall into open
    *below* and *above* bins. Repeatedly, the leftmost bin with 1 to *k* − 1 units is merged,
    together with any empty bins between them, with the nearest non-empty bin on its right (on
    its left when none is), until no bin has 1 to *k* − 1 units or one non-empty bin remains;
    empty bins are otherwise kept (D329: merging the smallest first, toward the neighbour with
    fewer units, gave the order it merged in away, and with it the counts merged). Minima and
    maxima are not reported; medians and quartiles are reported as the merged bin that contains
    them, read off the merged bins' counts, and not at all where the two values a quantile lies
    between are in two bins. No other statistic computed from the values themselves (a mean, a
    standard deviation) is reported: values are not counts, so the rule that hides counts from 1
    to *k* − 1 cannot protect them, and a mean over coarse bins gives the counts finer bins hide,
    a standard deviation of zero every unit's value (D329).
    Catalogue statistics follow the same rules, and pool categories with fewer
    than *k* units; a column's distribution is suppressed whole while its PRESENT count is, the
    pooled row of a list column, whose rows count under several values, has no count, and no
    curation evidence, which may quote counts, is served (D271). Descriptor text (labels,
    definitions, descriptions and pack extensions) is declared content and served as written, so
    no importer writes counts or other values computed from the rows into it (§10.1, D271).
  - **Memberships** (D383). Under *k*, a variable's memberships (`each: "category"`, §9.2) list
    only the categories the column declares (`false` and `true` for a boolean, a filtered
    column's allowed values), in their order, zeros included, whatever its units hold, both
    engines listing those alone (D384); there is no row of its other values, so the rows, and
    whether they pass `MAX_CATEGORIES`, depend on the descriptors alone, and no value it does not
    declare, nor its key, is named. Each row is disclosed as `some` of its one category is as a
    variable of its own (D329): its split of the position's units into those for which the
    category is known (the proportion's denominator) and those for which it is UNKNOWN is shown
    whole or not at all, either part from 1 to *k* − 1 hiding both; its TRUE units (the
    numerator) are shown only with it, and where neither its TRUE nor its FALSE units are from 1
    to *k* − 1, as a question's two categories merge; its estimate goes with both (`no_units`
    where the denominator shown is 0); its `excluded` is `null` whatever its counts; a position
    whose `n_true` is suppressed shows no row's counts, its rows still listed; rows are not
    merged; and the variable's `analysed` (`n`, `excluded_units`, `excluded`, and its `variables`
    entry) is `null`, since it combines the categories. Why not D320's split: a unit's answer for
    every category none of its rows holds is its default (D381), so a category that no member
    holds splits the units as the defaults do, and bounds every other category's FALSE and
    UNKNOWN units from above; a numerator shown beside a suppressed split, which D320 shows, then
    pins the split (example (a): 10 units under *k* = 3; beside a category split 0, 3 and 7, one
    split 5, 3 and 2 shows 5 alone, and of the splits with 5 TRUE only its UNKNOWN 2 reads so; on
    a category column that declares no values any constant is allowed, §6.4, so a value no unit
    holds gives the default split without a declared one). Why no maps: maps of categories that
    share defaults pin one another's reasons (example (b): 8 units under *k* = 3; a category no
    unit holds, 3 known and 5 UNKNOWN, has its map suppressed beside another's 4 UNKNOWN all for
    no information, shown, so the one unit UNKNOWN for the first and holding the second has its
    not-assessed count pinned at 1). Where the descriptors bound the rows each unit reaches (a
    path not proven open, above: one row on a one-to-one path, or where some set of the
    child's columns the gate keeps unique lies within its foreign key and columns a record
    filter fixes, a value map derives onto one value or another relationship holds; two where
    a boolean completes one; and on a longer path, where the last child is keyed within the
    unit's key, however many rows an earlier step reaches), the TRUE counts sum to at most that
    many times the units, and shown ones bound one the rule hides (10 units under *k* = 3, one
    row each: a, b and c held by 5, 4 and 1 each show 10 known, with TRUE 5, 4 and hidden,
    which 0 would not be, so it is 1; two rows each, four categories held by 10, 5, 1 and 4: 20 −
    19 gives the hidden 1); numerators alone (D297's rule) pin it too, so such memberships are
    withheld (above). The alternatives the refusal offers, asked together in one view, bound one
    another the same way, which the Limits disclaim (variables of the same rows), so the
    withholding keeps the rule's own claim true, not more. Nothing shown pins a count of a
    category's split, its reasons, or the units unknown for every category, to one value from 1
    to *k* − 1, for the variable alone or beside variables and cohorts whose paths share no
    relationship and no coverage table with it, where its path is one open down step from the
    unit, not a relationship of the unit table to itself (so that a unit may hold any set of the
    declared categories), and units' answers are independent, but under *k* = 2 (D383's brute
    force), and for rows the gate bounds: a pack's validator, which sees an import's data, may
    refuse rows by a rule no descriptor states, which the claim does not cover.
  - **Comparisons** (D337): a table of cohorts by categories is disclosed position by position,
    each position's categories merged as a distribution's are (above), so that a comparison and a
    distribution of one column in one call show the same rows; what the view adds is computed
    from the rows shown alone: its table's rows are the coarsest grouping of the listed
    categories of which every shown position's merged rows are unions, each count a sum of rows
    shown, and a test or a difference that needs a position whose variable's split is not shown
    is suppressed. Of numbers, only each position's count of units with a value is shown: means,
    standard deviations and medians, tests of any kind (a rank test counts the pairs of units
    ordered one way, which beside a histogram places units within a merged bin), differences and
    their intervals, a bootstrap's included (its bounds are differences of resampled medians,
    values units hold), are suppressed, and suppressed tests leave the family.
  - **Survival analyses** (`survival.km`, `survival.cox`) are refused under any *k*, the floor's
    included, whatever their cohorts' sizes, and applicability calls them `unavailable` there (§9.4,
    D351, D353). A curve's value at a time is a product over the event times before it, each factor
    one less the events over the units at risk, so its values can give the units at risk at an event
    time, and differences of those the censorings between event times: counts below *k* that the
    grid rule this bullet held until v0.8.19 showed while every count it listed was at least *k* (17
    units, 5 events and one censoring by month 1, then 5 events: the curve's 12/17 and 72/187 are
    reached only with 11 at risk at month 2, so one censoring). A rule that also merges intervals on
    their censorings might protect them; until one is specified and checked by brute force, survival
    is refused. A Cox fit's coefficients and a bootstrap's bounds are functions of the order of
    events and censorings, values units hold, which D329 and D337 withhold for values.
  - Caveat messages that carry counts (`LIFT_DIFFERS`) follow the count rules.
  - **Predicates of a result** (D320): at each position, a predicate's split of the cohort's
    units, those for which it is TRUE, FALSE and UNKNOWN, is disclosed exactly as a cohort count
    is (below), the cohort's `n_true` its size. A proportion shows its numerator where the TRUE
    units are shown, and its denominator, and the predicate's `excluded_units`, where the UNKNOWN
    units are (the size less them); the pass never leaves one of the three suppressed alone. A
    position whose `n_true` is suppressed shows none of its predicates' counts. With one predicate,
    `analysed` is its split's. With more, no count that combines predicates is shown: `analysed`'s
    `n`, `excluded_units` and `excluded` are `null`, since the predicates' own counts bound a
    joint count from both sides (the units for which none is known are at least the sum of their
    UNKNOWN units less the size once for each predicate after the first, and at most the fewest)
    and a map of their reasons bounds each predicate's own; each predicate's `variables` entry is
    disclosed as its split is. A figure that combines predicates may be shown only where the
    counts shown give it alone, and an `excluded` map is `null` wherever a map of the same units
    is. Every estimate, interval, effect, test and chart value is computed only from counts
    shown: an effect with both proportions' counts, a test with every position's, and a
    proportion whose numerator of 0 is shown beside a suppressed denominator withholds its
    estimate and interval with it, though both would be 0. TRUE and FALSE are treated alike, so a
    predicate and its `not` are disclosed alike, and a count a view shows as FALSE of one and TRUE
    of the other is suppressed in both. At one position, nothing shown pins a suppressed count of
    a predicate's split to one value, for a predicate alone or beside its `not`, `known` and
    `unknown` in one view, or beside predicates independent of it, but under *k* = 2, as for
    cohort counts (where a reason map of two UNKNOWN units split one and one is pinned too).
    Predicates that depend on one another are not covered: predicates of one column's categories
    share each unit's default, so beside one that no unit's rows satisfy another's shown TRUE
    count pins its hidden split (§8.4's Limits, D383's example (a)). The
    positions are disclosed apart, as the cohorts of one call are: positions that are unions of
    others give each other's counts by difference, and a two-valued collapse of a predicate (`P or
    unknown P`, `P and known P`) beside it is another predicate; §8.4's Limits disclaim both, as
    they do for cohorts.
  - **Cohort counts** (D297): the unit table's size is the total of the linked set `n_true`,
    `n_false` and `n_unknown`, and a member of it: from 1 to *k* − 1 it is suppressed
    (`size.denominator`), as the catalogue suppresses that table's `n_rows`, and a table of fewer
    than max(*k*, 4) units shows none of the three counts, zeros included. Over a larger table the
    size is shown, and every count's complement is a member too (it is a count of `not`, `known`
    or `unknown` of the cohort): a complement from 1 to *k* − 1 suppresses a non-zero count. One
    suppressed alone takes another with it: `n_true` and `n_false` each other (`n_unknown` when
    the other is 0), and `n_unknown` the smaller non-zero of the two, both on a tie; two
    suppressed beside a non-zero shown count take it too when the rules split their sum only one
    way. `n_true` and `n_false` are treated alike, so `not` of a cohort is disclosed as the cohort
    with the two swapped. `lift_differs` is a set alone, and is always suppressed, a 0 included:
    the units it counts lie on one side of the cohort (a strict cohort of `some` steps turns only
    unknown units definite when its lifts flip, so its `lift_differs` is at most, and may be, its
    `n_unknown`; one whose lifts turn definite units unknown bounds it by `n_true` or
    `n_false`), and `known` of the cohort counts that side beside the rest, so its
    `lift_differs`, shown beside counts all large, would give back by the size less it a count
    of the cohort the pass suppressed. The breakdowns are `null` with
    `n_unknown` or when any of their counts is from 1 to *k* − 1; the size's estimate is
    suppressed with its numerator or its denominator; `UNKNOWN_EXCLUDED` and `LIFT_DIFFERS` are
    carried whenever `n_unknown` and `lift_differs` are suppressed, worded to say nothing of
    whether a unit is unknown or would change; and the
    messages of `UNKNOWN_EXCLUDED` and `LIFT_DIFFERS` quote only the counts shown. Nothing shown
    pins a suppressed count to one value, for a cohort alone or counted with its `not`, `known`
    and `unknown` in one call, each with its `lift_differs`, but under *k* = 2, where 1 to *k* − 1 is itself one value. The
    cohorts of one output are otherwise disclosed each alone, so a count does not depend on what
    else its call counts (Limits). The digest is taken after the pass.
- Identifier columns (§5.4) never have value distributions in catalogue statistics, whatever the
  settings.
- `allow_row_ids: false` refuses the `ids` leaf, the `summary.members` analysis and predicates on
  identifier columns, and requires `min_cell_count`. `summary.members` is also refused under any
  `min_cell_count`, the effective *k* of §7.6 (a deployment floor included), whatever its cohort's
  size (a refused analysis, D353): a unit key is held by one unit, so no count rule protects a list
  of them, and lists of cohorts whose sizes are shown give by difference the keys of a set of any
  size (every unit, `not X` and `X and known X` give the units whose `X` is unknown), which would
  make every count the pass hides a list of named units. Under *k* keys may be written into a
  document (`ids` leaves, predicates on identifier columns), never read out of an output, and
  applicability says `summary.members` is unavailable (§9.4, D332). The setting is each release's
  own (the floor and its dataset's `min_cell_count`, and for a draft the latest published release's
  too, D275), so a release published without one is listed, as its counts are shown.
- A pack's analysis (§10.1) is refused under any `min_cell_count`, the effective *k* a deployment
  floor included, and applicability calls it unavailable there (§9.4; a refused analysis, D353): its
  values are an arbitrary function of every member's values, which the pass cannot tell apart into
  counts it protects, statistics of values it withholds and values units hold (D344). It is refused,
  and unavailable, where the dataset allows no row ids too, as `summary.members` is, since it is
  handed each member's values in the keys' order and can give them back as a table of units; and it
  is never handed an identifier column's values (§5.4), nor a value whose `where` tests one (D342).
- **Limits.** These settings reduce casual disclosure. They do not prevent inference across
  repeated queries (for example by differencing two counts, or two cohorts of one `count_cohort`
  call, which are disclosed apart, unless one is the other's `not`, `known` or `unknown`), and
  they are not a privacy guarantee. A two-valued collapse of a cohort is such another cohort:
  `X and known X` counts `X`'s `n_true` beside its `n_false` + `n_unknown`, and `X or unknown X`
  its `n_true` + `n_unknown` beside its `n_false`, so that, shown whole when they are large, they
  give back counts `X`'s own pass suppressed beside a shown one (over 24 units under *k* = 4, `X`
  of 8, 13 and 3 shows only its 13, and `X and known X` shows 8, 16 and 0). No pass over one
  cohort's count can prevent that: the collapse's counts are its own, and suppressing them for
  `X`'s sake would need the pass to recognise every cohort that differs from another by few
  units, which is differencing again; so is a predicate's collapse asked beside it in one view
  (D320). Whether cohorts share units is reported too: a view of an analysis that assumes
  independent groups is refused with the count of what they share, and a result not refused
  says they share none, which beside the sizes of the other cohorts and of the unit table can
  bound a suppressed size (D318). A result's positions are disclosed apart, as the cohorts of one
  call are, so a view whose cohorts are unions of one another, allowed with `overlap: "allow"`
  (`A`, `not A` and `known A`), gives a predicate's suppressed count at one position by
  difference of those shown at the others (a numerator of 12 over `known A` beside 10 over `not
  A` gives the 2 over `A`), as the same cohorts asked in separate calls do (D320). Restricted
  data needs access control, which is outside v1 (§1.2). Without it, whether a cohort was counted
  on the server within the retention period of a count's issuances, and whether a view's result
  was issued within that of a result's (§12.2), is visible to anyone who can write the cohort or
  the view (its id's status in the derivation log, and `validate_document`'s `status`), though
  not when; issuance ids are not guessable, and `explain`
  gives no issuance's document as written (D302). Distributions (D329): under *k* a call reads a
  column's values with one set of edges only, across its views and whichever variable reads them
  (the column, or `max`, `min` or `mean` of it), and a `count` of the same canonical rows with one
  (a `count` whose rows are the same but written otherwise, a redundant condition added, is keyed
  apart), but two calls with other bins give by difference the units of a bin that merging hides
  (bins `[0, 45, 100]` beside `[0, 44, 100]` give those in [44, 45)), as the same cohorts asked in
  separate calls do, and so does the catalogue's range-edged histogram of a column
  (`describe_column`) beside a view's `bins` over a cohort of every unit, and two variables of one
  column that are not histograms of its values (`some` of two value sets, a column and a `count`
  over its rows) give by difference what one of them hides, as a predicate beside its collapse does,
  and so do two histograms with the same bins of variables whose units or rows differ (another
  `empty`, `where`, `lift` or aggregate: `max` of a column beside the same with `empty` 0 gives the
  units without rows in its lowest bin), as two cohorts that differ by few units do; an `excluded`
  map, whose reasons overlap, gives by inclusion and exclusion the units excluded for two reasons,
  as a cohort's `unknown_by_reason` does. **Variables and cohorts whose paths share a relationship
  or a coverage table share their coverage and each unit's default**: a variable's memberships;
  `some` or `every` of the column's values; a `count` of its rows; another column of those rows,
  or of rows below them; the same memberships under another `lift`; and a cohort of the view that
  asks about those rows. So in one view each can bound what another hides, as two variables of
  one column do: `some` of one category beside `some` of another pins a unit's suppressed reason
  through their excluded maps (D383's example (b)); predicates of one column's categories in
  `compare.existence` bound one another the same way, and D320's split pins them (example (a));
  and a cohort of the units that hold a category, beside a cohort of every unit, gives its own
  suppressed UNKNOWN count through a category no unit holds (example (c): of 10 units under
  *k* = 3, 7 UNKNOWN for it among every unit less 5 in the cohort gives the cohort's 2). The
  brute forces of D320 and D329 took their predicates and variables to be independent; the
  follow-up is #73. Within one variable's memberships, the categories' counts give, by
  difference or by inclusion and exclusion, the units that hold one category among those UNKNOWN
  or FALSE for another, or that hold two, as cohorts that differ by few units give theirs, and a
  membership row beside `some` of a union of its values gives their intersection, as two value
  sets do: the rule of memberships protects each category's own counts and their joint
  `analysed`, not those cells (D383). **Units that read the same rows** (through an up step, such
  as several units of one parent) have one answer between them, so where group sizes are shown,
  counts are multiples of them: every brute force of this section takes units' answers to be
  independent. **A column unique across its table**, alone or beside columns the descriptors
  bound (record-filtered, boolean or derived ones), in any set the gate keeps unique (its
  table's key, a relationship's parent columns or a one-to-one relationship's child columns),
  gives each category to one unit at most, or to as many as those bounded columns' values allow
  (a key `[grade, season]` with `season` record-filtered to one value makes `grade` unique), so
  beside a shown split whose FALSE units are all but one a hidden TRUE count is 1 (of
  10 units under *k* = 3, awards keyed by the award, two of three declared held once each show
  10 known and a hidden numerator, and 0 would be shown), as `some` of that category pins it
  today (D329); the follow-up is #73. **A count of the rows** shown in any call (a table's
  `n_rows` in the catalogue, a `count` of them, or `some` of each category) bounds the sum of a
  column's TRUE counts over a cohort of every unit even where the descriptors leave the rows
  open, so where the rows barely exceed the units a hidden count is pinned (of 10 units under
  *k* = 3, one row each in the data, a keyless child's grades show 5 and 4 of 10 beside
  `n_rows` 10, so the hidden third is 1), as `some` and `count_cohort` pin it today; the
  follow-up is #73. And catalogue statistics still pool categories (D271), so
  declared values missing from a list are those pooled, each 1 when the pooled count is their number
  (#49). Under *k*, whether a call is refused over the answer cap, `query_seconds` or `query_memory`
  depends on the data (for a materialisation, on how many distinct values its cohorts' units hold,
  which the count rule does not protect), as a run's time and memory already did (D327).

### 8.5 Charts

Charts are Vega-Lite specifications generated on the server from result values. Their data is
inline (`data.values`), copied from the result after the disclosure pass; they contain no
transforms that compute numbers, no expressions built from data, and no URLs, `$schema`
included; a value that is `null` has no row (D322). Clients render
them with a CSP-safe interpreter and a loader that makes no network requests (§14).

### 8.6 Refusals

A refusal is `{code, path, message: [Segment, …], alternatives: [Segment, …], limit?: {name,
max}, counts?: [cohort counts]}`. `code` is a stable enum of `UPPER_SNAKE_CASE` codes, defined
with the schemas (pack codes are namespaced, `<pack id>.<CODE>`); `path` is a JSON Pointer into
the document as written, or `null` (where a problem lies inside a value a parameter supplied, the
pointer is that of the `"$name"` string, and the message names the parameter; where a key cannot
be written in a pointer because it is not Unicode text, the pointer is that of its object);
`alternatives` lists what *is* available (A3); `limit` names the limit hit (§14); `counts`
carries cohort counts with their ids where a refusal reports numbers (e.g. the overlap of §7.4);
they are issued, and recorded, as `count_cohort`'s are.
Refusals of a descriptor, such as one `propose_descriptor` receives, point into that descriptor
in the same way, and rules that span the descriptors of a release point into the list of them
(`UNKNOWN_DESCRIPTOR` where a descriptor names one the release does not hold).
A tool that takes a document (`validate_document`, `count_cohort`) points the refusals of the
document into the document as written, not into the request that carries it; problems of the
request itself, and of its JSON text, point into the request (D299).
`validate_document` returns every refusal, sorted by (`path`, `code`), with these bounds:
refusals with the same `path` and `code` are merged; a `null` and a refused parameter
reference are reported wherever they are, except inside a `params` refused as a whole; no
reference is looked up while `params` is refused or is not an object, but a malformed reference
is refused wherever references are substituted, whatever `params` is; the other checks report
nothing inside a value already refused (a `null`, a refused parameter reference, `params`
refused as a whole, or an array or object with more members than it may have) or in a clause
whose `kind` was refused, and nothing that follows only from a `null` (an object lacking that
member, or a cohort's datasets or `unmapped`, or a view's cohorts, being unknown); and after the
first 1,000, one `LIMIT_EXCEEDED` refusal with `path` `null` says how many more were found. The other tools fail
with the first refusal (HTTP 422, or an MCP tool error).

---

## 9. Analysis registry

### 9.1 Entry

Each analysis is a descriptor (`kind: analysis`, §5.1) plus an implementation:

```jsonc
{
  "kind": "analysis", "id": "survival.km", "version": "1.1.0",
  "label": "Kaplan–Meier survival",
  "definition": "Kaplan–Meier estimate per cohort; log-rank test; medians and landmark survival with intervals; difference in medians and unadjusted hazard ratio, with intervals, versus the reference cohort.",
  "fields": {
    "requires": [
      { "role": "endpoint", "kind": "endpoint", "on": "unit" },
      { "role": "cohorts", "min": 1, "max": 6 }
    ],
    "params":  { /* JSON Schema, generated from a Pydantic model */ },
    "returns": { /* JSON Schema of `values` */ },
    "methods": { /* named methods, as in §9.5; "implemented directly" where library defaults differ */ },
    "assumptions": ["independent censoring", "independent groups"],
    "uses_reference": true,
    "assumes_independent_groups": true,
    "cross_dataset": { "method": "log-rank test and Cox fit stratified by dataset; curves and medians pooled" },
    "randomness": { "seeded": true, "replicates": 2000 },
    "caveats": ["UNKNOWN_EXCLUDED", "INVALID_EXCLUDED", "UNCONFIRMED_SEMANTICS", "SMALL_N", "PH_VIOLATED",
                "NOT_ESTIMABLE", "COHORTS_OVERLAP", "TIME_ORIGIN_MISMATCH",
                "CONFOUNDED_WITH_DATASET", "POOLED_ACROSS_DATASETS", "UNMAPPED_COMPARISON",
                "SCOPE_PARTIAL", "COVERAGE_PROPOSED", "LIFT_DIFFERS", "DRAFT_RELEASE"],
    "min_group_n": 10,
    "min_events": 5
  },
  "curation": {}
}
```

The example is `survival.km` as v1 ends it: 1.1.0, with the hazard ratio (M3.3b, D355), and a
`cross_dataset` method from M6. Its 1.0.0 entry has no hazard ratio or `PH_VIOLATED`, and 1.1.0's
`cross_dataset` is `null` until M6 (D346, D348); refused under any disclosure setting, no version
of it carries `SUPPRESSED` (D351, D353). An entry names a `library` only where one computes its
values; the core's use none (D349).

- Analyses are registered only by the core and by packs, through code review. Users cannot
  upload analyses in v1.
- `requires` entries are `{role, kind?, on?, datatype?, min?, max?, predicate?}`, with `kind` one
  of `endpoint`, `column` or `table`, distinct roles and `min` ≤ `max`; `predicate` cites a pack's
  requirement predicate as `"<pack id>.<name>"` (§10.1). A view of a pack's analysis binds each
  `column` requirement by role in its `columns`, each `endpoint` requirement by role in its
  `endpoints` (D352), and gives what else it takes in `options`, which the entry's `params`
  schema checks (D341).
- `library`, `randomness`, `min_group_n` and `min_events` are optional; every other member of
  `fields` is required, `cross_dataset` included (§7.5).
- `"on": "unit"` means the endpoint must be on the unit table itself, not reached by a lookup:
  with samples as the unit and a patient-level endpoint, a patient with several samples would be
  counted several times.
- `caveats` lists every code the analysis can raise, exhaustively.
- `cross_dataset: null` means the analysis cannot run across datasets (§7.5).

### 9.2 Several values per unit

A column is **multi-valued** for a unit when it is below the unit or list-valued. A view reads a
column as a **variable**, `{column, via?, aggregate?, values?, where?, lift?, empty?, bins?,
count?, each?}`: `values` goes with `some` and `every` alone, `where` (the conditions a row reached at
the last down step must meet, on its table as an `exists` leaf's `where` is) with `count`, `max`,
`min` and `mean` and with `count: "rows"`, `empty` with `max`, `min` and `mean`, and `bins` with
numbers; a variable resolves on the unit table in the release of the view's cohorts, one value
per unit or the unit excluded with its reasons (D325, D326), or, with `count: "rows"`, the rows
below the unit (below, D378), or, with `each`, its memberships of each category (below, D380). A variable's `where` may hold pack leaves, expanded as a cohort's
are (§7.3, D345).

- Analyses that declare `assumes_independent_groups` use one value per unit, so a view MUST give
  an `aggregate` for each multi-valued column it uses: `count`, `max`, `min` or `mean` (numeric
  aggregates), or `some` or `every` with a `values` set (*some row has a value in V*, *every row
  has a value in V*). `max` and `min` on categories require `ordered` permissible values.
- `some` and `every` aggregates are existence questions and follow §6.5 in full, including
  evidence and open scope with `SCOPE_PARTIAL`.
- Numeric aggregates are computed over the rows reached at the last down step of the column's
  path through the children kept at every earlier down step (dropped as in §6.5, step 3, under
  the aggregate's `lift`, default `strict`), pooled: `mean` is the mean over those rows, not a
  mean of means. A unit's numeric aggregate is UNKNOWN, with the corresponding reasons, unless the
  unit is in scope and closed at every down step; if an earlier down step keeps no child, it is
  UNKNOWN (`NOT_COVERED`). When the last relationship's coverage has scope columns, the view MUST
  restrict them to a finite set of values, and the parent row must be listed for all of them. A
  numeric aggregate carries `COVERAGE_PROPOSED` when the coverage of any down step it relied on is
  proposed. A view that leaves such scope columns open is refused (`OPEN_SCOPE`); one that
  gives a numeric aggregate of what it cannot take (`mean` of what is not a number, `max` or
  `min` of what is neither a number nor an ordered category, or any aggregate of a column with
  one value per unit) is refused (`AGGREGATE_NOT_ALLOWED`), and one that reads a multi-valued
  number without an aggregate (`AGGREGATE_REQUIRED`); `max` and `min` of an ordered category
  compare its listed order, a value outside it counting as UNKNOWN (`NO_INFORMATION`).
- Row states: rows whose value is UNKNOWN or NOT_ASSESSED make `max`, `min` and `mean` UNKNOWN,
  with their reasons; rows whose value is NOT_APPLICABLE are skipped; `count` counts rows
  whatever their values.
- A closed unit with no pooled rows has `count` 0. For `max`, `min` and `mean` the view gives
  `empty`: a value to use (e.g. `0` for *highest grade, none recorded*) or `"exclude"` (the
  default), in which case the unit is excluded with reason `NO_ROWS`.
- Descriptive analyses count units per category, with a denominator per category (the units for
  which *has a row in this category* is known), so a unit with rows in several categories counts
  in each and the output carries `multi_membership: true`; or, if the view asks for `count:
  "rows"`, they count rows, labelled as rows. Numeric multi-valued columns in descriptive analyses
  need an `aggregate` or `count: "rows"`. Per-category denominators are a variable's
  memberships, asked for explicitly with `each: "category"` (below); the engine gives them from
  M3.2e-2a-1 and `summary.distribution` from M3.2e-2a-2, their disclosure rule from M3.2e-2b
  (#52, §8.4, D377, D380, D383). Before M3.2e-2a-2 a multi-valued categorical column without an
  `aggregate` was `NOT_SUPPORTED` in descriptive analyses (D324, D335), and a variable's `each`
  `NOT_SUPPORTED` there; from it (D382), a multi-valued column with none of `aggregate`, `count`
  and `each` is `AGGREGATE_REQUIRED` in every analysis. The refusal of a
  multi-valued column without an `aggregate`, and of an aggregate its column does not take, lists
  the aggregates it takes: `count` of any column, `max` and `min` of numbers and ordered
  categories, `mean` of numbers, and `some` and `every`, of a list's items these two alone, those
  alone that run in its place with the variable's path, `where` and `empty` and that the analysis
  reads (D377, D380), and beside `bins`, which divide numbers, those alone whose values are
  numbers (`count`, and of numbers `max`, `min` and `mean`; D382).
  In a descriptive analysis the first also lists `count: "rows"` for a number, category or boolean
  column below the unit, never for a list and never below a step whose coverage has scope
  columns, whose rows it pools as `count` does, beside `bins` for a number alone, and
  `each: "category"` for a category, boolean or list column, never beside `bins`, which
  memberships do not take; `count: "rows"` not under a disclosure setting, which withholds it,
  and `each` under one only where its path is one open down step from the unit (D383), and each
  only where the analysis reads that form, as its own phase 2 checks it (D378, D379, D380,
  D382). An analysis that assumes independent groups reads one value per unit, and so does a pack's
  (D341), so there such a column is `AGGREGATE_REQUIRED` as a numeric one is, offering neither, and
  `count: "rows"` and `each` are `INVALID_VALUE` (D335, D380).
- **Rows** (`count: "rows"`, from M3.2e-1, D378). A variable of a column below the unit (a path
  with a down step) with `count: "rows"` and no `aggregate`, `values` or `empty` (else
  `CONFLICTING_MEMBERS`) counts the rows a numeric aggregate of the column would pool, pooled as
  above (its `where` on the last step, its `lift` at the earlier ones), each row's value read
  through the lookups after the last down step. `count` and `count: "rows"` count a row once for
  each path through the kept children that reaches it and once for each unit that reaches it. A
  unit that is not in scope or not closed at a step, or whose `where` or record filter is
  UNKNOWN, is excluded with its reasons, as a numeric aggregate's is, and its rows are not read;
  a pooled row whose value is not PRESENT (NOT_APPLICABLE, NOT_ASSESSED or NO_INFORMATION by its
  cell's state, NO_PARENT for a lookup that reaches no row) is left out and counted by reason,
  and its unit is not: an UNKNOWN `where` excludes the unit, an UNKNOWN value the row. It reads
  category, boolean and numeric columns; a column with one value per unit is `INVALID_VALUE` at
  `…/count`, and so is a list column (D382), since a list's items are not rows that a step pools,
  offering what runs in its place: `some` and `every` but beside a `where`, which they do not
  take, or `bins`, which divide numbers, and, but beside `bins` or `where`, `each: "category"`,
  under a disclosure setting too (D383). It is withheld under any disclosure setting (§8.4,
  D379).
- **Memberships** (`each: "category"`, from M3.2e-2a-1 in the engine and M3.2e-2a-2 in
  `summary.distribution`, D380–D382). A variable of a multi-valued category, boolean or list
  column with `each: "category"` and no `aggregate`, `values`, `empty`, `count`, `where` or
  `bins` (else `CONFLICTING_MEMBERS`) asks, for each category *c* of the column, the
  existence question Q_c: `some` row reached at the last down step of its path (through `via`,
  under its `lift`) whose value is *c*, or `any` of a list's items, following §6.5 in full
  (NOT_APPLICABLE is FALSE, §6.4). Per category, its units are TRUE, FALSE or UNKNOWN by reason,
  the numerator TRUE and the denominator the units known. The categories listed over a cohort are
  the declared values in their order, zeros included (`false` and `true` for a boolean), then
  the others, in UTF-16 order, where some member's answer for one is not its default (the
  answer for a category none of its rows holds and no coverage lists: a row that holds it, or a
  coverage that lists it, makes it); for a column the last step's record filter filters, the
  filter's allowed values alone, zeros included; at most `MAX_CATEGORIES`. A unit is known where
  its answer for some listed category is known, or, with none listed, where its default is; else
  it is excluded under the reasons of all of them. An analysis that reads one value per unit
  (one that assumes independent groups, or a pack's) refuses it (`INVALID_VALUE` at `…/each`),
  and so does a column with one value per unit or one that is not categorical, each offering
  only what runs in its place, as the analysis's own phase 2 checks each form (leaving `each` out
  where the analysis reads the column as it is, or the aggregates its path allows that it
  reads), and nothing, saying so, where no form runs;
  `UNDECLARED_DATATYPE` and `ROW_IDS_NOT_ALLOWED` apply. `summary.distribution` gives each
  category's units over those for which it is known, a unit counting in each it holds
  (`multi_membership: true`); under a disclosure setting, its declared categories alone, each
  disclosed as a question's split, and it withholds memberships where the descriptors bound
  the rows each unit reaches (§8.4, D383, D384).
- Aggregates are part of the reference evaluator (§13.3).

### 9.3 Determinism

- **Arithmetic.** DuckDB computes only `SUM`, `COUNT`, `MIN`, `MAX` and `AVG` over integer and
  DECIMAL columns, `MIN` and `MAX` over floating-point columns too (they pick one of the values
  and compute nothing, `-0.0` being read as `0.0`; D326), and `BIT_OR` and `BOOL_OR` over integers
  and booleans, for values that enter a digest (D294). Every other aggregate (any aggregate over
  floating-point values, standard deviations, variances, quantiles) is computed in Python: sums
  with correctly rounded summation (`math.fsum`), statistics with exact algorithms over values in
  canonical order.
- **Order.** Before every library call, rows are sorted by (cohort position, dataset manifest
  hash, the RFC 8785 serialisation of the unit key, the row key). Set-like arrays in outputs
  (caveats, affected paths, reasons) are sorted. Category arrays in `values` follow the declared
  permissible order, else canonical value order.
- **Fits.** Model fits use single-threaded linear algebra pinned to a fixed code path, fixed
  starting values, and convergence tolerances set by the analysis version.
- **Randomness.** Resampling uses the number of replicates set by the analysis version, seeded
  from the computation id (§7.6), so that raising a disclosure floor does not change resampled
  values that are still reported: each cohort is resampled within itself from a stream seeded by
  the SHA-256 of the computation id, the column and the position, and no clock or state is read
  (D338).
- **Rounding.** Before hashing, floating-point fields (only) are set to 0 if their magnitude is
  below 1e-12 and then rounded to 10 significant digits (the double's exact value, correctly
  rounded, ties to even, read back as the nearest double; D283), and a value that rounds past the
  largest double is refused, as a non-finite one is; an analysis may declare coarser precision
  for specific values. Integers are never rounded.
- **Scope.** The invariant (§7.6) is guaranteed within a deployment and on CI's reference
  platform. A difference across platforms is a bug to fix with stricter determinism, never
  accepted silently. The thread-count determinism tests use a fixture of at least one million
  rows whose tables each hold at least as many row groups as the threads compared, because
  DuckDB runs a scan in parallel only across row groups (D372, D373).
- A dependency upgrade that changes a golden digest requires bumping the affected versions
  (§7.6).

### 9.4 Applicability

`applicable_analyses(dataset, unit)` matches each entry's `requires` against the dataset's
descriptors and returns, for each analysis, `available`, `unavailable` (naming the missing
requirement) or `available_with_caveats` (e.g. an endpoint whose event coding is
`imported_default`). The dataset page and `describe_dataset` both show this, and `list_analyses`
given a dataset; without a unit, each keyed table is tried and the best status kept (D316). A
refused analysis (§8.4, D353: `summary.members`, the survival analyses and every pack's) is
`unavailable` under the release's effective *k*, naming `min_cell_count` as missing, and one that
lists or hands each member's values in the keys' order (`summary.members` and every pack's) where
its dataset allows no row ids, naming `allow_row_ids`: exactly where every view of it is refused for
disclosure. A form a disclosed analysis withholds (`count: "rows"` and `each`, D379, D382) leaves
it `available`, since its other views run. A disclosed analysis may still be `unavailable` under *k* where no view
of it could show more than counts (`compare.columns` over a unit with no categories, naming
`columns` and `min_cell_count`, D337); a pack's analysis is `unavailable` wherever it requires a
column of dates or datetimes, naming that role (D341).

### 9.5 Core analyses (v1)

**Estimability.** Before computing, each analysis checks its inputs and marks values not
estimable (§8.2) instead of computing them. Where several rows apply to one value, the first of
them gives its reason. A test is chosen by the number of cohorts it uses (Welch's *t*,
Mann–Whitney or Fisher's 2×2 test for two):

| Condition | Values not estimable |
|---|---|
| A cohort with no units, or no units for which the variable is known | its estimates and every pairwise contrast with it (`no_units`); omnibus tests (chi-squared, Fisher, Welch's ANOVA, Kruskal–Wallis, log-rank) use the other cohorts and record the positions they used, and are not estimable (`no_units`) if fewer than two remain |
| A cohort with no events (survival) | its difference in medians and its hazard ratio (`no_events`): its units are left out of the Cox fit (the limit of the full fit as its coefficient tends to −∞) but stay in `analysed`, its curve and the log-rank test (unless the next row applies); if it is the reference, every value of the Cox fit (`no_events`) |
| A log-rank test whose variance is 0 (e.g. all units censored, or no event time at which two of its cohorts are at risk), or so near 0 that its statistic lies beyond 2^53 − 1 or is not finite | that test (`zero_variance`) |
| Fewer than two values, or zero variance, in a group (numeric) | its standard deviation, every *t*-test or ANOVA that includes it, and the Welch–Satterthwaite interval of every mean difference that includes it (`zero_variance`) |
| Every analysed value tied (numeric) | the Mann–Whitney and Kruskal–Wallis tests over them (`zero_variance`) |
| A zero denominator | the proportion and every contrast built on it (`zero_denominator`) |
| A numerator of 0 in either proportion of a risk ratio | the ratio and its interval (`no_events`) |
| A contingency table that, after removing cohorts with no known units and dropping all-zero category columns (done before choosing Fisher or chi-squared), has fewer than two columns | its test (`degenerate_table`) |
| A covariate level with no events in a Cox fit | that level's term: for `survival.cox`, `separation` where the likelihood's recession cone keeps its coefficient's direction one-signed (it runs to −∞) and `zero_variance` where the cone leaves it undetermined (D360); its units stay in `analysed` |
| A hazard ratio or Wald bound beyond 2^53 − 1, or that rounds to 0 (a Cox fit) | that member alone (`separation`); the view is not refused (D346, D355) |
| In a Cox fit whose only terms are cohorts (`survival.km`), a cohort with events and a path through the risk sets' graph (an edge h → e wherever cohort h is at risk at an event of cohort e) to the reference and none back, or from it and none back | its hazard ratio and interval (`separation`: the likelihood sends its coefficient to −∞ or +∞), decided exactly on the risk sets, not after fitting (D356) |
| In such a fit, a cohort with events and no path to or from the reference | its hazard ratio and interval (`zero_variance`: not identified against the reference, D356) |
| A Cox term with covariates (`survival.cox`) whose estimate is infinite: its coordinate keeps one sign on the likelihood's recession cone and is not 0 on all of it, decided exactly (D360; not after fitting, as R's `coxph` flags it) | that term's estimate and interval (`separation`, with the sign) |
| A Cox term with covariates (`survival.cox`) not identified: in an exact dependency of the columns over the risk sets (every column of it), dropped by `cholesky2` at 0 as R drops it, left undetermined by the recession cone (its coordinate takes both signs on it), or dropped by the finite part's fit (D360, D361) | that term's estimate and interval (`zero_variance`) |
| A fit that does not converge (for cohorts only, the fit of D356 of the reference and the cohorts it ties to both ways, where R's iteration from 0 does not end at its maximum and the ascent, with R's iteration from its point, does not either) | every value of the fit (`not_converged`) |
| A test of proportional hazards whose extended information is singular (e.g. every event at one time), where R's `cox.zph` fails, or whose statistic lies beyond 2^53 − 1 or is not finite | that test (`zero_variance`); a fit of cohorts one of which is `separation` has none (`separation`), else one of which is `zero_variance` none (`zero_variance`, D356); a fit with covariates has the test of its finite part's estimated terms (D361) |
| A survival curve that never falls below 0.5 and does not end at exactly 0.5 | its median and the differences that use it (`not_reached`) |
| A survival curve at 0 | its pointwise log-log bounds from that time on (`zero_denominator`), which the median's interval rule skips, as R's `survfit` does; bounds where the curve is 1 equal 1 |
| A landmark time or grid time after the cohort's last follow-up | that cohort's landmark estimate, or curve value, and its bounds there (`beyond_follow_up`) |
| A bootstrap bound whose order statistic is infinite | that bound (`not_reached`) |

**Other rules.** NOT_APPLICABLE cells are excluded from the column comparisons of
`compare.columns` and counted (§6.6); in existence predicates they are FALSE (§6.4) and so known.
Benjamini–Hochberg correction covers the primary tests of a `compare.columns` or
`compare.existence` view only; tests that cannot be computed leave the family and are counted.
Quartiles use R's default definition (type 7). A bootstrap interval at level 1 − α is formed from
the ⌈B·α/2⌉-th and ⌈B·(1 − α/2)⌉-th order statistics of the B replicates, ordered with −∞ below
every finite value and +∞ above; a replicate whose statistic is undefined (e.g. both medians
unreached) counts as −∞ for the lower bound and +∞ for the upper bound.

| Id | Returns |
|---|---|
| `summary.distribution` | Per column, per cohort: for categories, units per category (proportions over units whose value is known; per-category denominators for multi-valued columns, §9.2); for numbers, n, mean, standard deviation, median, quartiles, minimum and maximum, and a histogram (bins as in §8.4, from the parameters, the column's declared range or, without disclosure settings, *B* equal-width bins between the minimum and maximum; *B* = 10); observation-state counts (each column's `analysed` units excluded by reason: NOT_APPLICABLE, NOT_ASSESSED, NO_INFORMATION and the rest). A column with `count: "rows"` (§9.2) gives the same over the rows its units reach, each once for every path and unit that reaches it: rows per category over the rows with a value (`counts: "rows"`, `category_rows`), or n rows and their statistics and histogram (`number_rows`), with the rows left out by reason (`excluded_rows`), its `analysed` counting units; it is withheld under disclosure settings (§8.4; D378, D379). A column with `each: "category"` (§9.2) gives its memberships (`memberships`, `multi_membership: true`): for each category listed over the cohort, the units for which some row (or item) has it over those for which that is known (`counts: "known"`, the category's leaf key as `predicate`), its UNKNOWN units in the proportion's `excluded` by reason, a unit counting in each category it has; under disclosure settings, its declared categories alone, each disclosed as a question's split with no `excluded` map and its `analysed` suppressed, and withheld wherever its path is not one open down step from the unit, the descriptors then possibly bounding the rows each unit reaches (§8.4; D380, D382, D383, D384). Parameters: `columns`, 1 to 8 variables (§9.2), each a category (category and boolean columns, `max` and `min` of ordered categories, and `some` and `every`, whose categories are `false` and `true`) or a number (number, integer and time-offset columns, `count`, `mean`, and `max` and `min` of numbers), or `count: "rows"` of a category, boolean or numeric column below the unit, with `bins` for a number, or `each: "category"` of a multi-valued category, boolean or list column. Descriptive only (D328) |
| `summary.members` | The unit keys of exactly one cohort's members, each its values in key order as they are stored, sorted as step 8 of §7.6 sorts an `ids` leaf's members (RFC 8785 serialisation, UTF-16 code units), paginated with `offset` (0 by default) and `limit` (1 to 1,000, 100 by default), with the key columns and whether more follow. Refused where the dataset allows no row ids and under any disclosure setting (§8.4). Descriptive only; no chart (D331, D332) |
| `compare.columns` | Parameters: `columns`, 1 to 8 variables (§9.2), categories or numbers as `summary.distribution` takes them, without `bins`, and `level`, 0.95 by default. Per cohort: for categories, units per category as `summary.distribution` gives them; for numbers, n, mean, standard deviation and median. Categories: chi-squared test of independence, or Fisher's exact test for 2×2 tables, over the cohorts with a value and the categories some of them hold; per-category differences in proportions versus the reference, with Newcombe hybrid score intervals. Numbers: primary test Welch's *t* (two cohorts) or Welch's ANOVA (more); secondary test Mann–Whitney (exact when both groups have fewer than 50 values and no ties, otherwise the normal approximation with continuity correction, as R's `wilcox.test`) or Kruskal–Wallis, reported unadjusted; difference in means versus the reference (Welch–Satterthwaite interval) and in medians (bootstrap percentile interval, 2000 replicates, each cohort resampled within itself). Benjamini–Hochberg q-values over the columns' primary tests. Under disclosure settings a number shows only its units, and categories what their shown rows give (§8.4; D336–D338) |
| `compare.existence` | Per existence predicate (e.g. *some grade ≥3 adverse event*, *a TP53 mutation*): the proportion per cohort over units for which it is known, with a Wilson score interval without continuity correction; risk difference (Newcombe hybrid score interval) and risk ratio (Katz log interval; see the estimability table for zero numerators) versus the reference; Fisher's exact test for two cohorts, chi-squared for more. Parameters: `predicates`, 1 to 16 clauses asked of every unit of the unit table, without `ids` or `cohort` leaves, and `level`, 0.95 by default; per position and predicate, `lift_differs` (D319, D323) |
| `survival.km` | Parameters: `endpoint` (an endpoint on the unit table; by default the one usable there, D347), `landmarks` (at most 16 strictly increasing times, 0 or more; none by default), `grid` (1 to 100 strictly increasing times; without one, a step at every time a follow-up ends, at most 2,000 a cohort) and `level` (0.95), in the endpoint's time units (D348). Per cohort: Kaplan–Meier curve over left-truncated risk sets (entries from the endpoint's `entry`, §5.8), with log-log (Greenwood) pointwise intervals; median, implemented directly as R's `survival` defines it in `quantile.survfit` (the smallest time at which the curve is below 0.5; where it equals 0.5, to within √ε ≈ 1.49e-8, over an interval, that interval's midpoint; where it ends at exactly 0.5, the midpoint of the time it reached 0.5 and the last follow-up), with a Brookmeyer–Crowley interval on the log-log scale using the same crossing rule; landmark survival at requested times, with intervals. Between cohorts: the log-rank test over left-truncated risk sets {entry < t ≤ time} (the Mantel–Haenszel statistic, summed over datasets when stratified), equal to the score test of R's `coxph(Surv(entry, time, status) ~ cohort + strata(dataset), ties = "exact")`; difference in medians versus the reference, with a bootstrap interval (resampling within cohort, 2000 replicates; a replicate whose median is not reached counts as +∞); from version 1.1.0 (M3.3b, D355), after each difference in medians, the unadjusted hazard ratio versus the reference with its Wald interval, from a Cox fit (Efron ties) of the reference and the cohorts with events that the risk sets tie to it both ways (D356), and that fit's Grambsch–Therneau global test of proportional hazards as in `survival.cox` (D357), `PH_VIOLATED` when p < 0.05 and `SMALL_N` under 10 events per term; the log-rank test's degrees of freedom are the rank of its covariance (§7.5).
