| D353 | Disclosure classes (M3; refines §8.4, §9.4, D317, D332, D337, D344 and D351) | Every analysis is `disclosed` or `refused` (§8.4). The core states each of its analyses' class as a required field of `registry.CoreAnalysis` (with, for a refused one, why, in a refusal's words), so none exists unclassified; every pack's analysis is refused and hands its members' values, whatever its entry says (`registry.disclosure_of`). Disclosed: `compare.existence` (D320), `summary.distribution` (D329), `compare.columns` (D337). Refused: `summary.members` (D332, which also lists keys), `survival.km` (D351), and `survival.cox` when it comes (M3.3e). Phase 2 checks a resolved view's disclosure (`views._disclosure`) before anything else it checks that could run pack code or report an identifier column (`_packed`): an analysis that lists or hands each member's values in the keys' order, over a dataset that allows no row ids, is `ROW_IDS_NOT_ALLOWED`; a refused one under the effective *k* is `WITHHELD_UNDER_K`, a new refusal code (§8.6's enum); both at `/views/<i>/analysis`, naming the analysis and the setting (`_setting`: the dataset's `min_cell_count`, a draft's latest published release's, D275, or the floor) and, under *k*, why, never a value, their alternatives `count_cohort` and the disclosed core analyses. A view phase 1 refuses reports phase 1's refusals first. Applicability names `min_cell_count` and `allow_row_ids` exactly where phase 2 refuses for them (`registry._refused`), and still evaluates a pack's requirement predicates, which read the release's descriptors, not its rows; `compare.columns`' `CATEGORIES_UNDER_K` (D337) stays, a disclosed analysis with nothing to compare. `run_analysis` raises before any query if a view phase 2 withholds reaches it (`registry.withheld`), as `members.list_members` raises on a *k*. `CoreAnalysis` refuses a refused analysis without a reason, a disclosed one with one, and a disclosed one that lists keys. An analysis leaves the refused class only with a rule in §8.4 and a brute force whose bounds its decision states. A disclosed analysis may withhold a form of its parameters under any *k*, at that form's member, and stays disclosed and `available` (`CoreAnalysis.withheld_forms`, D379): `summary.distribution` withholds `count: "rows"` (M3.2e-1, #52), which settles its class, and a variable's memberships, `each` (M3.2e-2a-2, D382). `test_disclosure_classes` checks, for every registered analysis (the core's and a test pack's) under the dataset's *k*, the floor, both, a draft's latest published release's *k* and no row ids, that phase 2 refuses exactly the refused ones (and the key-listing ones without row ids) with the code, path, setting, reason and alternatives above and names no value, that the pack's predicate and `run` are not called, that a disclosed one is not refused for disclosure (but for a form it withholds, D379), and that applicability agrees with phase 2; the catalogue's tests (`test_analyses`, `test_pack_analyses`, `test_survival_analyses`) check over a store that no tool runs a query or names a value, and that `run_analysis` raises before any query on a withheld view From M3.2e-2b (D383), `summary.distribution`'s withheld forms are `count: "rows"` alone; its memberships are disclosed by D383's rule, and withheld by its own check wherever the path is not one open down step from the unit. | #2's planning agreement: D332, D337, D344 and D351 each decided "refuse under any *k*" apart, with three codes (`ROW_IDS_NOT_ALLOWED` under *k* for members, `NOT_SUPPORTED` for packs and survival) and three code paths; M3.3b, M3.2e and M4's pack analyses need the rule next. Two classes, not three: a class between them (counts shown, statistics of values withheld) changed no code path, and each disclosed analysis's own decision says what it withholds. `NOT_SUPPORTED` means a later milestone, and `summary.members` is refused for good; a client needs one code that says that only a release with no disclosure setting, or another analysis, answers (no lower setting does). The class lives in the core, not in the analysis descriptor, so a pack cannot declare its values disclosed (D344) |
| D354 | M3.3 in three parts (M3.3b; refines §15, D315 and D346; refined by D358) | M3.3b is `survival.km` 1.1.0: of each cohort versus the reference, the unadjusted hazard ratio, and the global test of proportional hazards, that D13 and D67 put in it (D355–D357). `survival.cox` (covariates, dummy coding, complete cases, stratification by a column) is M3.3c; until then the registry lists it as M3.3c's (`LATER`), so a view of it is refused `NOT_SUPPORTED` naming M3.3c (D317), where D346 named M3.3b. `survival.cox` is to reuse D356's rule and fit for a view whose only terms are cohorts, so the two analyses cannot disagree there; its other covariates vary by unit and need a separation rule of their own | The unadjusted fit's covariates are indicators of the cohorts, constant within one, so the fit runs on the risk table's counts per cohort (a Newton step costs the event times times the cohorts and the terms squared, and each event the terms squared, not a pass over the units) and its separation is decided exactly on the risk sets' graph (D356); `survival.cox`'s covariates need a design matrix, dummy coding and complete-case accounting that share nothing with the curves or this fit. #2: a PR that spans independent surfaces is split |
| D355 | `survival.km` 1.1.0 (M3.3b; refines §9.1, §9.5, D346 and D348) | Version 1.1.0 adds, of each other position versus the reference in view order, after its difference in medians, the `hazard_ratio` effect: exp(β) of the fit of D356, with the Wald interval exp(β ± z·SE) at the view's `level` (`method: "wald"`); and `values.view.proportional_hazards`, D357's test (`method: "grambsch_therneau"`, its positions those with events), absent, like `test`, for a view of one cohort. `effects` holds at most 2 × 5. The entry's methods gain `wald` and `grambsch_therneau` and its caveats `PH_VIOLATED`, raised on `/values/view/effects` and `/values/view/proportional_hazards` where the test's p < 0.05; `SMALL_N` is raised on `/values/view/effects` where the fit has fewer than 10 events per term. A hazard ratio's first reason: `overlapping_cohorts`; `no_units` for a position without units; for a reference without units or events, or no other cohort with events, every value of the fit (`no_units`, `no_events`); `no_events` for a position without events (D179); D356's `separation` or `zero_variance`; `not_converged` where the fit reaches no maximum (D356); `zero_variance` where the fit's variance of the term is not positive (a guard: a fit at a maximum has full-rank information); and a hazard ratio or bound that `ieee.exp` takes beyond 2^53 − 1 or to 0, that member alone (`separation`). The test's: `overlapping_cohorts`; the fit's own reasons; `separation` where a cohort's hazard ratio is D356's `separation`, else `zero_variance` where one is its `zero_variance`; `not_converged`; and `zero_variance` where D357 finds none or its statistic lies beyond 2^53 − 1. The fit's and the test's loops spend their work (D350: a cohort at an event time times the terms squared, each share of a tied time's events the terms squared, the graph's cohorts squared an event time); at the listing's cap, over D350's shapes with times drawn from 1 to 1,000, the longest stretch between looks measured 0.21 s, and the fit and its test took up to 27 s (6 × 83,333 units), so such a view, whose bootstrap already takes longer, is refused as late. The golden results of `survival.km` are rewritten with the version bump and nothing else | D13 and D67 put the unadjusted hazard ratio and its test in `survival.km`, and D346 held them back for a fit that could be trusted at the edges; a hazard ratio beside a cohort R's fit sends to ±∞ agrees with R only as far as R's iterations went, which the fit of D356 does not depend on |
| D356 | Separation in a Cox fit of cohorts, decided on the risk sets, and the fit's maximum (M3.3b; revises §9.5's rows on infinite and uninformative Cox terms and D158 for fits whose only terms are cohorts; refines D179) | With indicators of the cohorts against a reference, along a direction d of the coefficients (the reference's held at 0) a term of the partial log-likelihood, with Breslow's, exact or Efron's ties, never falls if and only if the group of each event at that time attains the largest d among the groups at risk then; so the directions along which the likelihood never falls are those with d_e ≥ d_h for every event of cohort e and every cohort h at risk at its time: the graph with an edge h → e for each. Of the cohorts with events (event-free ones are left out, D179), one in the reference's strongly connected component is estimated; one with a path to the reference and none back is `separation` (its hazard ratio tends to 0), one with a path from it and none back `separation` (it tends to ∞), and one with neither `zero_variance` (its coefficient is not identified against the reference). No rounding decides any of these (`coxfit.standing`). The estimated cohorts' hazard ratios are the maximum of the model of the reference's component alone, the others' units left out: no other cohort is at risk at its events, so it is the limit of the fit of every cohort as the others' coefficients leave for ±∞, and it exists. R's iteration for it (`coxfit.newton`) is `coxph(ties = "efron")`'s on those units: `coxfit6`'s without an entry column and `agfit4`'s with one (as R chooses from `Surv`'s form; `timefix` is not replicated, D349), their sums in their order (event times from the last to the first, each tied time's events added a share at a time), the 0/1 columns neither centred nor scaled (`nocenter`), at most 20 iterations, converged at a relative change of the log-likelihood of 1e-9, its iterations counted as R counts them; `agfit4`'s recentring of the linear predictor, and the overflow error R raises there, are not, so where R stops the iteration goes on. Newton's steps from 0 need not reach the maximum: a step can land where the information vanishes to rounding, `cholesky2` drops a pivot and the iteration stops with the score far from 0 (R then drops the coefficient as singular), or the iteration runs out. So a point is the maximum only where the information has full rank and half of uᵀI⁻¹u, the gain a Newton step predicts, is at most 1e-9 of max(1, |log-likelihood|) (R's own tolerance, on that gain): R's iteration from 0 is the fit where it converges at such a point; else `coxfit._ascent` climbs from 0 (Newton's step where the information has full rank, the score where it has not, each at most 1 in any coefficient and halved, at most 60 times, until the log-likelihood rises by 1e-4 of what its slope predicts, at most 200 steps) to such a point, and R's iteration from there, as `coxph` runs it with that `init`, is the fit; where the ascent finds no such point, or R's iteration from its point does not end at one, the fit is `not_converged`. R's iteration here omits `agfit4`'s recentring, so far from 0 it can take another path than R's: where only R's recentred path converges, the ascent reaches the same maximum (one of some 11,000 fuzzed fits, within 2.2e-8 of R's). Its sums are per cohort (count × weight) where C sums per unit, so it agrees with R to rounding, not bit for bit. `reference/cox.R` holds 33 cases, 30 of which fit two cohorts or more (ties, delayed entry, step halving both ways, exp's range left at the first step, a first step into a flat region with and without entries, fits that run out both ways and estimable ones that do from 0, six cohorts, 12 random); on the 29 where R's iteration from 0 ends without an error, `newton` agrees with it within 1e-10 (relative, with a floor of 1: |a − b| ≤ 1e-10·max(1, |b|); measured within 1.4e-14 in coefficient and standard error and 3.5e-14 in log-likelihood where R converged), with R's iteration counts, converging where R converges without dropping a coefficient, and within 1e-3 where it runs out (values never shown); and on every one `fit` reaches the maximum that R reaches from the best of 12 starts refitted to 1e-13, within 6.1e-8 in coefficient, 1.2e-7 in standard error and 3e-14 in log-likelihood (R's own fit from 0 is as far from it). R's own flag (`coxph.fit`'s rule on the score at the final β, and `agreg.fit`'s) is not used. A test realises every set of edges over 2, 3 and 4 cohorts (4,164) with delayed entry and checks the rule against R's fit of every cohort from two starting points (an estimated cohort's coefficient the reduced fit's from both, a lower one's below −6 and a higher one's above 6 from both) and against the likelihood itself (along the graph's direction it rises to a limit that the reduced fit maximises and that shifting an apart cohort's component does not change) | R's flag rests on the score at the final β, which near separation is rounding noise: the first port missed 9 of 300 fuzzed separations, and a pivot that cancelled below 0 halved its step. R runs out of iterations in about half of separated fits, so `not_converged` beside a separated cohort was chance, and an apart cohort converges to a value R does not flag (of the test's 793 apart cohorts, NA in 288, moved by its starting point or sent past ±6 in 461, and in 44 left within 0.1 of where the other start leaves it). The graph decides exactly what the likelihood does. The first review found that R's iteration from 0 stops short of an existing maximum, or runs out, on fits of the reference's component that R fits from a better start (hazard ratios of 3e-15 with a zero-width interval where the maximum gives 0.0017): of 2,392 fuzzed fits, 136; with the certificate and the ascent every one reaches the maximum, and so did every one of some 11,000 more that the second review fuzzed against R from 16 starts; where R's iteration from 0, without the recentring, reaches it, the fit is that iteration's |
| D357 | The test of proportional hazards (M3.3b; refines §9.5) | `cox.zph(fit, transform = "km")`'s global test (survival 3.5-8: `zph1` without an entry column, `zph2` with one) of D356's fit, over its units only: g(t) = 1 − S(t−), S the Kaplan–Meier curve of those units pooled, less g's mean over their events; the covariates centred by their mean over the units; the score of the terms times g and the information of the model extended by them, accumulated as `zph1` accumulates them at the fit's coefficients, the terms' own score taken as 0; the statistic uᵀI⁻¹u by Gaussian elimination with partial pivoting (as `solve`, LAPACK's `dgesv`), with the terms' number as its degrees of freedom. Where the extended information's `cholesky2` (`toler.chol`) has less than full rank, where R's `solve` fails or gives what rounding makes, there is no test (`zero_variance`). Over `reference/cox.R`'s fits it agrees with R's test of the same fit within 1e-8 (relative, with a floor of 1; the test amplifies the fit's convergence tolerance: measured within 1.4e-14 on well-conditioned fits and 4e-10 on the flat region's), and with R's test of the maximum refitted to 1e-13 within 1e-5 (measured 3.9e-6), giving none exactly where `cox.zph` fails on the same fit; where R's fit from 0 drops a coefficient, `cox.zph` tests the rest, and this tests the fit that reaches the maximum | `cox.zph` is what D67 names; testing the reduced fit over its own units is testing the fit whose hazard ratios are shown, and R's `solve` is the arbiter of singularity only up to rounding, which the Cholesky rank decides the same way on every platform |
| D358 | M3.3's Cox model in three parts (M3.3c; refines §9.5, §15, D315 and D354) | M3.3c is the Cox model of a design (`analyses.coxph`, D359): any covariates per unit, strata, delayed entry, R's handling of columns, the certificate and ascent of D356 and the test of proportional hazards with strata, held to R where no coefficient is separated; it has no registry entry and changes no output. M3.3d decides separation and identification in such a model exactly, by the likelihood's recession cone (its lineality, an exact LP with cuts, refined level by level, and the fit of the part that stays finite). M3.3e is the `survival.cox` analysis: its entry, parameters, coding, complete cases and their `analysed`, output, reasons, caveats, readback and goldens. Until then the registry lists `survival.cox` as M3.3e's (`LATER`), and a view of it is refused `NOT_SUPPORTED` naming M3.3e (D317) | Two cold reviews of one plan for all three found the cone's rule and the finite part's fit wrong as first stated, the LP uncomputable as stated, and the complete-case accounting in conflict with §8.1's `analysed`; each part is independent of the others' code and reviewable alone (#2: a PR that spans independent surfaces is split) |
| D359 | The Cox model of a design (M3.3c; refines §9.5 and D349; builds on D356) | `analyses.coxph.fit` fits the Cox model of units that each carry a stratum, an entry (or none: every unit or none has one, as the call says), a time, whether it ended in an event, and a row of covariates: Efron's ties, per stratum, over left-truncated risk sets, by `coxfit.maximise` (R's iteration from 0 where it ends at the maximum, else from the point D356's ascent reaches) over a model of rows, identical units one row with a count (sums of the count times the weight, where C sums per unit: values agree with R to rounding). Its columns are R's (`coxph.fit`, `agreg.fit`, survival 3.5-8): one whose values all lie in {−1, 0, 1} is neither centred nor scaled (`nocenter`); another is centred by its mean without an entry column (`coxfit6`), and with one by the value of the first unit `agfit4` sorts (the latest to leave in the first stratum, the first given among ties, of the units whose follow-up spans an event time of their stratum, the only ones `agreg.fit` counts, found here on the times as they are where `agreg.fit` shifts each stratum's by a multiple of the range and so, at a time one ulp from an event time, can round a unit in or out; `agfit4` subtracts it inside its loop), and scaled by the units counted over the sum of their distances from that centre; coefficients and variance are reported on the columns' own scale, and the certificate and ascent run on the scaled ones. A column whose spread from its centre, or whose scale's square, no double holds is left out as unscalable (values not finite or beyond ±1e308, within about 1e-154 of one another, or spread beyond about 1e162; R stops on an infinite value and leaves out a unit with a NaN, where this leaves the column out; R stops on some, and on others gives a coefficient of order 1e153 without a standard error, or one of order 1e-166 with a standard error of 0); a fit whose coefficients or variance on the columns' own scale are not finite has not converged. With an entry column, where R's centre can leave η far from 0, every weight is exp(η − c), c moved to the mean η of the units at risk whenever that is more than 200 from it and the sums already made, where any are, rescaled with it, as `agfit4` and `zph2` recentre, but for the sums and log-likelihood terms of a tied time's events already added, which R leaves at the old c and this moves with the rest (`zph2`'s second shift, of η at the end of a time, changes only rounding and is not taken); without one, as in `coxfit6` and `zph1`, it is exp(η). As in R, a unit whose η lies beyond `exp`'s range from that mean overflows, and the fit then does not converge. A column that `cholesky2` finds dependent on those before it, in the information at 0 on the scaled columns, is dropped, as R reports it NA; R decides at every iteration and may keep, in a narrow band of near dependence (a column within about 10^−6 of a combination of others), a column this drops, whose values there R cannot give to any precision; this departure is stated. `coxph.proportional_hazards` is `cox.zph(transform = "km")`'s global test with strata of a fit that converged: the Kaplan–Meier transform of time over every unit pooled across strata, less its mean over their events, the covariates centred by their unweighted mean over every unit, risk sets per stratum, the terms' own score taken as 0, the statistic by partial pivoting; none where the extended information, each row and column divided by the square root of its diagonal, has less than full rank by `cholesky2`, where R's `solve` fails; R's also fails where only the columns' scales make its reciprocal condition number fall below ε (columns of scale 1e6 beside 1e-6), and there this gives the test, and near that bound the two differ; and R's `cox.zph` stops where its recentring overflows `exp` ("exp overflow due to covariates"), where this, which rescales only sums that hold something, gives the test (on the fixture's far unit, 1.1065708983862 as 80-digit arithmetic gives it, within 1e-10, and on its strata far apart, 3.396175775074547, within 1e-9). `zph2` counts a unit its sweep meets past its entry (one whose follow-up spans no event time) as at risk, so that where one is its sums are never reset and keep the rounding of the units that left, an error that grows with their weight without bound; this resets them as the fit does and agrees there with exact arithmetic, not with R (on the fixture's case R is 3.4e-5 from it). `reference/coxph.R` holds 36 designs (numbers, 0/1 and −1/0/1 columns, integers, a repeated, a constant and a summed column, columns of scale 1e6 and 1e-6, every unit twice, one to three strata, with and without entries, ties; a unit far from the others, on which R recentres; a near dependence; a risk set that empties; a unit whose follow-up spans no event time; a sentinel value far from the others without entries; strata far apart with entries; every event at one time, on which R's `cox.zph` fails and this gives none): `fit` agrees with R within 1e-10 (relative, with a floor of 1) in coefficient, standard error, the whole variance and log-likelihood, with R's iterations, dropping exactly R's NA columns, and the test within 1e-8; where the information is ill-conditioned (the near dependence, the far unit) rounding is amplified by its condition, and they agree within 1e-5; with an entry column the sweep adds and subtracts each unit, whose cancellation, R's and this port's alike, can leave the two some 1e-8 apart where strata sit far apart (both about that far from exact arithmetic), and is not bounded: where the units that have left outweigh those at risk by more than 2^53 (η spread over the sweep beyond about 37), the sums of those at risk are lost, in R and here alike, the certificate reads the same sums, and the fit does not converge where the exact maximum exists (a second review's 20 units with entries: both stop at −0.28 where the maximum is at −1.58). On cohort indicators in one stratum it is `coxfit.fit` within 1e-12. The weights' sums are C's operations (`ieee`); a unit's η is summed correctly rounded. Every pass spends its work (D350): at `MAX_INPUT_CELLS`' shapes (110,000 units × 13 columns, with entries and without, 250,000 × 3 with entries, 500,000 × 1) the longest stretch between looks measured 0.84 s (a sort of 500,000 rows) and a fit with its test took up to 16.7 s, on this project's 4-core container (a second review measured 1.06 s and 29 s on an idle one: the figures are the machine's) | `survival.cox` needs the model of any design, with strata, before its separation rule and its analysis can be built on it; R's handling of columns is what makes the fit R's where R's is right, and the certificate is what makes it right where R's is not, within what its sums hold; the recentring is what lets an entry column's centre, which R chooses, stand far from the data |
| D360 | Separation and identification in the Cox model of a design (M3.3d; revises §9.5's rows for `survival.cox`, D140's "dropped" and D158; builds on D356, D359) | `analyses.coxph.separated` decides every column of a design by its likelihood's recession cone C = {d : v·d ≥ 0 for every pair}, a pair being an event e and a unit h at risk at its time in its stratum (entry < t ≤ time) and v = x_e − x_h, in exact arithmetic (`analyses.cone`). Each column is scaled exactly to integers (a power of 2 a column, which changes no decision). First, `fit`'s columns, as R takes them: the unscalable left out, then those `cholesky2` drops at 0 (R's NA). Then the span of the v's, found without forming the pairs (every event at t joins its stratum's risk set at t, and a unit's risk sets are those of an interval of event times, so the pairs' graph has the components one sweep per stratum finds, and the span is that of each unit's x less its component's first), in an exact echelon form over the kept columns then the dropped: every column in the support of its orthogonal complement L_0 (an exact dependency, a column constant within strata, one that differs only on units in no pair) is not identified (`zero_variance`, every column of the dependency, where R drops only the later), and the echelon form's non-pivots (the later of each exact dependency, the dropped among them) are fixed at 0, so that every d is uniquely a d′ zero there plus an element of L_0 and the cone is the cone of the pivots plus L_0; nothing else leaves the model, so that the other estimates are R's. A column `cholesky2` drops that is not exactly dependent (a dependency that rounding hides, x3 = fl(x1/10 + x2/10), or the near-dependence band of D359) leaves the model as R leaves it, and its partners are decided as R decides them (a stated departure: exact dependencies, which integers and 0/1 columns give, make every column of theirs `zero_variance`; one rounding hides does not). Over the kept pivots, the cone by levels: at each, g = Σ v over the level's pairs (Σ_e (n x_e − Σ_h x_h), running sums per class) and the exact programme max g·d over the level's cone and the box [−1, 1]^m, positive exactly where the cone is more than its lineality; where it is, every class is split by the exact value x·d (no pair has x_h·d > x_e·d, the pairs with x_h·d < x_e·d leave, and tied events, which pair both ways, never split), and the next level starts from the classes; at most one more level than the kept columns' rank. The programme is solved as its dual, min Σ(μ⁺ + μ⁻) subject to −Σ λ_p v_p + μ⁺ − μ⁻ = c, λ, μ ≥ 0 with m rows, whose simplex multipliers are d, by a revised simplex over integers (the basis's determinant and adjugate, each pivot an exact division), Dantzig's rule and Bland's after 20 degenerate pivots, from μ⁺_i where c_i ≥ 0 and μ⁻_i elsewhere, by columns: pricing is one exact pass that finds each event's most violated pair (its risk set's largest x·d, a heap with lazy deletion), the 16 most violated added a round, rounds between passes priced over the last pass's pairs alone, the programme ending only at a pass that finds none violated, so that its d lies exactly in the cone; the cuts found are pooled across levels and programmes, a cut serving one while its units share a class; at an optimum of 0 the basis is the Farkas certificate −c = Σ λ v, λ ≥ 0. The pairs left at the last level are the cone's implicit equalities (the directions' lexicographic combination, Σ N^(K−i) d_i for N large, lies in the cone and is strict on every pair that left), so the span of the cone is the orthogonal complement of theirs, span_F. A column is estimated where span_F holds its unit vector; otherwise, where its coordinate keeps one sign on the cone, `separation` with that sign (+1: the coefficient runs to +∞; −1: to −∞), read from the first level's direction whose coordinate is not 0 (the lexicographic combination's) or else from the programmes max ±d_j over the strata; where it takes both, `zero_variance` (the limit leaves it undetermined: R's value depends on its start). Precedence: unscalable, then `zero_variance` (L_0's support, then `cholesky2`'s drop), then the signs, then the finite part's own drop, which takes only a column not separated (a separated column's value in the finite part is never reported, and a rounding residue there does not undo its exact sign). On cohort indicators this is D356's graph rule (LOWER −1, HIGHER +1, APART `zero_variance`); a cohort or covariate level with no events has d = −e_j in the cone and so is `separation` (−∞) or, where its coordinate can also be positive, `zero_variance`, where D158 dropped it; a view of cohorts alone without a stratum stays D356's (D364). Every pass and pivot spends its work (D350), an integer operation by its operands' limbs (a pass by the design's largest value's): at `MAX_INPUT_CELLS`' shapes, on this project's 4-core container, 110,000 units × 13 columns took 18.7 s without separation and 20.9 s with an exact dependency, and 32.3 s with a column separated (about 6 s at 20,000 units), each with 6–7 s more for its test, so that at the cap a separated design passes the call's 30 s and is refused as late (D350); 250,000 × 3 with entries took 28.6 s and 500,000 × 1 21.8 s, both separated, and the longest stretch between looks measured 1.11 s (a sort of 500,000 rows); hostile shapes cost more and are refused as late in the same way (a second review measured 13 columns of values spanning 1e±150, every one separated, at 93 s for 5,000 units, the master's pivots on large integers, and a design separated only up to rounding, finite in exact arithmetic, at 121 s for 20,000 units, D359's ascent over a nearly flat likelihood), the longest stretch between looks there 0.16 s | #61's plan, after four cold reviews: R's `coxph` flags separation after fitting, by a heuristic that misses and misreports; the likelihood's cone decides it exactly, and in exact arithmetic, since rounding residues must decide neither separation nor dependence; "separation" that also took the undetermined columns contradicted D356 and R; removing every column of a dependency changed the other estimates; an echelon form taken before `cholesky2`'s drop fixed a column R keeps |
| D361 | The finite part of a design's Cox model and its test of proportional hazards (M3.3d; refines D357 and D359) | The finite part is the model stratified by each unit's class, its stratum and its covariates less span_F (the reduced echelon form's remainder over every scalable column, so that two units share a class exactly where their difference lies in the pairs' span at the last level: the pairs within the classes are the cone's implicit equalities, whichever directions the programmes found), fitted by `coxph.fit` over the kept pivots but span_F's non-pivots (fixed at 0: every estimated column is a pivot, so the estimated coefficients and their variance do not depend on the choice), `agreg.fit`'s units per class; its estimated coefficients are the limit of R's full fit (the reviews' e1: 0.7664; b6: R's fit of the one stratum that identifies it, within 1e-9), its log-likelihood the supremum of the full model's (every R fit from starts 0 and ±3 that keeps its separated columns lies below it, the best within 1e-6), and only its estimated coefficients and their variance are reported, its other columns' values depending on the choice. A column not separated that the finite part's own `cholesky2` drops (its information is exactly of full rank; only a near dependence can), or that it cannot scale (with an entry column its units are counted per class, and a column's spread over them can fall below what a double holds), is `zero_variance`, and the other estimates are then the fit without it (stated); every column has exactly one label: estimated, `separation`, `zero_variance` or unscalable. `coxph.separated_hazards` is the global test of proportional hazards of the estimated columns in the finite part's model, `cox.zph(transform = "km")`'s: the classes are its strata, its other free columns are fitted beside the estimated ones and their score taken as 0 as the terms' own are, and the Kaplan–Meier transform is pooled over every unit, as R's `cox.zph` pools it (D359). Where the columns' dependencies are exact, it depends on neither their order nor which columns of a dependency are fixed (one that rounding hides is decided as R decides it, by order, D360) (the estimated columns are pivots in every order, and a score test with its other columns fitted is invariant to their reparametrisation); where nothing is `separation`, the classes are the strata and the finite fit is R's, and it is R's formula for the estimated columns' term (where R's `cox.zph` stops because another term's or the global extended information is singular, R's formula for that term still gives this; D359's reciprocal-condition departure stands), its global test where R estimates exactly those (a column constant within strata, or a dependency rounding hides, is R's NA and not tested); on cohort indicators all estimated it is D357's (within 1e-8). Where a cohort or column is separated it tests the finite part, which R's `cox.zph`, testing a fit it cannot certify, does not; D357, which pools the transform over the reference's strongly connected component alone, stays that of views of cohorts alone (M3.3e). `reference/cone.R` holds 17 designs (the plan reviews' e1, e2 with ties and entries, e3, b1, four exact and within-stratum dependencies, b5, b6 alone and with two events tied, a separated combination of two columns, a cohort apart from the reference, an exact sum beside an independent column, a large column whose exact partner R drops first, a separated contrast of two columns beside a column the finite part fits and does not report, and a column constant within strata beside a stratum whose units share their covariates) and R's fits from three starts; where R's fit of the finite part alone is simple, its coefficient and `cox.zph`'s test of the estimated columns in the finite part (its classes the strata, the transform pooled over every unit), which this gives within 1e-9 | The cone decides which terms are finite; their values need the fit of the part that stays finite, whose classes must not depend on the directions the programmes happened to find, and their test the finite part's model; a closure of the classes the estimated columns' likelihood holds, tried first, depended on which column of a dependency is fixed and departed from R's own test on designs R fits |
| D362 | `survival.cox` in parts (M3.3e-1; refines §9.5's row, §15 and D358) | `survival.cox` comes in three parts: M3.3e-1 its model of cohorts and covariates as a function of each member's cells (`analyses.cox.model`, D363–D365), held to R, with no registry entry and no output; M3.3e-2 the analysis (its entry, `CoxParams`, whose covariates M3.3f makes a union of forms told by their members without changing an id (D366, D370), phase 2's refusals, canonical params, dispatch, caveats, readback and goldens); M3.3f predicates as covariates. A stratum by dataset is one stratum while a view's cohorts come from one release (`MIXED_RELEASES`) and every entry has `cross_dataset: null` (D348), and arrives with M6's cross-dataset analyses | Predicates need a clause's truth listed per member, with the evaluator and SQL agreeing (which the question variables' listing gives, D371), independent of the model; two cold plan reviews found the analysis too large for one reviewable PR, and the model and the entry independent |

Per position, each covariate's, the stratum's and the endpoint's counts, over every member (so that `n` + `excluded_units` is `n_true` for each), are values (`CoxPosition.variables`), and the envelope's `analysed` is the complete cases, with no `variables`. Coding over the complete cases of every position pooled: a number as it is, a boolean 1 against 0 (its level `true`, its baseline `false`), a category one 0/1 column per level but its baseline, the most common level among those with an event among the complete cases (ties by the smallest canonical text, as UTF-16 code units), an ordered category's levels in its declared order and another's in canonical order; a covariate whose column is constant over the complete cases (a category of one level, a constant number) has one term, `zero_variance`, and no column. Each value takes the first reason that applies: `overlapping_cohorts` (every value, where the view allows overlap and cohorts share units); `no_units` where there is no complete case and `no_events` where none has an event (every value); a cohort term's `no_units` or `no_events`, the reference's reason first and then its own position's, where either has no complete case or no event (the covariates' terms still come from the fit, which leaves the reference's units apart as D179's limit does); a constant column's `zero_variance`; the fit's `separation` (with the direction of the cone's sign or D356's standing) or `zero_variance`; `not_converged` for the estimated terms; `zero_variance` for a variance that is not positive; `separation` without a direction for a ratio or a bound beyond 2^53 − 1 or that rounds to 0 (as a well-identified coefficient can be for a column's units alone; M3.3e-2 states the columns it takes). | D365 | `survival.cox`'s values (M3.3e-1; refines §8.1 and §8.2) | `CoxValues`: per position (`CoxPosition`), the events among its complete cases and the counts of D363; for the view (`CoxView`), `terms` (`CoxTerm`: a cohort, by `position`, or a covariate, by its index, a categorical or boolean one's `level` against its `baseline` as data, none for a constant one; the hazard ratio, its Wald interval and Wald p-value, `chi_squared_p((β/SE)², 1)` as R's `summary` gives it; and `direction`, `infinity` or `zero`, for a term separated by the cone or by D356), in order: the cohort terms in view order, then each covariate's in parameter order; `covariate_tests` (`CovariateTest`, the joint Wald test βᵀV⁻¹β of each covariate of two or more columns, which no baseline changes); and `proportional_hazards` (`ModelTest`, naming the indices of the terms it tested, its degrees of freedom their number, none where it was not computed, so that a view of one cohort and covariates has one). `reference/survcox.R` holds six designs (two and three cohorts, one cohort; numbers, booleans and categories with missing values; a category whose counts tie; strata, with and without entries), R's fit with the baseline built by D363's rule: every term's ratio, bounds and p-value, every joint test and `cox.zph`'s global test agree within 1e-9 (the p-values and test within 1e-8), and n and the events R counts are the complete cases' | Terms carry what R's `summary.coxph` shows; the joint test and the test of proportional hazards are what the data give whatever the baseline; a test that names positions needs two cohorts |
| D366 | `survival.cox`'s entry and parameters (M3.3e-2; refines §9.5, D290, D317 and D362) | Version 1.0.0; `requires` an endpoint on the unit table and 1 to 6 cohorts; `uses_reference` and `assumes_independent_groups` (cohorts that share units refuse it without `overlap: "allow"`, and under it every value is `overlapping_cohorts`, D364); `cross_dataset` `null` until M6; no randomness; `min_group_n` 10, `min_events` 5; methods `wald`, `recession_cone` and `grambsch_therneau`, which its intervals and tests name; `refused` under any *k* (§8.4, D351, D353). Parameters (`CoxParams`): `endpoint` (as `survival.km`'s, D347), `covariates` (0 to 8 variables of §9.2, one value per unit, in the terms' order; none by default), `stratum` (a variable, or `null`) and `level` (0.95, at most 1 − 10⁻⁹); canonical as `{"covariates", "endpoint", "level", "stratum"}`, each covariate and the stratum its variable's canonical form (D325), the stratum `null` for none, so that the covariates' order is part of the id and how they are written (names, key order) is not. A covariate is a variable, told by its members: M3.3f adds `{"predicate": <clause>}`, whose canonical form no variable's can be, so no id changes then. Erasure reads the variable covariates and the stratum as a view's variables and the predicate covariates as a view's predicates (D290, D325, D370), in a canonical view (the reading of D348 for survival views' times, which matched every `survival.*` view and read only times, now reads them too) and as written: their `values` and `empty` constants on their columns, their `where` clauses. `registry.LATER` is empty from here; D317's mechanism stays for a later core analysis | A tag in every covariate would put the form where a client must write it and, in Pydantic's error locations, between a covariate and its members; the members tell a variable from a predicate already, and a variable's canonical form keeps its id whatever M3.3f adds |
| D367 | `survival.cox`'s phase 2 (M3.3e-2; refines §9.5 and D363) | A covariate's coding is its resolved variable's: `some`, `every` and a boolean column a boolean; a `number`, `integer` or `time_offset` column, a `count`, a `mean`, and `max` or `min` of numbers a number; a `category` column a category, in its permissible values' order where it is `ordered` and in canonical order otherwise; a `string` column a category in canonical order; `max` or `min` of an ordered category a category in its order. After the disclosure check (so that under *k* only `WITHHELD_UNDER_K` is said, D353), each covariate and the stratum is refused at its place: a column of no coding (dates and datetimes; resolution refuses their aggregates, D325) `NOT_SUPPORTED` at `…/column`, listing the datatypes taken; an identifier column's values (§5.4; not a `count`, which reads none) `INVALID_VALUE` at `…/column`, and an aggregate whose rows' `where` tests an identifier column or the endpoint's own columns (as D370 refuses a predicate's leaves) `INVALID_VALUE` at the leaf; the endpoint's own time or status column (an endpoint is on the unit table, and resolution takes no question or aggregate of a unit's own column) `INVALID_VALUE` there; `bins` `INVALID_VALUE` at `…/bins`; `count: "rows"`, `ids` and `cohort` leaves in `where` and pack leaves as every view variable's (D335, D345). Refused once the data show it, each where it is written: a unit's time or entry beyond ±(2^53 − 1) `NOT_SUPPORTED` at `/views/<i>`, as `survival.km`'s (D348), so that a view `survival.km` refuses has no Cox model either; a number covariate with a complete case's value beyond ±(2^53 − 1) `NOT_SUPPORTED` at `…/covariates/<j>` (an integer beyond it has no exact double, and rounding would merge values that coding tells apart; an excluded member's value never refuses); a category's level or baseline that a term shows (a constant covariate's shows none) longer than 10,000 characters `LIMIT_EXCEEDED` there (`text_characters`), and one that is not Unicode text (a lone surrogate or a noncharacter) `NOT_SUPPORTED` there (`cox.NonTextLevel`, `analyses.common.unwritable`, D382); more than 8 columns after coding `LIMIT_EXCEEDED` at `…/covariates` (`cox_parameters`), and more than 100 stratum levels at `…/stratum` (`strata`); and a number covariate whose column the design's fit cannot scale (its spread's scale, or that scale's square, is no double, D359) `NOT_SUPPORTED` at `…/covariates/<j>`, where D364's model labelled it `zero_variance`, which it is not. A well-identified coefficient whose ratio or bound lies beyond 2^53 − 1 stays `separation` without a direction (D364). The readback names the endpoint as `survival.km`'s does (the same words), the cohorts' terms versus the reference by position, each covariate by its variable's readback and how it enters (a number's ratio per 1 more, in its column's units where declared, a `count`'s per row more; a boolean 1 for true against 0; a category one term per level against the most common level among those with an event, an ordered one in its declared order), the stratum, the level, the tests and the complete cases. One chart: each term's hazard ratio as a point where it is shown and its Wald interval as a rule where both bounds are, on a log scale, a row per term drawn, in the terms' order, labelled `cohort <name>` or `covariate <j>`, with `: <level> vs <baseline>` for a level, the level and the baseline each quoted as a JSON string | A result's caveats and chart are its data's; a point drawn only with both bounds would hide a ratio the result shows |

Its canonical form is `{"predicate": <its canonical clause>}`, existence's predicate form, which no variable's form holds; the covariates keep their written order. It codes as a boolean: 1 where the clause is TRUE and 0 where it is FALSE, a value leaf on a cell that does not apply being FALSE before any negation (§6.4), so that a negated one is TRUE there, where a boolean column excludes such a cell; a unit for which it is UNKNOWN is left out of the complete cases, counted under its reasons (§6.6). A `some` or `every` question covariate is not flipped: its lift is the variable's own, as for every analysis's question variables.
