| D372 | The thread-count determinism tests (M3.4; refines §9.3's Scope, §13 and D315) | `tests/core/determinism/` compares, on one store, the answers of catalogues whose query workers differ only in `query_threads`: each answer bar its issuances (ids, digests, values, `analysed`, caveats and readbacks). The fixture is an orchard of 1,000,000 trees (a string key; categories, one ordered; a boolean; a number with missing cells; `ring`, about 200,000 distinct numbers; `block`, 127 categories it does not declare, whose groups DuckDB gives in an order that differs across threads, so the server must sort them; a list; time offsets and a status with unknowns, the endpoint `ep:felled` with delayed entry), 1,558,441 harvests (129,870 trees have none; one tree in eleven has three more, weighing 0.1, 0.2 and 0.3, whose sum as doubles depends on its order (they are written after every other harvest, in other row groups, which the order of a sum's terms does not need); one weight in thirteen is missing) and 519,480 weighings, each row written from its number; the harvests' coverage is `parents: "all"`, the weighings' lists every other harvest (`weighed_harvests`, 779,221 rows) and is scoped to grades A and B, so a harvest outside the list is `NOT_COVERED`, which only `assessed` drops, and a question through both depends on its lift (§6.5; a scope alone gives `OUT_OF_SCOPE`, which both rules drop). It is published once per module by a test-only pack's importer: the core's file importer reads the first 3,000 trees' rows and the curation is written into its descriptors as `imported` entries (the fields it inferred keep their `proposed` statuses), and the full files are the raw snapshots, so no cell of the million is inferred; about a minute and 2 GB. Its table blobs are written with row groups of 2^17 rows, and each holds at least as many as the threads compared, since DuckDB scans a file's row groups in parallel (D373); a test pins each table's rows and groups (8, 12, 4 and 6), and that `block` declares no values and holds 127 while `soil` declares its 3. The calls, and the SQL paths each drives: `count_cohort` of three cohorts (counts, a lift, `min_count`, `every`, a negated list leaf) and `compare.existence` of 16 predicates over three cohorts (the crossing, the other lift rule's counts included, `lift_differs` above 0), each at 1, 4, 4 again and 3 threads; one `run_analysis` of `summary.distribution` over all the trees (joint values, a `mean`'s value rows, `max`'s extremes, `count` and `some` over empty rows), `compare.columns` over two cohorts (the units they share, none as they are disjoint: overlap is refused by default), `summary.members` of a third of the trees (a listing past its 150,000th key), `survival.km` and `survival.cox` over small cohorts (inputs with the endpoint's rows and delayed entry; cox with a number, an ordered category, a `mean`, a predicate that holds a lift and a stratum), and a test pack's analysis handed its inputs (a column, a `mean`, a `some` and the endpoint's rows), each at 1 and 4. `validate_document` and `explain` run no query and are not compared. Every issuance compared computed its values (`cache_hit` false): the catalogues compared turn the result cache off (`Catalog.cache`, D376), so no answer is compared with a cached copy of another, and each call's thread counts start at one and hold another; a test of the default suite shows that a catalogue with it on, at other threads, is given the first's content. The tests carry the `million` marker, which `addopts` deselects; CI runs them in a job of its own (`determinism`, beside the server checks, `timeout-minutes` 25, its step 20), and a test of the default suite checks that the marker is registered and deselected, that each module there and below but its conftests assigns it at its top level (pytest collects `*_test.py` too), that CI's `determinism` job runs them with `-m million` in a step with no `if`, `continue-on-error`, `shell` or `||`, followed by a step that reads its junit report and requires one passing test per test function there, none skipped (`.github/scripts/determinism_report.py`), so that no edit to the step can leave the job green without them, and that the core step passes no `-m`, `addopts` or environment of its own, any of which could replace `addopts`' `-m` | §9.3 requires identical digests at thread counts over a million rows; a store built through the file importer's inference and a curation session took about four minutes and 3 GB; a release whose tables are each one row group ran as if on one thread, and a fixture without a scoped coverage two steps down never reached the lift's paths |
| D373 | Scale, measured (M3.4; amends §14 and the figures of D318, D327 and D333) | On D372's fixture, with 4 cores, each call's wall time and the least `query_memory` among those tried (2, 3, 4, 6 and 8 GiB): `count_cohort` of three cohorts 7.0 s on one thread and 2.1 s on four, within 2 GiB; the crossing of 16 predicates over three cohorts (three of them two steps down, whose lifts are crossed under both rules) is refused at one thread with 2 and 3 GiB and takes 38 s with 4 GiB, 21 s on two threads, and on four is refused with 4 GiB and takes 12.5 s with 6 GiB, so the tests give 8 GiB; over six cohorts it takes 71 s on one thread and 25 s on four; the document of five views (distribution, columns, members, km and cox) takes 88 s on one thread within 2 GiB, while on four the 2 GiB default refuses its member listing (`/views/2`) and 8 GiB takes 42 s; the pack's echo 7.9 s and 2.9 s within 2 GiB. So under the defaults the crossing is refused for memory and the five views, and the six-cohort crossing, for time. Before the fixture listed covered harvests and had the late ones, nine predicates on the tree's columns and one step down, or three two steps down, each fit in 2 GiB at one thread (13 s and 16 s): DuckDB's memory grows with a crossing's predicates, their related rows and its threads. The defaults stay (one thread, 2 GiB, 25 s): a call past them is refused with its limit (`LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, `query_memory` or `query_seconds`), never approximated, and a figure of one fixture built into the configuration would refuse deployments that run well; §14, `aibi.example.toml` and `config.py` say that `query_memory` should grow with `query_threads` and a document's predicates. A table blob of fewer than 2^20 rows (`parquet.ROW_GROUP`) is one row group and DuckDB scans it on one thread whatever `query_threads`, so threads gain little below a million rows (a scan of a one-group million-row table took 3.2 s on one thread and 3.5 s on four, and 3.1 s and 0.9 s in groups of 122,880 rows); smaller groups change every blob, and so every manifest and id, and are left to a later change. Earlier figures, on a fixture of one row group per table, no scoped coverage and predicates of the unit's columns and one step down, as D318's (research for this slice): a `mean` over a million units 10.2 s on one thread and 8.6 s on four, the server at 430 MB (D327); `summary.members` at the cap 5.6 s and 6.0 s, the server at 211 MB, and eight concurrent listings at the cap under a 30 s deadline all answered by it, with two workers two finishing (15 s and 23 s) and six refused as late at about 29 s, with eight workers all eight refused as late at about 28.5 s, the server peaking at 526 MB and 674 MB (D333); a crossing of 16 predicates over six cohorts, 46 s on one thread and 15 s on four with 3 GiB (D318) | D318, D327 and D333 left their figures at a million units to M3.4; D318's crossing was measured on predicates of the unit's columns and one step down, and §14's promise of 16 predicates and 6 cohorts at 4 threads within 2 GiB did not hold on related rows |
| D374 | The result cache in three parts, and a result's digested content apart from its rendering (M3.5a; refines D315, §15 and D120) | M3.5 comes in three parts: M3.5a separates a view's digested content from its rendering; M3.5b is the store's result cache (its tables, invalidation, limits and erasure); M3.5c is `run_analysis`'s lookup, hits and fills. In M3.5a, `results.digested` gives a view's `Digested`: its cohorts, their population, the units analysed, the values as the analysis left them and the caveats the envelope carries (the analysis's, the view's static caveats and `NOT_ESTIMABLE`), with their digest (§7.6), which is what the result's id determines. A `Digested` takes its digest itself and holds typed values only if they give its values as JSON text, so none disagrees with itself. `Digested.content` writes it as JSON text, every number as carried, with the result id it is of, its digest and a hash of the rest; `Digested.read` reads it back for a view only when the hash holds (the digest rounds numbers and reduces caveats, so only the hash sees a change below its resolution), it is of that view's result, names the view's cohorts as the view does and, taken again, gives the digest it was written with (so a mis-keyed row, or one whose content changed, is refused rather than rendered beside another view's readback), its values checked by the analysis's values model (`CoreAnalysis.values`, beside its parameters' model), which gives the typed values its charts read; a pack's analysis's content is not read, its values having no model of the core's, so a pack's result is never given from the cache. `results.render` gives the envelope of digested content for one issuance: `values_from` names the issuance whose queries gave the content (a cache hit when it is not the issuance itself, §8.1), and the derivation, source, readbacks and labels come from the view as this call canonicalised it, the charts drawing the content's typed values with the view's labels, so content filled under one document's cohort names is rendered with another's (D84, D120); the caveats, their messages included, are the content's; the derivation's engine and packs' versions are this call's, which a hit's lookup requires to be the filler's (M3.5b); `envelope`, for a view run now, is `render` of its `digested` content with itself as `values_from`. No output changes: every golden stands, and over each golden document the content read back renders the envelope but its issuance. The written content also holds `text_format`, the wording it was written under (hashed, not digested); content of another wording, or none, is a miss the call fills again, logged as nothing (D399). | The cold plan review of M3.5 found three parts that can each be reviewed alone: digesting and rendering were one function (`envelope`), the cache's store can be tested without the tool, and planning a call's hits and misses is where the risks lie; charts read typed values, which a hit must rebuild from stored content |
| D375 | The result cache's store (M3.5b; refines §8.1, §12.2, §14 and D120) | The app DB's migration 7 adds `result_cache` (a result id, the issuance that filled it, the engine and the packs' versions that ran its queries, its effective *k*, its bytes and when it was last used, by a counter the cache keeps, never the clock), `result_cache_contents` (the content, apart, so that marking a row used writes a small one); the manifests a row read are its result's own in the log (`derivation_releases`, kept while any issuance of the result is, so while its filler is; migration 7 indexes them by manifest, which `LOG_RELEASE_FACTOR` still covers). Its triggers keep it whatever path changes the log, taking nothing from the caller on trust: a row is filled only by an issuance of its own result whose queries ran (not a hit), whose derivation erasure has not taken, with that issuance's engine and packs' versions and the *k* its result id holds, and only while every release its result read is published and not withdrawn (a draft is never cached); once filled, only its use changes (its content, filler, engine, versions, *k* and bytes are fixed, its content written once, counted in its bytes and gone only with it, and a result is removed before it is filled again, so no `REPLACE` changes either); it goes with its filler (a foreign key that cascades when pruning or erasure removes the issuance) and with any manifest its result read when that is withdrawn, in the withdrawal's transaction (§12.2, D173); `result_cache_usage` keeps its bytes (its content's pages, charged as the log's rows are, `stored_sql`, the engine's and packs' text twice over, and 512 bytes a row for its keys, counters and index entries), apart from `log_bytes`, which counts only what pruning frees. `ResultCache` (`Store.results`): `lookup(result, engine, packs)` finds a row only when this engine and these versions filled it (a result id holds the packs' results versions alone, §7.6, so a row another engine or other versions filled is a miss, and so a hit always names a source this engine ran); `fill(db, result=, issuance=, content=)`, in the caller's transaction (the one that records the call's issuances; `RuntimeError` otherwise), caches nothing when `result_bytes` is 0, the row would take more than a quarter of it or a release the result read is not published or is withdrawn (a draft's result is declined before anything is written), refuses a filler that is not an issuance of the result whose queries ran with its own writes undone (a savepoint), so a refused fill removes and evicts nothing, evicts the least recently used rows until it fits, keeps the first fill of a result by one engine and versions (a second whose content differs is logged as an error by its kind alone, since one id gave two contents, §7.6), and replaces a row another engine or other versions filled; `touch(db, result)` marks it used in the hit's transaction; `purge(floor)` removes the rows cached under a lower disclosure floor (their *k* below it, or none), which no call gives again once the floor is raised, since their ids hold their *k*; `clear()` empties it (§13.4's provenance test); when the store opens, `trim()` evicts the least recently used rows until the cache is within `result_bytes` (all of them at 0), so a lowered bound holds at once, and the server purges the rows under its floor when it starts. Erasure empties the whole cache (a redactor of `result_cache`): it is evictable, so a result that held the person, or whose cohorts' derivations erasure took, is never given again, and the redaction's `VACUUM` rewrites no cache. `[cache] result_bytes` configures it (§14) | The cold plan review of M3.5: a hit that bumps `used_at` on the row holding the content would rewrite its overflow pages under `secure_delete`; triggers and foreign keys keep the cache right on every path (pruning, erasure, withdrawal) where code could forget one; the id holds neither the engine nor a pack's full version |
| D376 | `run_analysis`'s hits and fills (M3.5c; refines §8.1, §12.2, D318 and D372) | After the views are checked (phase 2's refusals, the disclosure classes and every limit, §8.4, D353), each view whose result may be cached (not `summary.members` nor a pack's analysis, which list or hand their members' values, D333, D353, and none over a draft, §8.1) and whose result the store's cache holds, filled by this engine with these packs' versions and read back as its content (D374), is a hit; a row that does not read back for its view is a miss, logged by its kind alone, and the call's transaction drops it before its fill replaces it (so it is never taken for a second fill of one id with another content, §7.6). A hit leaves every plan: no query of it runs, its cohorts are counted only if a view that misses names them, and a call whose views all hit runs no worker (a catalogue without workers still refuses first, as every call does, D300); its envelope is rendered from the cached content for this call (its source, readbacks, labels and charts this call's, D120), naming as `values_from` the issuance that filled the row. Every other view that may be cached fills it with its content as computed, by the issuance this call records for it (the log gives the row its engine, versions, *k* and manifests, D375). Hits and fills are checked and written in the transaction that records the call's issuances (`issue_all`'s `then`, after they are recorded and before the deadline is asked, so they are kept exactly when the issuances are): each cohort a hit names must be recorded and not erased (erasure empties the cache, D375, so this is the check that holds if a row outlived it), and a hit's filler must still be in the log (`SourceGoneError`, D289; one that is of another result, or a hit, is an error, which the cache's triggers make impossible); otherwise the call runs once more without the cache, canonicalised again as a first run of the document would be (so a hit never names what the log no longer holds). A row evicted, cleared or replaced between lookup and recording is still a hit, its content in hand and its filler kept. A hit is given under the query limits it was filled under: it runs no query, so none of this call's limits applies to it. `Catalog.cache` (on by default) turns both off for a catalogue: the thread-count determinism tests (D372) and tests of planning use it. | §8.1 and D120 fix what a hit gives; the plan review of M3.5 found that a hit which records no count issuance could return a result whose cohort's id erasure took, and that the filler may be pruned between lookup and recording, while an evicted row's content in hand is still right |
| D377 | M3.2e in three parts (M3.2e; refines D324, D335 and §15) | M3.2e (#52) comes in three stacked parts, each a PR with its tests, their decisions numbered in the order they land: M3.2e-1 a variable's `count: "rows"` in `summary.distribution` (D378), withheld under any *k* by a mechanism for a disclosed analysis's withheld forms (D379); M3.2e-2a per-category memberships of multi-valued categorical columns (their semantics, the reference evaluator, SQL, canonical form, readback and chart), withheld under any *k* by M3.2e-1's mechanism; M3.2e-2b the memberships' disclosure rule and a brute force over worlds generated from data, which lifts 2a's refusal (D353: a rule and a brute force in one change). Until M3.2e-2a lands, a multi-valued category, boolean or list column without an `aggregate` stays `NOT_SUPPORTED` in descriptive analyses, naming M3.2e-2a (`resolve.PER_CATEGORY`) and listing as alternatives the aggregates its column takes (`resolve.aggregates_taken`: `count`, `max` and `min` of an ordered category alone, `some` and `every`; of a list's items `some` and `every` alone), as `AGGREGATE_REQUIRED` and `AGGREGATE_NOT_ALLOWED` list theirs, and `count: "rows"` but for a list and under a disclosure setting (D378, D379), those alone that run in its place (`resolve._offered`, D380: that its path allows, `resolve.aggregates_over`, that run beside the variable's `where` and `empty`, and that the view's analysis reads), and so does a list column's `count: "rows"`; 2a and 2b each get a revision of the plan before either starts. Revision 3 of the plan splits M3.2e-2a in two (D380): M3.2e-2a-1 gives memberships in the engine (`Variable.each`, their resolution, canonical form and leaf keys, the reference evaluator and SQL), with `each` `NOT_SUPPORTED` in every descriptive analysis, naming M3.2e-2a-2, as D378 held `count: "rows"` back from the analyses that assume independent groups; M3.2e-2a-2 gives them in `summary.distribution` (their values, the joint `analysed`, readback, chart, withholding under any *k* and goldens), where a descriptive analysis asks for them with an explicit `each` and the bare multi-valued categorical column becomes `AGGREGATE_REQUIRED` (`PER_CATEGORY` goes), its alternatives offering `each` but under a disclosure setting M3.2e-2b landed as D383 (the rule, the withholding wherever the path is not one open down step from the unit, and the brute force) and D384 (the engines' declared listing). | The cold review of #52's plan found a blocker and eight majors in the memberships (the categories §6.5 refuses, the counting of pairs and defaults, the joint `analysed`, worlds from data) and none left in row counts once their accounting and disclosure were fixed; row counts reuse the pooling that numeric aggregates already hold to SQL, so they can be reviewed and land alone |
| D378 | Counts of rows (M3.2e-1; refines §8.1, §9.2, §9.5, D325, D326, D327, D328 and D330) | A variable with `count: "rows"` (`schema.analyses.Variable`) takes no `aggregate`, `values` or `empty` (`CONFLICTING_MEMBERS`, the model's own check, in every analysis) and may take `where`, `lift` and, for numbers, `bins`; an analysis that assumes independent groups refuses it (`INVALID_VALUE` at `…/count`, D335). In a descriptive analysis it resolves (`resolve._rows`) to a variable of kind `rows` whose chain is a numeric aggregate's (`_pooling`: the path's down steps, the `where` on the last, the `lift` at the earlier ones, a cohort's caps and `OPEN_SCOPE`), and reads its column, so `UNDECLARED_DATATYPE` and `ROW_IDS_NOT_ALLOWED` apply; a column with one value per unit is `INVALID_VALUE` at `…/count`, and a list column `NOT_SUPPORTED` at `…/count`, naming M3.2e-2a (a list's items have no pooling to reuse; `INVALID_VALUE` from M3.2e-2a-2, D382); `summary.distribution` takes categories (category and boolean columns) and numbers (number, integer and time-offset columns) and refuses the rest `NOT_SUPPORTED` at `…/column`, `bins` on categories `INVALID_VALUE`. In a descriptive analysis, a column below the unit given neither an `aggregate` nor `count` is refused (`resolve._not_single`: `AGGREGATE_REQUIRED` for numbers, `NOT_SUPPORTED` for categories until M3.2e-2a-2, which makes it `AGGREGATE_REQUIRED` for both, D382) with `count: "rows"` among its alternatives for a number, category or boolean column, never for a list, nor under a disclosure setting (D379). Its canonical form (`canonical.variable_form`) is `{"count": "rows", "column", "rows", "lookup"?}`, which no other form holds, a view's canonical parameters adding a number's `bins`, which erasure reads as constants on its column, as a `max`'s (D290: they divide the column's values, not numbers of rows). **What is counted**: the rows a numeric aggregate pools (`Evaluator.pool`, `sql.pooled`), each once for every path through the kept children that reaches it and once for every unit that reaches it, as `count` counts them (§9.2). A unit not in scope or not closed at a step, or whose `where` or record filter is UNKNOWN, is excluded and counted in `analysed` under its reasons, as an aggregate's unit is, and its rows are not read; `analysed.n` counts the units pooled, those that reached no row included. Each pooled row's value is read through the lookups after the last down step: PRESENT, it is counted; else the row is left out under one reason (its cell's NOT_APPLICABLE, NOT_ASSESSED or NO_INFORMATION, or NO_PARENT for a lookup that reaches no row), counted in the column's `excluded_rows`, and its unit is not: an UNKNOWN `where` excludes the unit, an UNKNOWN value the row. **Shape**: two members of a position's columns, `CategoryRows` (`kind: "category_rows"`: categories listed as a category column's are without *k*, the declared ones first, zeros included, then the others its rows hold in UTF-16 order, at most `MAX_CATEGORIES`, each the rows in it over the rows with a value, `{position, predicate: null, counts: "rows"}`) and `NumberRows` (`kind: "number_rows"`: `n` the rows with a value, their mean, standard deviation, median, quartiles, minimum, maximum and histogram, divided by the view's `bins`, else the column's declared range, else the data, by the exact arithmetic of D326 and D328), each with `excluded_rows`, every reason listed, zeros included; `compare.columns`' summaries are unchanged. `UNKNOWN_EXCLUDED` is carried, affecting `/values`, where a row is left out for a reason other than NOT_APPLICABLE, in a message of its own; with no row, the statistics are `no_units`. The column's readback states its column and lookups, the rows reached and their multiplicity, and the view's adds a sentence on rows only where a column counts them; its chart is the categories' bars or the histogram, titled rows, not units. **Engines**: the evaluator gives a pooled unit the number of rows it reached and each row as read (`UnitValue.rows`), which `materialise(…, rows=True)` counts (`RowCounts`); the compiler's statement adds, beside the units excluded, the units pooled by the number of rows each reached (in `r`, so that `v` holds the column's values alone), the rows by value and the rows left out by reason, from one relation of the pooled rows joined to their values (`rows_variable`; `row_values` without skipping), every count a `COUNT` and every flag a `BIT_OR`, values grouped and never summed or averaged (§9.3); a count of rows is never listed per member (`CompileError`). A Hypothesis property holds the compiler to the evaluator on such variables beside others over random cities (lookups after the last down step, two down steps with lifts, down, up and down one relationship, so that a unit reaches each of its rows once for each of their siblings, scope columns restricted, record filters, undeclared coverage, rows NOT_APPLICABLE, NOT_ASSESSED, null, outside a list or of a dangling lookup). **Version**: `summary.distribution` stays 1.0.0: every view that ran before gives the same output (the goldens stand; `order_rows_distribution` is added), and the forms added were refused before, so no id they issue was issued and no cached row gives another; the entry's definition, `params` and `returns` change at 1.0.0, and its values model only gains members of a union, so content cached before reads back (D374) | #52 asked for counts of rows in the descriptive analyses; pooling them as `count` does gives one multiplicity, which the compiler already holds to the evaluator, and reading values as `mean` reads them, but leaving out the row rather than its unit, keeps every pooled row accounted for without losing the unit |
| D379 | Withheld forms (M3.2e-1; refines §8.4, §9.4, D329 and D353) | A `disclosed` analysis may withhold a form of its parameters under any disclosure setting: `registry.CoreAnalysis.withheld_forms` maps a member's path below the parameters (member names, `*` for any item of a list) to why, in a refusal's words, and only a disclosed analysis holds one, each path lying below its parameters' model, every name one of the members of the model it is below and every `*` an item of a list (`__post_init__`), so that a misspelt form cannot stop withholding what it names. `summary.distribution` withholds `columns/*/count`: a count of rows totals each unit's number of rows, a statistic of values that D329 withholds, and one unit's rows can be *k* or more (10 units, nine with 1 row and one with 2: under *k* = 3 their `count` is one bin of 10 units, and a row total of 11 gives back the unit with 2); from M3.2e-2a-2 it withholds `columns/*/each` too, memberships whose categories are predicates that depend on one another (D382). Under the effective *k* (the dataset's, a draft's latest published release's, D275, or the floor), whatever the cohorts' sizes, phase 2 refuses a view that gives one `WITHHELD_UNDER_K` at the first member that does, in the parameters' order (a model's members as it declares them, a list's items in turn), whatever the order of the forms (`/views/<i>/params/columns/<j>/count`; `registry.withheld_form`, `views._withheld_form`), before anything else it checks of the view's variables, so that no `MISSING_MEMBER` at `…/bins` comes first and no edges are recorded against a later view's, naming the analysis, the setting that binds and why, never a value, its alternatives the forms of the variable that run under *k* in its place (`distribution.forms_under_k`, which for memberships gives `some`, and `every` where the path allows it, with `values` alone, D382): each aggregate its column takes over its path (`resolve.aggregates_of`; with a `where`, which closes every step for the count as for an aggregate, each it takes, `resolve.aggregates_taken`), `count` (each unit's rows as its value, a count the pass protects) and a number's `max`, `min` and `mean` with `bins` where the histogram would take its edges from the data, `some` and `every` with `values`, keeping the variable's own `where` and `bins` (so `some` and `every`, which take neither, are not offered beside them, and an aggregate of categories is offered without the `bins`), and never the view without `count`, whose column is below the unit and so takes an aggregate; an aggregate a column does not take offers only those its `where` and `empty` allow. Resolution is told which releases are under a disclosure setting (`resolve`'s `under_k`, over the floor, a draft's latest published release's setting and the dataset's, as canonicalisation computes a cohort's *k*), so that no refusal there offers `count: "rows"` (D378); a test follows every alternative a refusal offers under *k*, and the member one names, to a view that runs, never meeting a refusal twice; `validate_document` lists it, `count_cohort` refuses it, no query runs, and `run_analysis` and `summarise` raise if one reaches them. Applicability is unchanged: the analysis stays `available` under *k*, since its other views run (§9.4). `test_disclosure_classes` checks under every setting that phase 2 withholds exactly there, with that code, path, setting and reason and no value, that applicability agrees, and that only a disclosed analysis withholds a form; the catalogue's tests check that no tool runs a query. Disclosing rows under *k* needs a rule of its own (a dominance rule, a row total being bounded by its largest unit's rows) and a brute force, left to a follow-up of #52 From M3.2e-2b (D383) the walk under *k* no longer excludes `each`, which is offered and runs where the path is one open down step from the unit, and `forms_under_k` keeps its memberships branch only for memberships withheld elsewhere. | D353 lets an analysis refuse what no rule protects rather than approximate; refusing the whole analysis would have withheld every distribution under *k*, while a form refused at its own member keeps the rest disclosed, the classes two, and the refusal where the view can change |
| D380 | Memberships: the surface and what each category is (M3.2e-2a-1; refines §6.5, §7.6, §9.2, D325, D335 and D377) | A variable asks for its memberships explicitly, with `each: "category"` (`schema.analyses.Variable.each`), which takes no `aggregate`, `values`, `empty`, `count` or `where` (`CONFLICTING_MEMBERS`, the model's own check; rows with conditions are `compare.existence`'s, D325) and may take `via` and `lift`. It resolves (`resolve._memberships`) on a multi-valued category, boolean or `list<category>` column to a variable of kind `memberships` whose `question` is its **template**: the chain `some` gives over its path, `some` with `min_count` 1 at every down step, the variable's `lift` on each intermediate question, no `exclude_self` and no `where`, whose value leaf asks about no value (`values: []`, `match: "any"` for a list); `ValueList` requires a value, so the leaf is built there and `_value`'s checks are repeated: `UNDECLARED_DATATYPE` and `ROW_IDS_NOT_ALLOWED` at `…/column` (it reads the column), the fields read (those `some` of one of its values reads, a test says), what `W_C` may mention and the caps. It is `INVALID_VALUE` at `…/each` in an analysis that reads one value per unit, one that assumes independent groups or a pack's (D335, D341, `ViewVariable.independent`, whose message says so), on a column that is not categorical and on one with one value per unit, each offering only what runs in its place, as the analysis's own phase 2 checks the column in each form offered (`ViewVariable.reads`, the check phase 2 itself makes: `distribution.summarises` for `summary.distribution` and `compare.columns`, the latter reading neither a count of rows nor memberships from M3.2e-2a-2, `views._compares`, D382; `views._cox_unread` for `survival.cox`, a column it codes, no identifier's values (`count` reads none), not its endpoint's time or status, and rows whose conditions test neither (D370), the view's endpoints resolved before its variables; `views._pack_unread` for a pack's, no identifier's values and no conditions that test one (D342), values of `packs.TAKEN` when read as they are, and its role's datatype and `"on": "unit"`): where the column holds one value per unit, leaving `each` out where the analysis reads it as it is, else nothing, the message saying that no form runs; else the aggregates that run (`resolve._offered`): those the column's path allows (`resolve.aggregates_over`: those it takes, `resolve.aggregates_taken`, no `count` of a list's items, `max` and `min` of an ordered category, less, below a step whose coverage has scope columns, those that pool rows, `count`, `max`, `min` and `mean`, whose `where` must restrict them, `OPEN_SCOPE`, and `every` of a scope column of the last step's child row, `SCOPE_COLUMN_MENTION`, a column its rows look up being none whatever its name) that the analysis reads. D377's refusals of the bare column and of an aggregate the column does not take offer the same, the latter beside a `where` only the aggregates that pool rows and run with it (`resolve._pooled`, `_pooling` asked with its refusals set aside, their rows' conditions checked as above) and beside an `empty` only those of which it is a value, and `count: "rows"` only where no step has scope columns. Until M3.2e-2a-2, `each` is `NOT_SUPPORTED` at `…/each` in `summary.distribution`, naming M3.2e-2a-2 (`resolve.MEMBERSHIPS`), once resolved (phase 2, `views._distributed`), offering the aggregates its path allows, and a view so refused records no histogram's edges that a later view's must match (§8.4: it reads none); tests follow each alternative offered, for a column of every datatype on the unit and below it and for columns below coverage scoped by value, in `summary.distribution`, `compare.columns`, `survival.cox` and a pack's analysis, to a view that runs, and pin that nothing is offered where no form runs. The bare multi-valued categorical column keeps D377's refusal. (M3.2e-2a-2 lifts both refusals and removes `MEMBERSHIPS` and `PER_CATEGORY`: `summary.distribution` reads memberships, and the bare column is `AGGREGATE_REQUIRED`, offering `each` where the analysis reads it, D382.) **Each category** *c* is Q_c, the template with `values: [c]` (`canonical.category_clause`), the existence question *some row (or item) has c*, by §6.5 in full: the numerator its TRUE units, the denominator its known units, its UNKNOWN units excluded by reason, NOT_APPLICABLE FALSE (§6.4); §6.4's refusal of constants outside the permissible values guards what a document writes, and a category is read from the data, so an undeclared value of a scope-by-value column is TRUE where a row holds it, FALSE where the coverage lists it for the parent or covers every value, and otherwise `NOT_COVERED` but for evidence; a value a coverage table lists is a category only where it is stored as the column's categories are (a boolean for a boolean column, else a string; `key_part`), since no row or item of the column can hold another, and a document could not write it (§6.4). A unit's **default** is Q_c's answer for a category that none of the rows it reaches holds and no coverage lists for it, the same for every such category (D381). **The listing** over a cohort: the declared values in their order, zeros included (`false` and `true` for a boolean), then the other values in UTF-16 order where some member's answer for one is not its default (its value, reasons or flags differ), as a row that holds it or a coverage that lists it makes it, a value held only under an UNKNOWN record filter included, its units UNKNOWN; where the last step's coverage filters the column on the child row (§6.5 step 1), the filter's allowed values alone, the declared ones in their order and then the others in UTF-16 order, zeros included (`W_C` may ask only about those, and the table records no row of another; its declared but disallowed values are not listed, and no caveat says so, since the readback states the filter); at most `MAX_CATEGORIES`, else the listing is `over` and nothing is counted, and neither engine gives its units or a joint count beside other variables (`membership_units` gives `None`, SQL's `CompiledMaterialised.read` no `Joint`, whose units would be over the categories its cap kept), which M3.2e-2a-2 refuses (`LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, D382). **Units** (`memberships.membership_units`): a unit's answers over the listed categories, or, with none listed, its default alone; it is known where one of them is known and otherwise excluded under the reasons of all of them, its flags theirs; so a unit is excluded with its real reasons, never reclassified: with nothing listed, a unit closed with no rows is known and shows no category, and one whose default is UNKNOWN is excluded under its reasons; `NO_ROWS` is never given. `variables.Materialised` of memberships holds no `values`, `memberships` (per listed category its units TRUE, FALSE and UNKNOWN by reason and its flags, `Membership`, and the units known) and, in `excluded_units`, `excluded` and `marks`, the units' as above. **Form**: `{"each": "category", "question": <the template's canonical clause tree>}`, which no other variable form holds; its `"values": []` is a member of a variable's form and never of a clause (§7.6), so the form names no category, and its written position maps to no leaf key, since a variable's leaves are those of its `where`, which memberships do not take (tested); a category's key is the leaf key of the template with `"values": [c]`, and for a declared value it equals `compare.existence`'s predicate `{kind: value, column, via, values: [c], quantifier: some, lift}` however its path is written (direct, with `via`, as a two-step `exists` or nested `exists` leaves, tested); the key of a short undeclared value can be found by trying values, which M3.2e-2b's rule must weigh. The template holds no constant, so erasure reads none (D290). **Version**: no bump. `summary.distribution` stays 1.0.0 and the semantics version is unchanged: no view that ran before changes (every golden stands, and 2a-1 adds none), and `each` was an unknown member of a variable, so no id was issued for it and no cached row gives another; its values model is unchanged Under a disclosure setting the listing is the declared values alone, whatever the members hold (D384). | #52's per-category denominators need a way to ask for them that no view could write before (a bare column was refused), and an existence question per category makes each row mean what `compare.existence` means by it, with the same keys; reading undeclared values from the data, and defining the listing by the default, keeps the counts of every unit that holds such a value, which a TRUE-only listing would lose where its answer is UNKNOWN |
| D381 | Memberships in the engines: the reference evaluator, and SQL as pairs plus default (M3.2e-2a-1; refines §9.3, §13.3, D291, D294 and D327) | **Evaluator** (`engine/memberships.py`): Q_c is asked with `Evaluator.truth` for every category that can be listed and whose answer could differ from a unit's default (the declared values, and but for a filtered column every PRESENT value or item of the column and, for a scope column of the last step, every value its coverage tables list in it that is stored as the column's categories are, `_of_kind`, as SQL's Z(p) reads them, `scope_values`; tested), and the default as Q_c of a string that no cell, item or coverage table of the release holds (`_fresh`, longer than every one held; tested), never from the template, whose value leaf admits no value and so would be closed vacuously (§6.5 step 4); where only a filtered column's allowed values can be listed, a category no one asks about may have another answer than the default (a value the filter disallows but the scope coverage lists is FALSE where the default is `NOT_COVERED`), and it is never listed, so no count reads it (`Answers`); `materialise`, `listing` and `membership_units` count them. **SQL** (`sql._Compiler.memberships`): the template's steps give, from the value leaf up, two relations per row: its default and its pairs, each category whose answer is not its default with that answer. **Why it holds**, step by step through §6.4–§6.7, under the template's constraints (`some` at every step with `min_count` 1, no `exclude_self`, no condition but the next step, `match: "any"`): for each row x there is a set C(x) with A(x, c) = D(x) for every c ∉ C(x), and D(x) is never TRUE. *Leaf*: a lookup after the last step that reaches no row gives `NO_PARENT` for every c, C = ∅; a cell not PRESENT its base result, C = ∅; a PRESENT value w gives D = FALSE and C = {w}, TRUE there; a PRESENT list gives D = `any` of its items with each PRESENT one FALSE, C its PRESENT items, each once. *Step 1*, at every step, as §6.5 reads each step's record filter on its own child rows (the last step's is not the only one, and a relationship the path takes twice reads its filter at both; tested): the record filter reads only x's cells, A′(x, c) = A(x, c) ∧ F(x) for every c, so D′ = D ∧ F, never TRUE, and C′(x) ⊆ C(x). *Step 2*: the parent scope reads the parent alone. *Step 3*: dropping reads each child's own answer. *Step 4*: closedness depends on c only at the last step and only when the column, with no lookup after it, is a scope column of its coverage: closed(p, c) = every(p) ∨ some listed whole tuple of p has c (a tuple with an unknown cell is not whole and closes nothing; tested), SCOPE_PARTIAL where it has other scope columns and every(p) does not hold (a group covering every value that lists a tuple too flags none; tested); a group covering every value belongs to the default, whose closedness is every(p) alone, else `NOT_COVERED`; everywhere else it is c's for every c. *Steps 5–7*: A(p, c) = Φ(scope, closed(p, c), the multiset of A(x, c) over p's children), so C(p) = ⋃ C(x) ∪ Z(p) (Z(p) the values the coverage lists for p in whole tuples, where the column is a scope column and they are stored as its categories are) and D(p) = Φ(…, the default's closedness, {D(x)}), never TRUE since no D(x) is. **Counting rule**: Φ reads t, f, u, the children kept and, per reason and per flag, whether some kept (or dropped) child has it; each is a count, a tally (`_Compiler.tallies`, as D291's `sibling_stats`), and Σ_x τ(A(x, c)) = Σ_x τ(D(x)) + Σ_{x: c ∈ C(x)} [τ(A(x, c)) − τ(D(x))]. So per step the defaults' tallies are counted per parent, and per parent and category the pairs' tallies less their defaults'; an OR holds a bit where its tally is above 0, and no OR is ever subtracted; §6.5's answers are taken of the default's tallies, and of the default's plus each category's; a pair is kept only where its answer (code, reasons and flag words) is not its parent's default's, and its flags alone can differ (tested): under `assessed`, a child FALSE for c with `SCOPE_PARTIAL` (its group lists c in one zone of two scope columns) whose default is `NOT_COVERED` is dropped from the default's children, so beside a child whose group covers every value the parent is FALSE both ways, with `SCOPE_PARTIAL` for c alone. A record filter's answer carries no flag (it reads the child row's own cells, and flags come from coverage, §6.5 step 7), so a filtered pair's flags are its own answer's. **At the cohort**: the categories are listed first, the declared ones by place and the classes of the members' pairs after them, `MAX_CATEGORIES` + 1 at most, in no order, before any pair is counted; the listing does not depend on DuckDB's order all the same, since where at most `MAX_CATEGORIES` are listed every one is kept, and past it the listing is `over` whichever are kept and nothing is counted (a test runs it on 1, 2 and 4 threads over tables of many row groups); the members' defaults are counted by (code, reasons, flag words), and each listed category's pairs and their displaced defaults likewise, and the server takes a category's units as the defaults' less its displaced ones and plus its pairs, orders the undeclared ones in UTF-16 order (DuckDB compares UTF-8 bytes) and checks that no count falls below 0 and every category counts the members; a unit's own pairs are ORed (`membership_units`: known where a listed pair is, or where it has fewer listed pairs than categories are listed, or none is listed, and its default is known). Every count is a `COUNT` and every aggregate a `COUNT`, a `BIT_OR` or `BOOL_OR` of one row's own pairs or items or of a combinator's operands, a `BIT_OR` of the flag words of the units one row of the result counts (`membership_rows`: the members known, or excluded by one set of reasons, whose flags are the union of theirs, as `memberships.materialise` takes it), or the `MIN` or `MAX` of integer codes and row numbers; nothing is summed (§9.3, tested). Memberships are never listed per member (`CompileError`); beside other variables their units enter the joint counts. **Tests**: a Hypothesis property holds the compiler to the evaluator on `Materialised` and the joint counts over random cities (an undeclared code a checklist lists, record filters on the column, on another column of its rows and on an earlier step, the inspections' before the violations' and readings', and on a relationship the path takes twice, lists with unknown and repeated items, NOT_APPLICABLE rows, booleans over undeclared coverage, a direct scope two steps down, both lifts, the parent scope, grouped coverage covering all or some, a lookup after the last step, a scope-partial column, and cohorts), with one flag to a word; a second holds each listed category's counts, UNKNOWN by every reason, to `compile_crossing` of its Q_c; a third, over another release (two scope columns listed together, a tuple listed with an unknown cell, grouped coverage with a group covering every value, alone or listing a tuple too, record filters on a scope column, on the first step and on the finds reached at two steps, lists with non-BMP items, values past U+FFFF and the empty string), holds the evaluator's answer for every category of a universe, held or not, but those a filtered column cannot list, to `Evaluator.truth` of Q_c, and the compiler, and a crossing of the listed categories and two unlisted ones, to the evaluator; scenarios for each case above. **Cost**, measured twice on D372's orchard with 4 cores through `compile_materialised` over all million trees, the least `query_memory` among 2, 3, 4, 6 and 8 GiB and the wall time there, the two runs' range: (a) `harvests.grade` (1,558,441 rows, three declared classes) 2 GiB, 5.0–5.1 s on one thread and 1.8–1.9 s on four; (b) `weighings.scale` (a category in the orchard's own descriptors) two steps down through the weighings' partial, scoped coverage, 2 GiB, 5.2–5.3 s and 2.0–2.1 s under `strict`, 5.6–5.8 s and 2.1–2.2 s under `assessed`; (c) 150 undeclared classes over a benchmark-only table of 1,500,000 rows below the unit, 2 GiB, 5.2 s and 1.9–2.2 s; (d) the baseline, `compile_crossing` of (a)'s three per-category questions, 2 GiB, 3.0–3.1 s and 1.1–1.2 s. All run under the defaults (one thread, 2 GiB, 25 s), where D373's crossing of 16 predicates is refused for memory: pairs plus default costs 1.6–1.7 times one predicate per category at three categories on one thread and 1.5–1.7 times on four, and does not grow with the categories as a crossing does | One relation per category would cost a crossing's, which at D373's scale needs 4 GiB and 38 s for 16 predicates and could not reach 150; a unit's answer for a category none of its rows holds is its default, so the work grows with the pairs its rows hold, not with the categories times the units. Tallies keep every step's arithmetic counts, which can be added and subtracted exactly, where ORs cannot be undone |

The reference evaluator's are `memberships.materialise_over` (each variable as `memberships.evaluated` gives it, memberships' units by `membership_units`, no joint where a listing is `over`), which both test harnesses (`analyses/conftest.materialise_by_evaluator`, `catalog/test_analyses.evaluated`) and the engine's tests use; it lives in `engine/memberships.py`, which reads both kinds of variable. **Caveats**: `UNKNOWN_EXCLUDED` naming the values where a unit is excluded, as for any variable, and in a message of its own affecting `/values` wherever some category has UNKNOWN units ("Units for which a category of a column of memberships could not be decided are left out of that category's denominator; its proportion's excluded counts them by reason"); NOT_APPLICABLE is FALSE there, never an exclusion (§6.4); the answers' flags raise `SCOPE_PARTIAL` and `COVERAGE_PROPOSED`; a view with a count of rows and memberships carries both messages (the digest reduces caveats of one code to one entry, by design); every other text is unchanged. **Readback and chart**: a column reads "for each category of the X, whether ⟨its template⟩", the template's value leaf "is that category", which only a template reaches (`readback._Reader.template`, asserted, so no cohort's readback does), and the view adds a sentence where a column is memberships, that a unit whose answer cannot be decided is left out of that category and counted by reason and that a unit may count in several, so the proportions need not sum to 1; the chart is one per column, a bar per category and cohort, not stacked, each label quoted as JSON, its description saying that a unit may have several (`charts._column_chart` names memberships with the other categories, never the histogram); other descriptions are unchanged.
