an analysis that reads one value per unit (compare.columns, survival.cox and every pack's) is offered neither and keeps `each`'s `INVALID_VALUE` (D380, tested); below coverage scoped by value the bare column is offered its memberships, which run there, a scope column's and a looked-up column named as one included (tested), and the tests of D380 that pinned `each`'s `NOT_SUPPORTED` in `summary.distribution` follow its alternatives to views that run instead. Refusals issue no id, so changing their codes changes none. **Withholding** (§8.4): `summary.distribution` withholds `columns/*/each` (`CoreAnalysis.withheld_forms`, still a path and why): a column's memberships split the cohort's units once for each of its categories, predicates that depend on one another (a unit may hold several), and no rule yet discloses their splits together. Under any effective *k*, whatever the cohorts' sizes, a view that gives memberships is `WITHHELD_UNDER_K` at `…/each` (the first withheld member in the parameters' order, `registry.withheld_form`), before anything else phase 2 checks of its variables, naming the setting that binds and why, never a value; its alternatives (`distribution.forms_under_k`, which branches on memberships) are `aggregate: "some"` with `values`, and `aggregate: "every"` with them where the path allows it (`resolve.aggregates_of`: the aggregates `resolve.aggregates_over` allows, read from the variable as resolved, so that they are those resolution offers without *k*: below coverage scoped by value, direct or grouped, not `every` of a scope column of the last step's child row, `SCOPE_COLUMN_MENTION`, round 1 of #72's review; `some` always runs), each an existence question of the categories it names, whose split the pass protects one predicate at a time (D320); `count_cohort` and `compare.existence` are not offered, being no form of the variable (the walk test follows only those) and `compare.existence` assuming independent groups; `validate_document` lists the refusal, `count_cohort` refuses, no query runs, and `summarise` and `run_analysis` raise if one reaches them (tested); D379's walk under *k* starts from every kind of the shop's columns, a bare one beside `bins` included, and from the visits' columns below coverage scoped by value, direct and grouped (`scoped(grouped=True)`), a scope column, another and one looked up. §8.4's Limits add that a column's categories asked one at a time are predicates that depend on one another, which D320's brute force (independent predicates) did not cover: M3.2e-2b's rule comes with a brute force over existence of a column's categories. Applicability is unchanged (§9.4). `ENTRY.definition` names memberships, `multi_membership` and their withholding. **Goldens**: `shop(reasons=True)` changes the shop's rows alone (the returns' reasons in turn, two undeclared, U+1F4E6 and U+FF61, whose UTF-16 order is not their code points', and a second return, of the next reason, on every third order that has one) and is off by default, so every golden stands; `returns_by_reason_distribution` (gold and silver, `returns.reason` each under `strict` and `assessed`) and `order_channel_memberships` (every customer and gold: `customers.tier`, the channel of the orders returned, read through a lookup after the last down step, and `returns.reason`, each under `strict`, whose joint leaves units unknown to every variable, under `NOT_ASSESSED` and `NOT_COVERED`) are added; `test_digested` reads both back, and a hit of a membership result is given from the cache. **Million test**: memberships have a view of their own over the one cohort of every tree (D372): `harvests.grade`, `weighings.scale` under `assessed` and `trees.tags`, each (§9.3). Measured on D372's orchard with 4 cores, other work running beside it, through `run_analysis` over all million trees, the least `query_memory` among 2, 3, 4, 6 and 8 GiB and the wall time there: the three together need 6 GiB on one thread and on four (4 GiB is refused both ways), 24.8 s on one thread and 8.8–12.0 s on four; `harvests.grade` and `trees.tags` alone need 3 GiB, 14.1 s and 6.3 s. So under the defaults (one thread, 2 GiB, 25 s) such a view at a million units is refused for memory (`LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, `query_memory`, after 8–10 s), as D373's crossing of 16 predicates is, and the determinism tests' 8 GiB and 300 s hold it; one memberships alone fits the defaults (D381). The job's budget is about 8 minutes in CI, where it took about 5: the view adds its two runs, about 35 s, and on the machine it was measured on, shared with other work, the whole job took 7 min 43 s with it (10 min 32 s without it, under heavier load), so all three are kept; the three together, not one alone, are what an operator's `query_memory` must allow. **Version**: no bump. `summary.distribution` stays 1.0.0 and the semantics version 1: no view that ran before gives another output (every golden stands), and a view of memberships, or of a bare multi-valued category, was refused before, so none was issued an id and no cached row gives another; the entry's definition and `returns` change at 1.0.0, and its values model only gains a member of a union, so content cached before reads back (D374) M3.2e-2b (D383) lifts the withholding: under *k* memberships list their declared categories alone (D384), each row disclosed as a question's split with no map, `analysed` suppressed, and are withheld wherever the path is not one open down step from the unit; `withheld_forms` loses `columns/*/each`, the alternatives offered in its place stand only there, and D383's Limits replace the sentence on a column's categories asked one at a time. | #52's per-category denominators in the descriptive analysis: each category's row is its existence question's proportion, keyed as `compare.existence` keys it, so the two read alike; memberships of one column are predicates that depend on one another, which no brute force has covered, so they are withheld under *k* as counts of rows are, their alternatives the single-category questions the pass already protects, until M3.2e-2b's rule; the bare column was refused only until memberships could be asked for, and asking for them explicitly keeps one value per unit the default everywhere |
| D383 | Memberships under a disclosure setting (M3.2e-2b; refines §8.1, §8.4, §9.2, §9.5, D320, D329, D353, D377, D379, D380 and D382) | **Rule** (§8.4, `disclosure.membership_shown`): under *k*, at a position whose cohort's `n_true` is shown, each listed category's split into its units known (TRUE or FALSE, the denominator) and UNKNOWN is shown whole where neither part is from 1 to *k* − 1 (`split_hidden`), else neither; its TRUE units (the numerator) with it where neither its TRUE nor its FALSE units are from 1 to *k* − 1; its estimate with both (`no_units` for a shown 0 denominator), else `suppressed`; its `excluded` always `null` (`suppressed`); where `n_true` is suppressed every row's counts are `null`, the rows still listed; rows are never merged; the variable's `analysed` (`n`, `excluded_units`, `excluded`, and its `variables` entry beside other variables) is `null` (`summarise` gives it `Shown(False, False)`). Only the declared categories are listed (D384; `summarise` asserts it). **Disclosed only where the path is proven open per unit; withheld everywhere else** (`resolve.bounded_rows`, `memberships.bounded_rows`, `distribution.withheld_under_k`): a column that is not a list (whose items are any number) runs under *k* only where its path is one down step from the unit table, not a relationship of the unit table to itself (its child rows would be the units, so the view's own population bounds the TRUE counts' sum within one call, S1 of round 2 after the redesign), and that step is open (`resolve.open_path`, `resolve.open_step`), the step's parent being the unit, so that a proof per parent row is one per unit; every longer path is withheld, whatever its steps (a later child's unique set may lie within the columns each unit fixes along the path, such as its key carried down a composite foreign key, and a per-unit proof over several steps is #75's). A step is open only where every set of its child table's columns that the gate keeps unique (`resolve.unique_columns`: the `primary_key`, `KEY_NOT_UNIQUE`; the `parent_columns` of each relationship whose parent is the table, unique whether or not they are its key, `KEY_NOT_UNIQUE`; the `child_columns` of each `one-to-one` relationship from it, `CARDINALITY_VIOLATED`) holds a free column (`resolve.free_column`): described, stored rather than derived, of a datatype the typer takes any value of (`number`, `integer`, `string`, `category`, whose undeclared values the gate allows, `date`, `datetime`, `time_offset`), no child column of a relationship from the table (`DANGLING_REFERENCE`), in no record filter of a coverage of one (`OUTSIDE_RECORD_FILTER`), and read, directly or through other derivations, by no derived column the gate checks (one in a unique set, a foreign key or a record filter: a value missing from a value map is UNKNOWN, and the gate checks the derived values there; a derivation checked nowhere bounds nothing, round 3's m3); a child table of which the gate keeps no set unique is open. The rule reads the gate's checks from one list (`resolve.GATE_CHECKS`: each refusal code by the function of `store.gate` that raises it, classified as keeping a set unique, restricting a column's values, or bounding neither, as `KEY_NULL`, `COVERAGE_NULL`, `NOT_APPLICABLE_IN_FILTER` and an import's drops do); `COVERAGE_UNKNOWN` restricts only a table a coverage names, which no relationship joins (§5.6), so never a step's child. Every other step is taken as bounded, whatever bound its columns give (a foreign key's is its parent table's rows, which is data): each unit then may reach at most a number of rows a reader can know, and the categories' TRUE counts sum to at most that many times the units, which pins one the rule hides (10 units under *k* = 3, one row each: a, b and c held by 5, 4 and 1 show 10 known each, TRUE 5, 4 and hidden, and 0 would be shown, so it is 1; the brute force finds it from 6 units, three categories each split 1, 2 and 3; two rows each, where a boolean completes the key, four categories held by 10, 5, 1 and 4: 20 − 19 gives the hidden 1), and numerators alone (D297's complement rule, the rule considered as a fallback, R2) pin it too (8 units, *k* = 3). Round 1 of #74's review found the first version of this check, which withheld only one-to-one paths and children keyed within their child columns, missing a key completed by a record-filtered column or a boolean (both reproduced through `run_analysis`). Round 2 found its fix, which read only the child's key, the step's own relationship and the step's own coverage, missing bounds the gate enforces through other descriptors: another coverage's record filter on the child (P1), a keyless child whose columns are another relationship's `parent_columns` (P2), a second `one-to-one` relationship over the child columns (P3) and a key column a value map derives onto one value (P5), each pinning a hidden 1 through `run_analysis`. So the test is inverted: a step is open only where proven so from the gate's own checks, and everything else is withheld, conservatively (a bound at or above the declared categories would not pin), and the rule's claim is stated for paths with an open step, where a unit may hold any set of the declared categories. Round 3 found that claim false: a path whose first step is open (visits keyed by the tree and an integer) and whose last child is keyed by the tree alone, under a composite foreign key `(tree_id, visit)`, has one row per unit (Q1; two with a boolean in the key, four categories, Q2), each pinning a hidden 1 through `run_analysis`. As the third instance of one class ("a descriptor bounds a unit's rows"), it ended review at #2's cap, and the claim was narrowed to what is proven per unit, a path of one open down step from the unit; review restarts at round 1. The alternatives the refusal offers, asked together in one view, bound one another the same way (variables of the same rows, which the Limits disclaim), so the withholding keeps D383's own claim true and no more. Phase 2 refuses such memberships under any *k* with `WITHHELD_UNDER_K` at `…/each` (`views._withheld_form`, the first withheld member in the columns' order, whether withheld so or by `withheld_forms`), naming the setting and why, offering `some`, and `every` where the path allows it, with `values` (`distribution.forms_under_k`); `resolve._offered` offers `each` under *k* only where the path is one open down step from the unit; `summarise` and `run_analysis` raise if such a view reaches them. `withheld_forms` is `{"columns/*/count"}`. **Caveats**: under *k* the memberships' `UNKNOWN_EXCLUDED` message is carried whenever a view has memberships, whether or not a unit was excluded, saying that no count by reason is shown (round 1 of #74's review), and `SUPPRESSED` adds a sentence on memberships only where a view has them, so no other view's message changes. **Brute force** (`tests/core/analyses/test_membership_disclosure.py`, 55 tests, about 50 s in all on 4 cores): palettes built by `engine.build` whose units realise every multiset of up to three rows (two for a boolean) over each configuration's alphabet, covered or not, their answers read by the reference evaluator as types (per declared category the truth value and reasons, and a flag), with a closure test that one more row realises no new type; configurations `plain` (a child's category under direct coverage, rows of each declared value, an undeclared one, an empty cell and one not assessed), `three` (three declared), `filtered` (a record filter on another column of the rows, its cell known, not assessed or empty), `scoped` (coverage scoped by the column, a checklist of each subset), `parent` (a parent scope in, out or unknown), `boolean`, `list` (a list on the unit, its cell empty, not assessed or not applicable), and the bounded `one_to_one`, `keyed` and `filtered_key` (each unit one row at most: one-to-one, keyed by its parent's key, or keyed by it and a column the record filter fixes) and `boolean_key` (two rows at most, a boolean completing the key, four categories). **A**: every world over the types, deduplicated as its per-category TRUE, FALSE and UNKNOWN counts, the units UNKNOWN for every category and the flag, under the rule: no shown count is from 1 to *k* − 1, and among the worlds that read the same no secret (per category T, F, U, T + F, F + U, T + U, and the units unknown for every category) takes one value from 1 to *k* − 1 that the shown counts and the size do not give linearly, at (8, 3), (10, 3), (11, 4) and (12, 5) for `plain`, `filtered`, `scoped`, `parent`, `boolean` and `list` and (7, 3) and (8, 4) for `three`; **A2**: the same with each category's UNKNOWN units under each reason among the secrets, for `plain` at (6, 3) and `filtered` at (5, 3); **A3**: in the plain model, where holdings are independent given the units' defaults, every intersection of the defaults each output allows, over any number of categories (up to `MAX_CATEGORIES`), for 4 to 20 units and *k* = 3, 4, 5; **B**: `summarise` whole over every world of the plain palette's units of a, b and an undeclared z, covered or not, materialised by `materialise_over(…, declared=True)`, at (6, 3) and (8, 4): the output is a function of A's projection, names no undeclared value or key, shows no count from 1 to *k* − 1, and `analysed` is `null`; **C**: beside a category of the unit, over every world of five units, each variable's column and `variables` entry depend on its own worlds alone; **D**: examples (a), (b) and (c), and that the rule pins in the bounded configurations (from 6 units for one row each; by the example for two) while phase 2 withholds there, citing D383, and wherever a key column beyond the foreign key is a boolean, record-filtered or a foreign key, but not where it is an integer or an unfiltered category; the guards of phase 2's order, of `summarise` and of `run_analysis` are tested. **The rule held to the gate** (`tests/core/engine/test_open_steps.py`): every refusal code the gate raises, by the function that raises it, read from its source, is classified in `GATE_CHECKS`, and every site that names a code or refuses is counted (`SITES`, round 3's m1), so a check added to the gate, in a new function or beside another, fails the test until the rule reads it; and over 400 generated shapes of a child table (its key a random set of its tree, its grade, two columns of five datatypes and a value map of one; a record filter on the step's coverage or on another relationship's; a second relationship from it, many-to-one or one-to-one; a relationship into it; the step one-to-one or not), wherever `open_step` says open, the store's import accepts one parent holding a row of each declared category and one more, differing in their free columns; the test requires at least 40 of each answer, and each clause of the rule dropped in turn fails it. Over 400 generated chains of one to three down steps from the unit (each table carrying its parent's key as its foreign key, its key drawn over inherited and own columns of four datatypes, the last one's possibly undeclared, record filters and one-to-one steps drawn too), wherever `bounded_rows` calls the path open, the store's import accepts one unit reaching a row of each declared category and one more at the last table, row *i* of each table under row *i* of its parent; round 2's rule (any open step) fails it, and so does one reading only the first or the last step. A derivation bounds its input only where the gate checks it (tested both ways). `catalog/test_analyses.py` holds P1, P2, P3 and P5 (withheld under a floor, `each` not offered for the bare column, the hidden count 1 without one), a keyless child that nothing bounds (disclosed), round 3's Q1 and Q2 (withheld, no `each` offered, c 1 without a floor), and paths of two down steps over a composite foreign key, bounded or open at each step (all four withheld). No pin was found outside the bounded configurations. *k* = 2 pins, as for D329. **Limits** (§8.4): variables and cohorts whose paths share a relationship or a coverage table bound one another (examples (a), (b) and (c), each reproduced through `run_analysis` at #72's head in the plan's review, in `compare.existence` and `summary.distribution` today, and filed as #73); within one variable, counts crossing two categories follow by difference; units that read the same rows are not independent; a column unique across its table, alone or beside columns the descriptors bound (record-filtered, boolean or derived ones), in any set the gate keeps unique (a key, a relationship's parent columns or a one-to-one relationship's child columns), gives each category to one unit at most, or to as many as those columns' values allow (S2 of round 2 after the redesign: a key `[grade, season]` with `season` filtered to one value), so a hidden TRUE count beside a split of all but one FALSE is 1, as `some` of it pins today (round 2 of #74's review, P4, added to #73); a pack's validator (`Validator.validate_source`), which sees an import's data, may refuse rows by a rule no descriptor states, bounding rows outside the claim, which covers the bounds the gate enforces (round 3's m2); and a count of the rows shown in any call (a table's `n_rows`, a `count` of them, `some` of each category) bounds the TRUE counts' sum where the rows barely exceed the units, pinning a hidden count on a disclosed path as `some` and `count_cohort` pin it today (round 1 after the redesign, m1, N1, added to #73). **Version**: no bump; `summary.distribution` stays 1.0.0 and the semantics version 1: every view that ran before gives the same output (without *k* memberships list as before, and under *k* no view without memberships sees a changed message), views of `each` under *k* were refused and so issued no id and filled no cache row, and the values model is unchanged (its docstring alone), so content cached before reads back (D374); `ENTRY.definition` changes at 1.0.0, as D378 and D382 did; goldens `order_channel_by_tier_under_k` and `order_channel_memberships_under_k` are added and none changes | #52's per-category denominators under a disclosure setting: a membership row is the question of one category, so D329's rule for a question variable, applied to each on its own, discloses it as `some` of that category already is under *k*, and shows no more than the alternatives D382 offered in its place, asked together in one view, which also showed maps and per-variable `analysed`; D320's split cannot be used, since a numerator shown beside a hidden split tells that the UNKNOWN units are few and a category no unit holds bounds them (example (a)); maps of categories that share defaults pin one another (example (b)); where the descriptors bound the rows each unit reaches, the TRUE counts sum to at most that many times the units and the rule, like the numerators-only rule, pins, so there the refusal stays; a blanket refusal would protect nothing the alternatives do not give, and the leak between dependent variables and cohorts reproduces in signed-off analyses, whose fix changes their outputs and so needs version bumps of its own (#73) |
| D384 | Memberships list their declared categories alone under a disclosure setting (M3.2e-2b; refines D380 and D381) | Under *k* both engines list only `memberships.fixed` (the declared values in their order, `false` and `true` for a boolean, a filtered column's allowed values), whatever the members hold: the reference evaluator's `memberships.listing(…, declared=True)`, threaded through `membership_units`, `materialise` and `materialise_over`, and SQL's `compile_materialised(…, declared=True)` (`_Compiler.declared`), whose `membership_listing` takes the branch a filtered column's already takes, an empty listing where the column declares none (its units counted by their default, as with nothing listed), both memo keys holding `declared`. `run_analysis` passes `declared` per materialisation (`run_views(…, declared=…)`), and `_materialisation_key` holds whether the view is under *k*; both test harnesses pass the view's setting. The differential test draws `declared`, and a scenario holds that 151 undeclared values are no `over` and that a declared column lists its declared values beside an undeclared one every unit holds. `LIMIT_EXCEEDED` under *k* is decided at run time, as without it, over the declared values alone | Whether a listing is `over`, a label `LongCategory` or `NonTextCategory`, and which pairs are read must not depend on undeclared data under *k*: filtering in `summarise` alone would leave `LIMIT_EXCEEDED` and `NonTextCategory` telling whether undeclared values are held; the engine change is reviewed on its own, apart from the rule |
| D397 | Server text holds only the server's words (M4.0f-A1, provisional; amends D126, D225, D229, D231 and D309; cites D277 and D296) | A `text` segment, a message's or a note's server sentence and a refusal's alternatives hold only the server's own literals and module constants, numbers, and enumeration and `Literal` values; everything else is a `data` segment, whatever its origin: a source's names and the ids derived from them (`infer`'s notes, `store/derive.py`'s problems, `store/tables.py`'s and `store/build.py`'s refusals), Arrow and DuckDB type strings, struct, list and map field names, union member names and a Parquet time zone (`schema.output.shown`, which is `data(escaped(name))` cut only between escapes; `escaped` moves to `schema.output`), units an agent stages through `propose_descriptor` and a curator accepts, a curated encoding, a library's message or class name (`csv.Error`, a decode error's reason, what pyarrow or calamine raised), the CORS origins and connection names a refusal offers. Each value or id is a token of its own, as D296 has it for readbacks (a composite key too); the template's words stay in `text` segments. **Carriers hold segments**: a message on its way to a refusal or a note travels as a `schema.output.Message` (a tuple of segments), never as one string to be wrapped later: `derive.Problem`, `tables.Problem` and `TableError`, `gate`'s failures and drops, `SourceError`, `UrlError`, `StoreRefused` (a part given as a string is the server's words), the Parquet errors, a snapshot's `Skipped.reason` (whose segments cross the reader's pipe, D225, and are validated again in the parent: a reason that is not a few valid segments, like a sheet's `skipped` that is not one of the two sentences the reader writes, is a fault of the reader (`ReaderError`, D225), not a refusal, and nothing of it is stored) and `infer._note`'s message; an exception's own `str` is `plain_text` of its message, for logs and tests, never server text. The import report's format is **`aibi.import-report/2`**; `store/build.read_report`, the one reader of reports, which both the operator's queue and the public `curation_queue` read through, gives every note of a **`/1`** report, of every kind, its kind's fixed text (`schema.pack_api.NOTE_TEXT`, moved from the catalogue beside `NoteKind`, the words D277 gives under *k*) instead of its stored message, keeping its subject (a `QueueText`, data), count and rows; a `/1` note of a kind it does not know is refused as an unknown format is (`ValueError`), and a `/1` blob is never rewritten, by a curation that carries it (D231), erasure or redaction. Every new import's report, and so its manifest hash, differs from one written before by the format string alone; descriptors, ids, digests and the joined text of every message are unchanged, so D231's "identical inputs give identical manifests" holds within a report format. The operator CLI prints a data token as plain text, which A6 asks for; marking tokens there is outside this rule. A note of `/2` is up to seven segments where a `/1` note was one, so fewer notes fit under `MAX_QUEUE_BYTES`; `truncated` counts those left out, and no disclosure rule changes. **Known exceptions until M4.0f-A2**: the sites that `server/scripts/census_text_sites.py` lists after A1's review (`schema/loading.py`'s Pydantic `msg`, the decoder messages of `schema.jsonio.JsonError` that `schema/loading.py`, `store/edits.py` and `mcp/server.py` wrap as text), and what is not a text hole but still holds several ids in one token (the composite designators of `schema/release.py`'s roles message and `importers/databases.py`'s foreign-key subject), are the server's text or one token where D397 says otherwise, and A2 converts them. The class test that enforces the rule over all of core (construction closed at the type, constants proved by their values, holes checked at run time) is M4.0f-A2's. Construction is closed by the type (D399); the known exceptions above are M4.0f-A2b's. | A6: a model reads a `text` segment as the server speaking, so a source's name, an agent's staged value or a library's message in one is an instruction in the server's voice (an injected id reached the curator as "arith reads numeric columns: …", and a unit as "… does not convert to s"); untrusted is the complement of the server's own words, not a list of origins, so no inventory of origins has to be complete; a carrier that holds a string can be wrapped as server text anywhere it travels, and one that holds segments cannot; stored reports cannot be refreshed (re-importing an unchanged source is `NO_CHANGE`), so they are read safely instead, with the words D277 already shows |
| D398 | Names that are not Unicode text (M4.0f-B, provisional; amends D225, D226, D232, D253, D266, D268, D307, D309, D310 and D397; cites D285) | **`data()` is total**: `schema.output.data` writes each lone surrogate (U+D800–DFFF) and noncharacter (U+FDD0–FDEF and the last two code points of every plane) as its `\uXXXX` or `\UXXXXXXXX` escape and cuts only between escapes, keeping the longest prefix that fits in 200 characters, marked `truncated`; on Unicode text it is the plain token, unchanged, so no id, digest, report blob or message changes for a name that is text. `shown` is the same function, kept by its name; anything but a `str` is a `TypeError`; `escaped` is retired, and no module of the core but `schema/output.py` reaches the escape or any other private name of that module (an import, attribute or string of `escaped` or of a `_`-prefixed name `schema/output.py` defines, taken from the module and checked over the core's syntax trees), since an escaped string made server text, or cut later, would break the rule. The escapes are for display only and never parsed back: a backslash is not escaped, so the text `a\udcff` and the lone surrogate show alike. **No name raises out of an importer** (D225, D226): `describe` refuses (`UNPARSEABLE_SOURCE`, naming the value as data, the message unchanged from D309's) any string it is given that is not Unicode text, in this order: the dataset's name, the source's location, then for each table its original name and label ("A table's original name"), its columns, its comment, its columns' comments and its parse evidence, then the dataset's packs. Earlier checks refuse before anything more is read: a file's or an archive member's own name, as it is listed ("A file's name"; a name over `MAX_STRING` characters is `LIMIT_EXCEEDED` where it was); a snapshot's names and comments (D307; D309's "a name from the catalogue" is a name from anywhere, and what a name can hold depends on the kind of database, so each kind is stated: **SQLite** stores a name as the bytes its client wrote, so a table's, column's, view's or foreign key's name can hold bytes that are not UTF-8, and the catalogue is read with each such byte a lone surrogate (`surrogateescape`), so that a table's or column's name is refused as data, a view's (or a virtual or shadow table's) is noted, and a foreign key to a parent so named is dropped with a note on the child's columns, naming no parent; a catalogue entry SQLite holds as a blob or a number, or rejects as malformed, stays "cannot be read", naming the driver's class (`TypeError`, `DatabaseError` or `UnicodeDecodeError`), a stated residual with a test; **DuckDB** validates its names as UTF-8, so no byte that is not UTF-8 reaches one, and a noncharacter is refused as data; **Postgres in a database of encoding `SQL_ASCII`** stores a name or a comment as bytes that are not UTF-8, which the server cannot convert to the session's UTF-8 and so refuses before any reaches the importer, so that the whole database is `UNPARSEABLE_SOURCE` "The database cannot be read (IOException)" whichever object holds the bytes, a view and a schema the connection does not read included: a stated residual, pinned by a test behind the `databases` marker, neither refused as data nor noted; **MySQL** names and comments are held in the server's character set, and the suite has no MySQL server: identifiers are expected to be validated as UTF-8 when created, which leaves no byte slot, but that is not tested, and the outcome for a name that is not text is unstated; the table limit (`import_tables`) counts every table listed, a name that is not text among them, and is refused before any name); and, at resolve, a connection's file whose location in its import directory is not text, or whose import directory's real path is not, which names the location and never an absolute path (D310). A directory, a link, any other kind of entry, a hidden entry, a file or member of a kind not read, an archive in a directory, an empty sheet and a view whose name is not text, where the database's kind can hold one (SQLite), are noted with it as data, as they were, never refused. A Parquet column's or field's name that is not UTF-8 is `UNPARSEABLE_SOURCE` naming its bytes as data (each byte that is not UTF-8 as its escape), where it named the library's class; a cell that is not UTF-8 is unchanged (D397). A URL's database that decodes to what is not Unicode text is `INVALID_VALUE`, before a snapshot is read. **Confinement** (D232): a path that cannot be a file's name (a lone surrogate outside U+DC80–DCFF) is refused first, then a NUL, then the rest, as before; U+DC80–DCFF is a byte a file system holds, and is read as a name. **Configuration** (D253, D310), a deliberate change: the server does not start when the real path of the data directory or of an import directory, or a connection's `path` or `schema`, is not Unicode text (UTF-8 and no noncharacter: one test, `is_text`, which the import's resolve applies to the same paths), since SQLite and DuckDB open the store's files, the uploads and a connection's file by a path given as text, and a location must be text to be described; before D398 a CSV below such an import directory imported. The remedy is to rename the directory. **HTTP and the CLI** (D266, D268): an upload's `extension` that is not text is `UNSUPPORTED_FORMAT`, as any other kind not read; the CLI refuses, as a usage error naming no value and before anything else, an argument, or a value of `AIBI_SERVER`, `AIBI_OPERATOR`, `AIBI_TOKEN` or `AIBI_HANDLE` (what reaches a URL, a header or a body), that is not UTF-8; `XDG_STATE_HOME`, a local path never sent, is used as the file system gives it, as before. A noncharacter is UTF-8: it is sent, and the server answers. **Reader faults** (D225, citing D285): every handler on the import path (`importers/`, `store/parquet.py`, `store/sources.py`) that can catch a `ValidationError` re-raises it first or is allowed with its reason, so an output a reader builds from a name it did not check is a fault (`ReaderError`, a 500 that fails loudly), never "cannot be read (ValidationError)"; a reader child sends a `ValidationError` by its class name alone, since its errors' messages, contexts and locations can hold a name. Any other exception still sends its traceback, which can hold a name or a cell's fragment: the accepted residual, a fault's log, as D285 logs a pack's. Not done: names that are text, NUL and control characters included, import as labels as they did; a pack importer's names stay `checks._string`'s (D400); `ImportOptions.dataset` from a library caller must be an identifier, which the router's pattern and the CLI's check hold; text escaped before it reaches `data` (by `repr`, `ascii` or `backslashreplace`, such as a damaged typed snapshot's `repr`, cut by slicing) is outside the ban, a residual class that the tests' check of every segment shown (never cut inside an escape) watches for names | A6 and §8.1: a token that could not be valid output raised out of the importers, a 500 with a traceback or a refusal that named the server's own class, for names a file system, a workbook or a database gives freely; making total the one function every shown name passes through closes the class, where checking origins one by one left the inventory incomplete; refusing a descriptor's string at the one place every importer reaches leaves no path around it; the order of the checks is pinned by a test generated from the code's own slots, not by prose; the CLI and the configuration cannot send or open what is not text, so they refuse it where it enters |
| D399 | Server text closed at its type (M4.0f-A2a, provisional; amends D397, D283, D285, D303, D343, D231, D374, D225 and D268) | **Construction is closed at the type.** `schema.output.TextSegment` is final (creating a subclass raises), has no `model_construct`, and its own validator admits an exact `TextSegment` instance, which every output re-validates (`revalidate_instances="always"`, pinned by a test), or a mapping only in a call whose validation context is a capability; anything else (a `dict` or any other `Mapping`, an object with a `text` attribute read through `from_attributes`, another model or output with a `text` member, JSON text, strings) is refused with the guard's own message (`GUARD_MESSAGE`, which names nothing it was given), by every entry Pydantic has (the constructor, `model_validate`, `model_validate_json`, `model_validate_strings`, a `TypeAdapter`), so every output that holds one refuses it, under every call option. An output is frozen but its lists are not, so **the outermost dump of an output validates the whole again** (`Output._omit_absent` calls `model_validate` on the instance, which re-validates every nested output, `revalidate_instances="always"`, and meets the guard): a mapping put in a segment's place in place (`append`, item or slice assignment) is refused by every dump entry (`model_dump`, `model_dump_json`, `json()`, a `TypeAdapter`, `pydantic_core.to_json`, a dump after `model_copy`), with the guard's message, and `hide_input_in_errors` on every output, and on each module-level `TypeAdapter` of the core whose type reaches server text (a bare adapter takes no model's configuration, but accepts `config=`; a test finds them by reflection), keeps what was refused out of the error's string. A validator that dumps the output it is validating says so (`dumped_in_validation`), which skips the second validation (not `check_values`) and is not validated twice; it is a capability like a boundary's: the static rule lets only the functions of `DUMPS_IN_VALIDATION` call it (the after-validators of `ResultEnvelope` and `CohortCount` and their digest check, each with its reason), by its name, never aliased, imported under another name or spelled as a string. Tuples and lists stay as the schemas have them. The capability is the validation context of **one call**: `text()`'s, and a listed **boundary**'s (`context=admitted_at(Boundary.X)`); Pydantic gives it to that call's validators and to nothing else, so no property, subscript, task, thread, generator or copied context holds it; a validator reached in the call (a nested validation of it) is given it, and could read it as `info.context` and pass it on or keep it, which the static rule bans (below). **`Boundary` is closed**: `REPORT_NOTES` (an import report's notes, read in `store/proposals.curation_queue`; stored), `RESULT_CACHE` (a cached result's content, read in `analyses/results.Digested.read`; stored), `READER_ANSWER` (a reader child's answer in `importers/worker.Reader.run`: its unpickling, `unpickled`, the one place a `TextSegment` is unpickled, and its refusals; the child runs the server's own code under the same guard and is not a privilege boundary, D225) and `CLI_CLIENT` (the operator CLI reading the answers of the server it is configured with, which it trusts as it trusts it with the curator token, D268). A test holds each member's sites, by file and function, so a new site is a new member, with its reason. A boundary site is one statement (`x = C`, `return C` or `C`, in no `async def`) whose one call is `<name or attribute chain>.{model_validate, model_validate_json, validate_python, validate_json}(<names, attributes, subscripts or constants>, context=admitted_at(Boundary.X))` or `unpickled(Boundary.READER_ANSWER, <name>)`. A segment that crossed a boundary unchecked (a skipped relation's reason, unpickled), and a pack's segments (a leaf's summary, D285), are validated again as the exact instances they are, with no capability. `Segment` is a tagged union, its arm picked by what the input is (a data segment, a mapping with no `text` key and an object with no `text` attribute are data; anything else is text and meets the guard), so that re-validating a message of data tokens does not try the text guard first (it cost +47% in a measure of 2,000 refusals); its JSON Schema is the same `anyOf`. **Writers validate** what a boundary reads back: the report writer validates a note's message as segments before it dumps it; the reader child sends its refusals dumped from `Refusals`, and refusals its writer refuses (a look-alike, or none) are the server's fault, raised as `ReaderError`, never the file's; the cache writes `_Written`; the server answers the CLI with validated outputs. **Stored text records what text meant.** Two numbers: `REPORT_CLASSIFICATION`, what counts as server text, raised only when that changes (M4.0f-A2b's conversions); and `CACHE_WORDING`, the server's wording, raised with any change to a template of the server's text (a test holds the digest of every template reaching `text()` or a function whose argument becomes server text, a sorted multiset so that moving code changes nothing), with any change to the text projection of a golden result (each segment's pointer, kind and text, and each chart's title and description; no number), and with every change of `REPORT_CLASSIFICATION` (a checked-in history of the pairs). A `/2` report is of classification 1 and is served as written only while the server's is 1, and otherwise read with each note's fixed text, as a `/1` report is (D231, D397); no blob is rewritten. A `/2` report written by A1's or B's writer, which did not validate its notes, is trusted as classification 1: no store holds one, since #89, #90 and #91 land together. A cached result records `text_format`, the wording it was written under, in its hashed content but not its digest, and is read back only under the server's wording; another, or none (a row written before D399, which reads as 0), is a miss the call fills again, and is not logged, since it is no fault (D374); there is no migration. **Pydantic 1's API is closed at the type**: `Output` raises for `copy`, `_copy_and_set_values`, `_iter`, `_calculate_keys`, `_get_value`, `construct`, `from_orm`, `parse_obj`, `parse_raw`, `parse_file`, `validate` and `update_forward_refs`, which build or copy without the checks of an output or reach its internals (`copy(update=...)` made a `TextSegment` of any text); its dumps (`dict`, `json`, `schema`) only read. **A static rule** over `src/aibi` (the core and every pack) bans, by attribute name whatever the receiver, the routes that skip validators, and a **run-time brute force** holds its table to what they do: every attribute name of `BaseModel`, `Output` and `TextSegment` is classified harmless or banned (a test fails on a name Pydantic adds until it is classified), and the brute force tries each name at run time on a `TextSegment` and on a carrier (called in many shapes, or read and written to, with a mapping, an `update`, a state or the instance, then looked at) (on the instance, the class, `Output`, `object`, the metaclass and a `TypeAdapter` of each; the names are those of `BaseModel`, `Output` and `TextSegment`, so a name only a `TypeAdapter` has, such as `core_schema`, `validator` or `serializer`, is not tried) and finds the names that make text: a name is banned exactly when it does, in both directions, so that a name a classification gets wrong, or flips, fails. A banned one (`model_construct`, `__setstate__`, `__getstate__`, `__reduce__`, `__reduce_ex__`, `__getattribute__`, `__getattr__`, `__setattr__`, `model_config`, `__pydantic_*__`, `__dict__`) is never named, nor spelled as a string (`getattr(x, "__dict__")`), but at a listed read site, with its reason; the name of the output module and those of the capability are never spelled as strings either; every identifier the syntax names but a definition's own name (`def`, `class`) and a `from` import's module (a keyword argument's, a pattern's, an import's alias, a parameter's, a `global`'s, whatever its node) is held to the banned and capability names too, not only `Name`, `Attribute` and string nodes; no classified name is assigned or deleted as an attribute, or named by `setattr` or `delattr`, whose name must be a constant; `getattr`, `getattr_static`, `attrgetter`, `methodcaller`, `__getattribute__` and `__getattr__` are given a constant name, but at a listed function; `setattr`, `delattr`, `getattr`, `vars` and `attrgetter` are never bound to another name; `BaseModel.<attribute>` and two-argument `super`, which skip an override, are never written; `vars(x)` is `__dict__`; `object.__setattr__` sets only a frozen dataclass's declared member in its `__post_init__`; `copy(update=...)` is never called, and no call that validates or copies is given `*` or `**` or its options by position; `exec`, `eval`, `compile`, `__import__` and `import_module` are never called; `ctypes` and `gc` are imported only where listed; no attribute of the output module, or of a class it exports, is assigned; a `TextSegment` is never built by a call (`text()` makes one); the capability's names appear only in boundary sites, every other `context=` is a dict literal, and the validation context is read (`info.context`) only at two listed functions (the configuration's path validator and the document's `_parsed`), each of which reads a dict and assumes none, so no validator forwards or keeps a capability, and a validator that reads the context must not assume a dict; and every route answers with a `Response` (FastAPI would dump and validate a returned model again, which the guard refuses: availability, not the invariant). **Threat model.** The adversary is data: text from a file, a database, a request, a document or a model, which reaches the server's code as values. The server's own code is trusted but fallible: it can make a mistake (a model built from a mapping, an object read through `from_attributes`, a stored blob read back, a list changed in place) and it is reviewed. The guard and the dump catch the routes a mistake takes; the static rule bans the reflective routes by a short syntactic list, so that a mistake does not take one without a reviewer seeing it. **This is not a defence against hostile code in the tree**: code that means to make server text from data can do so by routes the list does not name. **What D399 guarantees**: against data, and against a mistake that takes a route Pydantic offers or one the list names, no `TextSegment` is made except by `text()`, as an exact instance being re-validated, or inside a listed boundary's one call, and no output is dumped that holds a segment otherwise made. **Non-guarantees** (spellings the static rule does not stop, each found by a reviewer and left, since a longer list only moves the line): a `match`/`case` pattern on `__dict__` (a bare identifier, now flagged as a banned identifier, but a pattern can reach the dictionary other ways); a `copy.deepcopy(x, memo)` whose memo is kept, so that the segment's dictionary is among the memo's values; `inspect.getmembers`, whose members include the dictionary; `copyreg._reduce_ex`, whose state is the dictionary; `__builtins__["vars"]` and the like, a name spelled by arithmetic or built from parts; the core schema of a `TypeAdapter(TextSegment)` taken apart, its wrap validator removed and the rest run by a `SchemaValidator`, which makes an exact `TextSegment` from a mapping; and a context copied while a dump runs (`contextvars.copy_context`, or a task or a thread started by a validator or serializer reached in a dump), which carries the mark of the dump in progress, so that a dump run in it skips the checks (nothing in `src` does this). **What it does not prove**: `text()`'s argument, that is, f-strings, the functions that turn a string into text (`importers/errors.refused`, `StoreRefused`) and aliases of `text` (`partial(text)`, `getattr(output, ...)`), which A1's census lists until M4.0f-A2b proves it; nor what a validator a boundary's call reaches builds from a mapping (a test holds the only mappings of `src` with a `text` key, in a display, `{**m, "text": v}` included, or a `dict(text=...)` or `dict(**...)` call: `text()`'s own and a `/1` report's fixed text, a constant; a display of `**` alone or a computed key is not seen), nor a route through a name computed by arithmetic, which no rule over the syntax reads (`getattr` and the rest are given constants, and `exec` and `eval` are never called); nor the spellings above. A guard's refusal fails closed, as a `ValidationError`, and an existing `except ValidationError` handler may report it as an ordinary refusal; the tests assert the guard's own message. An id of a known pattern is data, and M4.0f-A2b marks it `x-aibi-data`. **Amends** D397 (construction closed by the type; its known exceptions are M4.0f-A2b's); D283 (a persisted caveat's text and a persisted note's text carry their wording or classification); D285, D303 and D343 (a pack's segments meet the same guard: `Pack.wording`, a pack's notes and refusals and `LeafKind.summary` are server text only as the pack's own literals, constants, numbers and enumeration values, which M4.0f-A2b proves over `aibi.packs`); D231 (`/2` is classification 1); D374 (the digested content carries `text_format`; another is a quiet miss); D225 (the child validates what it sends, and a fault of its writer is `ReaderError`); D268 (the CLI trusts the configured server's segments) | A1 made server text a rule and a census, and a census lists sites: a model built from a mapping, an object read through `from_attributes`, a stored blob read back or a reader's pipe could still make server text of data. Closing construction in the type's own validator covers every entry Pydantic has and every model that holds a segment, with no inventory to complete; an allow-list (the exact instance) closes what a deny-list (a `dict`) left open; a capability carried in one call's context cannot reach what that call's arguments evaluate or what its validators call, where an ambient one did (three plan reviews found routes through properties, tasks, copied contexts and re-entrant validators); the routes that skip validators are reflective and few, so a rule by name, with a brute force over Pydantic's own names, stops the ones a mistake takes (it is a short list, not a proof, and not a defence against hostile code); a list is mutable and a dump is the last place a segment is written from, so the dump validates again what it writes; writers that validate, and stored blobs that record what text meant, keep bytes the server wrote from carrying a look-alike or an older meaning back as server text; two numbers, not one, so that a typo in a template refreshes the cache without turning every stored report to fixed text for good |
