## Appendix B. Change log

- **v0.8.51** — Reshaped tables with absent values (M4.0b-1, D401): the pack `rebuild` extension
  point is removed, and the core rebuilds a reshaped table from its reshaped rows; a pack that
  unpivots a matrix declares the absent values it dropped, how many with their digest, and the empty
  cells, and the core writes the column's `absent` (§5), its report notes, the rule that keeps
  dropped cells from reading as anything but what they are (`check_release`), the gate's check of
  the coverage against the declaration and of the column's cells (§13.2), and the freeze of what the
  importer owns (§10.1, §12.2, §15 M4, D220, D245). The declaration is kept with the table in the
  manifest and the coverage is checked against it at every import and at every change whose inputs
  to the check (what it reads, the tables by their content hash, with its version, digested and kept
  with the declaration) changed (a re-import, which always checks, leaves `checked` out of its
  `NO_CHANGE` comparison); the listing is counted before it is built, and the declared cells count
  toward `import_cells`; the digest is a SHA-256 commitment to the sorted cells; a re-import that
  stops declaring a table it unpivoted is refused; an explode's coverage lists the units its source
  held. Unpivots without absent values (M4.0b-2) are deferred: their empty cells stay null rows.
  M4.0b was split again at its plan's round cap (#19).
- **v0.8.50** — Checks on a pack importer's result (M4.0a, D400): a pack's importer is called
  through `importers.checks.run_importer`, which keeps the reader's refusals, gives a `Refused`'s
  refusals, maps a `MemoryError` to `import_bytes` and anything else to `PACK_FAILED`, and checks
  its result before any validator: its shape, every source read by a table, the import's limits as
  the core's importers count them (`import_tables`, `table_columns`, `import_cells`, `import_bytes`,
  `decoded_bytes`, and long cells), and its notes (the kinds an importer writes, within the string
  and text limits) (§10.1). The checks take the result apart into a copy of exact types, which
  alone is handed on, so that nothing that passes them fails later; integers are within 64 bits, and
  `import_bytes` is per file, as for the core's importers. The reader reads at most the operator's
  `import_bytes`; the checks' own refusals stand only as the instances they raised; a pack's
  refusals are bounded as notes are and point to no path; and `pack_failed` looks a pack's exception
  type up by identity, for every pack guard. A refusal is the reader's or the checks' only when the
  core's own code raised it, read from its traceback (an interpreter hook the pack installed, or its
  subclass's method, raising inside their frames is the pack's); the reader makes its own path of an
  exact `str` first; and the checks compare types by identity and use the class's serializers, as a
  pack leaf's summary guard now does, what a descriptor's serializer reaches being the pack's
  failure; a pack whose process-wide hooks change what the core reads without raising, that compiles
  code under a core file's name or that runs native code is outside what is checked, as
  introspection is; the output check reads a value's type from the type's method resolution order;
  validators get a copy that shares nothing with what is built but immutable built-in values, and
  the limits are taken before the importer runs. `import_release` raises `ValueError` for a source
  no table reads. This is M4.0's first part: its plan review reached the round cap and was split
  into M4.0a and M4.0b, reshaped tables (#19).
- **v0.8.49** (provisional) — Server text closed at its type (M4.0f-A2a, D399): `TextSegment`
  admits an exact instance, or a mapping only in the one call whose validation context is
  `text()`'s or a listed boundary's capability (`REPORT_NOTES`, `RESULT_CACHE`, `READER_ANSWER`,
  `CLI_CLIENT`); it is final, has no `model_construct`, and is unpickled only at the reader's
  boundary. The report writer and the reader child validate what they write; a `/2` report is
  served as written only under classification 1 (`REPORT_CLASSIFICATION`), and a cached result
  only under the wording it was written with (`CACHE_WORDING`, a quiet miss otherwise, no
  migration). Pydantic 1's API is closed on every output, the outermost dump of an output
  validates it again (a list changed in place is refused), and a static rule over the core and
  the packs bans the reflective routes by a short syntactic list, its table held to a run-time
  brute force: a defence against data and against mistakes, with its threat model and
  non-guarantees stated, not against hostile code in the tree. No id,
  digest, descriptor or report blob changes. §8.1 and §14 say so; D397, D283, D285, D303, D343,
  D231, D374, D225 and D268 amended.
- **v0.8.48** (provisional) — Names that are not Unicode text (M4.0f-B, D398): `data()` is total,
  writing a lone surrogate or a noncharacter as its escape and cutting only between escapes, the
  identity on text; `escaped` is retired and `shown` is `data`. No name raises out of the
  importers: `describe` refuses every string it is given that is not text, in a stated order;
  a file's or member's name is refused as it is listed, a connection's file by its location at
  resolve, a URL's database, a Parquet name that is not UTF-8; confinement refuses a path that
  cannot be encoded first. The server does not start with a data or import directory whose real
  path is not UTF-8 (a deliberate change), and the CLI refuses an argument or a sent variable that
  is not UTF-8. Reader catches re-raise a `ValidationError`, which a reader child reports by its
  class alone. §8.1 and §14 say so; D225, D226, D232, D253, D266, D268, D307, D309, D310 and D397
  amended.
- **v0.8.47** (provisional) — Server text holds only the server's words (M4.0f-A1, D397): a
  `text` segment holds the server's literals and constants, numbers and enumeration and
  `Literal` values, and everything else is data, one value or id to a token: the ids in
  `infer`'s notes, `derive`'s problems and the builds' refusals, type strings, field and member
  names and time zones (`shown`), staged units, a curated encoding, libraries' messages, CORS
  origins and connection names. Messages travel as segments (`Message`) in every carrier. The
  import report is `aibi.import-report/2`; a `/1` report is read with every note's fixed text
  (`NOTE_TEXT`, now in `schema.pack_api`) by the one reader both queues use, and never rewritten.
  §8.1 and §14 state the rule positively; D126, D225, D229, D231 and D309 amended. The class
  test is M4.0f-A2's.
- **v0.8.34** — Memberships under a disclosure setting (M3.2e-2b, D383, D384): under *k* a
  variable's memberships list the column's declared categories alone, whatever its units hold,
  in both engines (`memberships.listing(…, declared=True)`, `sql.compile_materialised(…,
  declared=True)`, D384), and each row is disclosed as `some` of its one category is as a
  variable (D329): its split of known and UNKNOWN units whole or not at all, its TRUE units only
  with it and where neither they nor its FALSE units are from 1 to *k* − 1, no map of reasons,
  and the variable's `analysed` suppressed (§8.1, §8.4, `disclosure.membership_shown`, D383).
  Where the descriptors may bound the rows each unit reaches (a column not a list whose path is
  not one open down step from the unit, `resolve.bounded_rows`, `resolve.open_path`) they stay
  withheld, `WITHHELD_UNDER_K` at `…/each` by `summary.distribution`'s own check
  (`distribution.withheld_under_k`), since the TRUE counts then sum to at most that many times
  the units and pin one the rule hides; elsewhere `each` is offered and runs under *k*, and
  `withheld_forms` is `count: "rows"` alone (§9.2, D353, D379,
  D382). A brute force over worlds read by the reference evaluator from palettes of every small
  pattern of rows, in eleven configurations, with a product check over any number of categories,
  whole outputs and a variable of another table beside them, finds no pin but where the
  descriptors bound the rows each unit reaches. §8.4's Limits now name variables and cohorts
  whose paths share a relationship or a coverage table, which bound one another (examples
  (a), (b) and (c), which reproduce in `compare.existence` and `summary.distribution` today, #73),
  counts crossing two categories of one variable, and units that read the same rows; *Predicates of
  a result* says D320's claim holds for independent predicates alone (D320). No version is bumped;
  goldens `order_channel_by_tier_under_k` and `order_channel_memberships_under_k` are added.
  Round 1 of #74's review: the withholding covered only one-to-one paths and children keyed within
  their child columns, and missed keys completed by a record-filtered column or a boolean, which
  pinned a hidden count through `run_analysis`; it now covers every row bound the descriptors give
  (`resolve.open_step`), the rule's claim is stated for rows no descriptor bounds, the refusal
  cites D383, and the memberships' `UNKNOWN_EXCLUDED` message says under *k* that no count by
  reason is shown. Round 2: that fix read the child's key and the step's own relationship and
  coverage alone, and missed bounds the gate enforces through other descriptors (another
  coverage's record filter, a relationship's parent columns, another one-to-one relationship, a
  derived key column), so the test is inverted: a step is open only where every set of its
  child's columns the gate keeps unique holds a free column (`resolve.unique_columns`,
  `resolve.free_column`, from the gate's checks as `resolve.GATE_CHECKS` classifies them), and
  everything else is withheld; a test holds the classification to the gate's source and a
  property test holds "open" to the gate itself. §8.4's Limits name a column unique across its
  table (#73). Round 3 found that a path with an open step still bounds each unit's rows where a
  later child is keyed within the unit's key carried down a composite foreign key (Q1, Q2), the
  third instance of the class, which ended review at #2's cap; the claim is narrowed to what is
  proven per unit: a path of one open down step from the unit (`resolve.open_path`), every
  longer path withheld (#75 for a per-unit proof), a property test over generated chains of one
  to three steps importing one unit's rows through the gate, a derivation bounding its inputs
  only where the gate checks it, pack validators outside the claim, and the gate's check sites
  counted. Review restarts at round 1. Round 1 after the redesign: §8.4's and D383's Limits
  name a count of the rows shown in any call, which bounds the sum of the TRUE counts over the
  units (#73). Round 2 after the redesign: a relationship of the unit table to itself is no
  open path (its child rows are the units, so the view's own population bounds that sum), and
  the Limits name every source of a column's uniqueness across its table (#73).
- **v0.8.33** — Memberships in `summary.distribution` (M3.2e-2a-2, D382): a variable's
  `each: "category"` gives, per category its cohort lists, the units for which some row or item
  has it over those for which that is known, its UNKNOWN units in the proportion's `excluded` by
  reason and its key `compare.existence`'s (`memberships`, `multi_membership: true`, §9.5);
  `analysed` counts a unit known for some listed category, and so depends on the listing (§8.1);
  its own `UNKNOWN_EXCLUDED` message, readback sentence and chart; `bins` with `each` is
  `CONFLICTING_MEMBERS`; a bare multi-valued column is `AGGREGATE_REQUIRED` in every analysis,
  offering `each` in a descriptive one but beside `bins` and under a disclosure setting, below
  scoped coverage too, as D380's checks of each offered form allow (`compare.columns`',
  `survival.cox`'s and a pack's refuse memberships), and a list's `count: "rows"`
  `INVALID_VALUE`, offering what runs in its place (§9.2); memberships are withheld under any disclosure
  setting at `…/each`, offering `some` and `every` with `values`, and §8.4's Limits add that a
  column's categories are predicates that depend on one another (§8.4, §9.4, D353, D379); the
  million test gains a view of memberships (D382); no version is bumped. Round 1 of #72's review:
  withheld memberships offer `every` only where the path allows it (`resolve.aggregates_of`, as
  resolution offers it), beside `bins` a refusal offers only the forms whose values are numbers,
  and a category longer than `MAX_TEXT` is `LIMIT_EXCEEDED` at its column, as a covariate's
  level is (§8.4, §9.2, D368, D382). Round 2 of #72's review: a category, a covariate's level
  or a listed key that is not Unicode text is `NOT_SUPPORTED`, by D271's rule, where it was an
  internal error, a length counts code points, and a list's aggregate it does not take beside
  `bins` says to leave them out (§8.2, §9.2, D331, D368, D379, D382).
- **v0.8.32** — Per-category memberships in the engine (M3.2e-2a-1, D380, D381): a variable's
  `each: "category"` asks, of each category of a multi-valued categorical column, the existence
  question of its value by §6.5 in full, its categories listed from the declared values and those
  whose answer is not a unit's default, a filtered column's the filter's allowed values
  (§6.5, §9.2); their canonical form is their template with `"values": []`, a variable's member and
  no clause's, a category's key `compare.existence`'s for a declared value (§7.6); the reference
  evaluator asks each category, SQL counts pairs plus default with tallies, never subtracting an
  OR, proved step by step and measured at a million units (D381); `each` is `NOT_SUPPORTED` in
  `summary.distribution` until M3.2e-2a-2 and `INVALID_VALUE` where groups are independent; M3.2e-2a
  in two parts (§15, D377). Round 1 of #71's review: each refusal of `each` offers what runs in
  its place, the aggregates its column takes or leaving `each` out, and `summary.distribution`
  refuses memberships once resolved (§9.2, D380); past `MAX_CATEGORIES` neither engine gives a
  joint count (D380); the default is asked only of the categories that can be listed, a pair
  may differ from it in its flags alone, a record filter's answer carries none, and a third
  property holds the engines together over two scope columns, grouped coverage and filters on a
  scope column; the cost is measured again (D381). Round 2 of #71's review: SQL reads every
  step's record filter, not the last step's alone, as the evaluator does and D381's proof
  assumes, and the properties generate filters on earlier steps and on a relationship the path
  takes twice (D381); a refusal of `each` offers leaving it out only where the analysis reads
  the column as it is, and it and D377's refusal of the bare column offer only the aggregates
  the column's path allows (§9.2, D377, D380); the evaluator asks no value a coverage table
  stores as another type than the column's categories, as SQL lists none (D380, D381); a view
  refused in phase 2 records no histogram's edges (D380); scenarios pin a tuple listed with an
  unknown scope cell and a group covering every value that lists a tuple, and D381 says which
  aggregates OR flag words and why the listing's cap needs no order (D381). Round 3 of #71's
  review: every form a refusal of `each`, of the bare multi-valued column or of an aggregate the
  column does not take offers passes the analysis's own phase-2 checks of that form, so that
  `survival.cox` is offered no date, identifier or endpoint column and a pack's analysis no date,
  datetime or identifier, and the last offers only the aggregates its path allows beside the
  variable's `where` and `empty`; an analysis that reads one value per unit says so, not that it
  compares units; and tests pin `every` of a looked-up column named as a scope column, a
  category scope column listed as a boolean, and the cap at 150, 151 and 152 (§9.2, D377, D380);
  a column with no step down, which has no rows to pool, is offered no aggregate beside a `where`
  (D377).
- **v0.8.31** — Counts of rows in `summary.distribution` (M3.2e-1, D377–D379): M3.2e in three
  parts (§15); `count: "rows"` counts the rows a numeric aggregate pools, once for every path and
  unit that reaches them, its units in `analysed` and its rows left out by reason in
  `excluded_rows`, as `category_rows` or `number_rows` (§8.1, §9.2, §9.5); a disclosed analysis
  may withhold a form of its parameters under any *k*, and `summary.distribution` withholds
  `count: "rows"` (`WITHHELD_UNDER_K` at `…/count`, §8.4, §9.4); D353 amended; a multi-valued
  categorical column without an aggregate names M3.2e-2a. Round 1 of #70's review: a withheld
  form's path is checked against the parameters' model and the first withheld member is the
  first in the parameters' order (D379); a multi-valued column without an aggregate is offered
  `count: "rows"` but for a list, and its `NOT_SUPPORTED` lists its alternatives (§9.2, D377,
  D378); the property and a golden reach rows by two paths within one unit, lookups and `bins`.
  Round 2 of #70's review: under a disclosure setting no refusal offers `count: "rows"`, which
  resolution is told of, and a withheld one offers the aggregates that run in its place, with
  their `bins` and `values`, not the view without `count` (§8.4, D379); a multi-valued column's
  refusals list only the aggregates its column takes, `count` among them, `max` and `min` of
  numbers and ordered categories alone (§9.2, D377, D378). Round 3: the alternatives keep the
  variable's own `where` and `bins`, and those of an aggregate a column does not take follow its
  `where` and `empty` (D379).
- **v0.8.30** — `run_analysis`'s hits and fills (D376): hits looked up after phase 2 leave
  every plan and are rendered for their own call; fills and hits checked and written in the
  issuances' transaction; a hit whose cohort was erased or whose filler is gone runs the call
  again without the cache; `Catalog.cache`; D318 and D372 amended (§8.1, §12.2). Round 1 of
  #69's review: a row that does not read back is dropped and filled again; `SourceGoneError`
  only for a filler the log no longer holds. Round 2: a view any of whose cohorts reads a draft
  is never cached.
- **v0.8.29** — The result cache's store (D375): migration 7's `result_cache`, its contents
  apart, kept by triggers (a row goes with its filler, with a withdrawn release it read, and
  with everything on erasure), `ResultCache` (`lookup` by engine and versions, `fill` in the
  caller's transaction with LRU eviction, `touch`, `purge`, `clear`, `trim`) and
  `[cache] result_bytes` (§12.2, §14). Round 1 of #68's review: a row's engine, versions, *k*
  and manifests are the log's for its filler and result, never the caller's; a filled row
  changes only by being used; a lowered bound is kept when the store opens. Round 2: a refused
  fill undoes its writes, a draft's result is declined, a content is written once, the engine's
  and packs' text is charged, and the server's wiring of the cache is tested.
- **v0.8.28** — A result's digested content apart from its rendering (D374): M3.5 in three parts
  (§15); `results.digested` and `Digested` (written as JSON and read back through the analysis's
  values model, `CoreAnalysis.values`), `results.render` for one issuance with its `values_from`,
  and `envelope` as their composition; no output changes. Round 1 of #67's review: the content
  carries its result id and digest, which `read` checks, and a `Digested` takes its own digest. Round 2: the content carries a hash of itself, which `read` checks, and typed values must give the values as JSON text.
- **v0.8.27** — The thread-count determinism tests (D372, D373): an orchard of a million trees in
  row groups of 2^17 rows, published once per module by a test-only importer, and `count_cohort`
  and `run_analysis` of every core analysis and a pack's compared across `query_threads`, under the
  `million` marker in a CI job of their own (§9.3, §13); scale measured: DuckDB's memory grows with
  a crossing's predicates and threads beyond what §14 said, a table of fewer than 2^20 rows is
  scanned on one thread, and the defaults stay (§14; D318's, D327's and D333's figures). Round 1
  of #66's review: late harvests in other row groups whose weights' sum depends on its order, each
  table's rows pinned, no cached answer compared, the marker test's gaps closed, and D373's
  figures given per call on the final fixture. Round 2: `block`, 127 undeclared categories whose order
  out of DuckDB differs across threads, in the distribution's and columns' views; the row groups
  pinned; the marker test reads the job's step and the modules' top level. Round 3: a step that
  requires the report to hold every determinism test passed, `block`'s undeclared categories
  pinned, and D327's figures attributed to their measurements.
- **v0.8.26** — Predicates as `survival.cox`'s covariates (D370, D371): a covariate is a variable
  or `{"predicate": <clause>}`, told by its members; a predicate is checked as a view's are and
  refused where it tests an identifier or the endpoint's own columns; it codes as a boolean, a cell
  that does not apply as FALSE and UNKNOWN left out by reason; it is listed as a question of its
  clause, and one that holds a lift is also listed flipped, `CoxPosition.lifts` and `LIFT_DIFFERS`
  counting the members it changes; no id changes (§9.5). D362 and D366 amended. Rounds 1 and 2
  of #65's review: the first place written by position, the flipped columns' cost stated, an
  aggregate's `where` refused on identifiers as a predicate is (D367), a negation's coding. Round
  3: a predicate covariate's fields, packs, leaves and summaries are read once, from its predicate
  (D371).
- **v0.8.25** — `survival.cox` itself (D366–D369): its entry and parameters, covariates being
  variables and a stratum; each covariate's coding by its variable, and phase 2's refusals (dates,
  identifier columns, the endpoint's own columns, `bins`, one cohort without covariates); its run
  on listed members, refusing times, entries and numbers beyond ±(2^53 − 1), long levels, more
  than 8 columns or 100 strata and columns the fit cannot scale; caveats, readback and a forest
  chart; erasure reads its covariates and stratum; `registry.LATER` is empty (§9.5, §14). Every
  refusal of phase 2 and of `run_analysis` points into the document as written (D368).
- **v0.8.24** — `survival.cox`'s model of cohorts and covariates (D362–D365): `survival.cox` comes
  in three parts (§15); its complete cases, coding, fit, labels and values, held to R, with the
  baseline among the levels with an event, each covariate's joint test and per-term p-values (§9.5).
- **v0.8.23** — Separation and identification in the Cox model of a design (D360, D361): every
  column of a design is estimated, `separation` (with its sign) or `zero_variance` by its
  likelihood's recession cone, decided in exact arithmetic after R's own drop (§9.5); exact
  dependencies make every column of theirs `zero_variance`; the finite part is fitted stratified
  by the classes the cone's implicit equalities leave, and its estimated terms have the test of
  proportional hazards in it, R's where nothing is separated; D359's unscalable columns include
  those with a value that is not finite.
- **v0.8.22** — The Cox model of a design (D358, D359): M3.3's Cox model comes in three parts,
  M3.3c the model of a design, M3.3d separation in it, M3.3e `survival.cox` (§15); the model fits
  any covariates per unit over strata and left-truncated risk sets with R's handling of columns,
  the certificate and ascent of D356, and `cox.zph`'s test with strata, held to R.
- **v0.8.21** — Survival, M3.3's second part (D354–D357): M3.3 comes in three parts, M3.3b
  `survival.km` 1.1.0 and M3.3c `survival.cox` (§15); `survival.km` 1.1.0 gives each cohort's
  unadjusted hazard ratio versus the reference with its Wald interval and the Grambsch–Therneau
  global test of proportional hazards, with `PH_VIOLATED` and `SMALL_N` per term (§9.1, §9.5);
  a Cox fit whose only terms are cohorts decides separation exactly on the risk sets' graph, fits
  the reference's strongly connected component alone by R's `coxfit6` or `agfit4` where that ends
  at its maximum and from the point a safeguarded ascent reaches where it does not, and tests it
  by `cox.zph`'s method, held to R (§9.5); §9.1's example carries no `SUPPRESSED`.
- **v0.8.20** — Disclosure classes (D353): every analysis is disclosed or refused, as the core
  states it and every pack's refused (§8.4); a view of a refused analysis under any disclosure
  setting is refused in phase 2 with the new code `WITHHELD_UNDER_K`, where `summary.members` gave
  `ROW_IDS_NOT_ALLOWED` and packs' analyses and survival `NOT_SUPPORTED` (§8.4, §8.6), and one of
  an analysis that lists or hands units' values in the keys' order `ROW_IDS_NOT_ALLOWED` where its
  dataset allows no row ids; applicability agrees with phase 2 (§9.4); D332, D337, D344 and D351
  cite the rule.
- **v0.8.19** — Survival, M3.3's first part (D346–D352): M3.3 comes in two parts, M3.3a
  `survival.km` and M3.3b `survival.cox` (§15); a view of a survival analysis names its
  endpoint or reads the one usable on the unit table, whose rows are listed as a pack's inputs
  are, a status compared with the coding as the gate compares values (§5.8, §7.4); `survival.km`'s
  parameters (endpoint, landmarks, grid, level), curve, median, landmarks, log-rank test and
  difference in medians, their estimability and the log-rank test's degrees of freedom as its
  covariance's rank (§9.5); its methods held to R
  4.3.3's survival 3.5-8 and its bootstrap drawn row by row, seeded from the computation id and
  the position (§9.3); under any disclosure setting a survival analysis is refused and
  unavailable, since a curve's values give the censorings between its events, which replaces
  §8.4's grid rule for survival curves and models (§8.4, §9.4); a pack's analysis binds its
  endpoint requirements by role and is handed their rows (§9.1, §10.1); erasure reads a survival
  view's times as constants on its endpoint's time column (§12.2). After the first review, within
  v0.8.19: a Newton step that overflows `exp` is halved instead of failing the call (D349; with
  the Cox fit, now M3.3b's); a unit's time or entry beyond ±(2^53 − 1) is refused before any value
  is made, grid views included, a hazard ratio or bound out of range is `separation` (now M3.3b's)
  and a test's statistic out of range `zero_variance`, instead of refusing the view (D348, §9.5); every loop over units looks at
  the deadline (D350); D351's rationale says what its brute force shows, that §8.4's grid rule
  fails. After the second review, within v0.8.19: every floating operation of the time-to-event
  code is C's, a division by 0 and an `exp` that underflows included, so a step either way out of
  range is halved, a test holding the code to it by its syntax (D349); a time or entry that is
  not a number is invalid (D347); the deadline is looked at as endpoint rows are made, within a
  position's values and every 16,384 times round a loop, and a call at the listing's cap overruns
  it by 0.34 s at most as measured (D350); a pack's endpoint whose columns identify rows or people
  is never handed (D352). After the third review, which met #2's round cap in the Cox fit, within
  v0.8.19: the Cox hazard ratio and its test of proportional hazards move to M3.3b with
  `survival.cox`, `survival.km` 1.0.0 giving neither and 1.1.0 both (D346, D348, D349, §9.5);
  every loop of the survival methods looks at the deadline by the work its turns do, so ties
  cannot stretch the time between looks, measured at 0.28 s at most at the listing's cap (D350);
  the check that holds the time-to-event code to C's arithmetic sees `math` however it is reached
  and the modules and methods that would bypass it (D349). After the split's first review, within
  v0.8.19: an endpoint that declares no entry makes its analyses `available_with_caveats` (D347);
  the R reference gains a case whose median's lower bound lies above the median, as R gives it
  (D349). After the split's second review, within v0.8.19: a group alone at risk adds exactly 0
  to the log-rank covariance, so cohorts never at risk together have no test (D349, §9.5); an
  endpoint's fields, an undeclared entry included, are standing unconfirmed fields (D275); D350's
  bound is measured over lopsided positions in key order, 0.55 s, and 0.93 s with the garbage
  collector's pauses, which it states. After the split's third review, within v0.8.19: a
  bootstrap replicate whose curve ends just above ½ has no median, as `findq` finds none (D350).
- **v0.8.18** — Packs' analyses, M3.2's fourth part (D341–D345): a view of a pack's analysis
  binds its entry's `column` requirements by role and checks its `options` against the entry's
  `params` schema (§9.1, §10.1); the core lists each cohort's members with each column's value,
  by SQL and by the reference evaluator, in the keys' order of §9.3 without handing the keys, and
  runs the pack's `run` on them, its values checked against its `returns` schema and shaped as a
  result's `values` (§10.1); endpoint requirements wait for M3.3 (§15); under any disclosure
  setting a view of a pack's analysis is refused and applicability calls it unavailable, since
  its values are no counts the pass can protect (§8.4, §9.4); pack leaves in a variable's
  `where` are expanded, and their packs join the view's ids (§7.3, §9.2); erasure reads a pack
  view's `options` as what the core cannot read (§12.2). After the first review, within v0.8.18:
  the listing of inputs takes each cohort's members once, no more than its caps admit, and
  materialises every relation it reads, since DuckDB's inlined plan did not end over 512,000
  units (D342); a view of a pack's analysis is refused where its dataset allows no row ids, and
  no key column's values are handed (§8.4, D342, D344); what a pack gives is read under the
  guard of `PACK_FAILED`, within caps of values, characters and a string's length counted before
  it is copied, and no refusal quotes it (§14, D343); the call's deadline is looked at after the
  pack's values are checked and counted (D343); and applicability calls unavailable a pack's
  analysis whose every view is refused (§9.4, D341). After the second review, within v0.8.18: a
  variable's listing reads one join of the members to their units, since DuckDB's plan did not
  end two to-many steps down, and the members of a view's positions are taken once within
  `input_cells`, now 1,000,000 cells (§14, D342); no identifier column's values are handed, declared ones included, nor a value
  whose `where` tests one (§8.4, D342); and what passes a pack's guard, or a limit's error it
  raises, carries none of its text (D343). After the third review, within v0.8.18: a listing's
  time is stated as growing with the unit table's size times its cohorts times its columns, with
  its measured figures, and restricting each variable to the listed members is follow-up #56
  (§14, D342); a `covered` leaf's scope over an identifier column is refused as a value leaf's
  is (D342); and what passes a pack's guard is matched by identity and raised without its
  context (D343).
- **v0.8.17** — `compare.columns`, M3.2's third part (D335–D340): per-category denominators and
  row counts move to a part of their own, M3.2e (#52), for the descriptive analyses, and an
  analysis that assumes independent groups refuses them for good (§9.2, §15); `compare.columns`' parameters,
  values and estimability (§9.5), its methods held to R and its bootstrap drawn from the exact law
  of resampled medians, seeded from the computation id, the column and the position (§9.3); under
  a disclosure setting its categories are merged as a distribution's, its table and differences
  are computed from the rows shown, and a number shows only its units, no test or difference of
  values, which replaces §8.4's linked cohort × category table (§8.4); a materialisation counts
  the units its cohorts share where the analysis assumes independent groups (§7.4); CI's job and
  test steps have time limits of their own (D340).
- **v0.8.16** — `summary.members`, M3.2's second part (D331–D334): a view of it names one cohort
  and pages its members' unit keys by `offset` and `limit`, each key as stored, in the canonical
  form's order (§9.5); it is refused under any disclosure setting, not only for cohorts smaller
  than *k*, since lists of cohorts shown whole give by difference the keys of a set of any size
  (§8.4, §11.1); the reference evaluator and the SQL compiler list keys, read and ordered on the
  server within the answer cap and the call's deadline, and no key reaches the derivation log
  (§14). After the first review, within v0.8.16: a listing reads at most 1,000,000 members and
  takes its page from sorted runs merged lazily, one sort key per member, by the call's deadline
  (§14, D333); applicability calls `summary.members` unavailable under a disclosure setting or
  without row ids (§9.4, D332); datetimes are written with four-digit years, so early ones read
  back on every platform (D333); and CI names a failed test in an annotation (D334). After the
  second review: the refusal names a draft's latest published setting where that binds (D332); a
  page holding a text key longer than a result writes is refused (D331); datetimes are written by
  `isoformat`, and the listing's cost is stated by key type (§14, D333); a canonical-form fix that
  moves ids only with their text is not a semantics bump (§7.6); more than 10 failed tests are
  summarised in one annotation (D334).
- **v0.8.15** — Variables, aggregates and `summary.distribution`, M3.2's first part (D324–D330):
  M3.2 comes in four parts (§15, §10.1); a view reads columns as variables, resolved, canonicalised
  and read back like predicates, with `AGGREGATE_REQUIRED`, `AGGREGATE_NOT_ALLOWED` and `OPEN_SCOPE`
  (§9.2); aggregates pool as §9.2 says in the reference evaluator and the SQL compiler, which groups
  values, picks `max` and `min` and leaves means to the server (§9.3); a query's answer carries
  doubles and text in its value columns, and `run_analysis` materialises variables in its one worker
  run; `summary.distribution`'s parameters, values and estimability (§9.5), its methods held to R;
  categories and histogram bins merge from the left, in results and in the catalogue, and quartiles
  are placed by the merged bins' counts, since brute force found the old rules pinned counts (§8.4);
  and one chart per column (§8.5). After the first review, within v0.8.15: under *k* no statistic of
  the values themselves is reported, only merged bins' counts and quartiles' bins, since values are
  not counts; a category column's undeclared values are one unnamed row under *k*, and the category
  limit depends on the declaration alone; a variable's `values` and `empty` are erased as constants
  on its column; a view reads one variable with one set of bins, and a variable read twice runs
  once; values and standard deviations beyond ±(2^53 − 1) are refused and sums of doubles scaled; a
  run over its seconds or memory names the view; `count: "rows"` is `NOT_SUPPORTED`; and §8.4's
  Limits name distributions' differencing and the catalogue's pooled categories (#49) (D324–D329).
  After the second: a variable's `bins` are erased as constants on its column too, but a `count`'s
  (D325); a call reads a column's values with one set of bins across its views and variables, and
  §8.4's Limits name calls (D328, D329); `max` and `min` are picked in SQL, which §9.3 allows over
  doubles since they compute nothing, and the server reads a `mean`'s rows by the call's deadline
  (D326, D327); chart labels quote values and name bins by their intervals (D330). After the third:
  an ordered category's NOT_APPLICABLE row is skipped by `max` and `min` in SQL as in the evaluator,
  and a property draws multi-valued categories (D326); the server's averaging and `summarise` look
  at the deadline too (D327); the one-set-of-bins rule holds under *k* only, compares the edges a
  histogram takes and keys a `count` by the rows it counts, and a narrow range's repeated edges
  collapse (D328, D329); and §8.4's Limits name variables whose units differ over the same bins.
  After the fourth: the mean is the exact mean correctly rounded, the sum taken exactly over a power
  of two (D326, D328); §8.4's Limits name the answer cap's refusal as data-dependent, the
  catalogue's histogram as another set of edges and a `count`'s rows as keyed by their canonical
  form (D327, D329); the limit on a view's histogram edges is `bin_edges` (D325).
- **v0.8.14** — The analysis registry, phase 2, `run_analysis` and `compare.existence`, M3's
  first slice (D315–D323): M3 comes in five slices (§15); the registry holds the core's entries
  and the packs', matched for applicability (§9.4, `describe_dataset`, `list_analyses`) and served
  as resources, a pack's analysis run from M3.2 (§10.1); phase 2 checks each view against its
  entry and resolves its predicates as cohorts of one clause (§7.6, revising D284);
  `run_analysis` runs a document's views in one worker, refuses cohorts that share units with the
  count of what they share, and issues each result and cohort (§11.1); `compare.existence`'s
  parameters, values and estimability (§9.5), its methods held to R, the disclosure pass for the
  counts an analysis adds and a suppressed value's reason (§8.4), charts without `$schema` (§8.5),
  and `LIFT_DIFFERS` for a view's predicates (§8.3). After the first review, within v0.8.14: each
  predicate's split of a cohort's units is disclosed as a cohort count is, and nothing shown of a
  predicate or its `not` pins a suppressed count (D320, §8.4); issuances of results expire after
  `keep_result_issuances_days` and `run_analysis`'s counts with `count_cohort`'s, and an operator
  prunes them all (D318, §12.2, refining D300); a query two views ask runs once, the units two
  cohorts share are held to a cohort's caps, `level` is at most 1 − 10⁻⁹ (D319), and
  `available_with_caveats` needs `min` confirmed descriptors (D316). After the second review,
  within v0.8.14: with two predicates or more a result shows nothing that combines them (D320,
  §8.4), and whether cohorts share units is named among the Limits; `run_analysis` crosses cohorts
  with predicates in integer counts, whose answer never grows with the units, and prunes results
  alone while counts are kept (D318); the core's analyses of later slices are `NOT_SUPPORTED`,
  and `count_cohort` counts beside them (D317); and Fisher's test no longer rounds a p-value far
  in the tail to 0 (D321). After the third review, within v0.8.14: §8.4 promises no pinning at
  one position, its Limits naming positions that are unions of others and whether a view's
  result was issued; a crossing is one pass per cohort by `GROUPING SETS`, and `query_memory`
  should grow with `query_threads` (D318, §14); a kept result keeps its cohorts' counts (D318,
  §12.2); and D320's brute force runs over marginals at sizes that show counts.
- **v0.8.13** — The read-only catalogue page (D311–D314): `GET /` and `GET /datasets/<id>`,
  rendered by the server from `search_catalog` and `describe_dataset` through the same tool calls,
  as HTML without a script; every text escaped, its control and format characters shown as marked
  escapes, and data in `<bdi>`s; a content security policy that allows its one stylesheet by
  hash, and images and connections to the server only; every answer at the page's paths a page
  (refining D255 and D265); and a link from another site let through request protection
  (refining D257). §11.1 names the page, §12.4 says it is the server's until the React UI of M5,
  §12.5 puts it in `api/`, and §14 says how it renders text and which navigations pass. After the
  first review, the page's refusals are all pages, its responses `no-store`, a link from another
  site opens it (D314), format characters are escaped, and the elements, attributes and links it
  can write are allow-listed, within v0.8.13. After the second, a navigation from another site
  must be the person's (`Sec-Fetch-User: ?1`), page responses carry
  `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin`, and page views have a rate of their own (`page`,
  refining D259) that never spends the agents' `api` rate; links are built from an allow-listed
  character set and an unusable root path is refused; escapes are marked a run at a time. After
  the third, pages are rendered in the call's worker thread, a dataset's page shows at most 50
  tables and what starts from them, preflights at the page's paths spend the `page` rate, texts
  and lists from data are cut, and D311 and D314 state the page's cost and what bounds a hostile
  page (the `page` bucket, not `Sec-Fetch-User`).
- **v0.8.12** — Database snapshots after their second review (D308–D310, amending D305–D307): a
  MySQL snapshot reads its catalogue and rows through the attached catalogue in the scanner's one
  read-only transaction, at an isolation level that reads a snapshot, in UTC, refusing tables of
  engines without transactions, decimals over 38 digits, zero dates and table names that differ
  only in case; every Postgres `numeric` and `money` is read as exact text and its catalogue
  filtered on the server; rows are ordered by their bytes whatever the collation; SQLite's
  `VIRTUAL` generated columns are refused; a driver's failure names its table; names that are not
  Unicode text are refused, or noted, as data tokens (D398), and skipped relations noted up to
  `import_tables`; foreign keys match names ignoring ASCII case alone and are not kept when they
  repeat a column; a connection's file must be in a directory no one else can write, and a
  Postgres URL's query cannot name another server or database. D306 says what reading a SQLite
  file in WAL mode makes beside it. After the third review, within v0.8.12: MySQL database names
  that differ only in case and Unicode-case collisions are refused; a URL holding `#` is refused
  and a Postgres query read as libpq reads it; foreign keys on the same columns in another order
  are noted; a partition of another schema's table is noted; arrays of `numeric` and `money` are
  read as lists of text; SQLite's `-0.0` and `0.0` are ordered by sign; the worker names at most
  `import_tables` skipped relations and lists SQLite's from `sqlite_schema`; a connection's file
  must be in a directory the server's user or root owns, and refusals name it by its location.
  After the fourth review, within v0.8.12: a server's URL is read by the server's own grammar and
  the driver given a connection string built from what it names (D310); MySQL name collisions
  follow the server's collation; partitioned tables with a foreign partition are skipped and
  noted, partitions judged by their root; SQLite's virtual tables are found by their root page;
  a connection's file must be the server's user's or root's. After the fifth review, within
  v0.8.12: a server's database and user are bounded as the server keeps names, and the snapshot
  reads back the database its session opened, refusing another, from which the provenance is
  recorded (D310); numeric hosts must be IPv4 addresses in dotted decimal, and hosts are recorded
  in canonical form; no persistent secret is read; a connection's file others can write is
  refused; a schema holding `/` is refused; SQLite virtual tables are found by their `sql` too,
  and Postgres partitions pending detach are noted (D309). After the sixth review, within
  v0.8.12: MySQL names DuckDB's scanner cannot quote safely (a backtick, a backslash, a control
  character, and a `'` in the database) are refused before any row is read, since a crafted
  table name read another database's rows (D308). After the seventh review, within v0.8.12: a
  Postgres session resolves names in `pg_catalog` alone with `row_security` off, its catalogue
  queries name `pg_catalog` throughout, and tables with row security or virtual generated
  columns are noted and not read (D309, D310); a MySQL `localhost` needs `socket`, a `?` in any
  MySQL value is refused, the URL's database must be a `SCHEMATA` name exactly, and a view's
  name is not held to the scanner's quoting (D308, D310). After the eighth review, within
  v0.8.12: a Postgres table whose column's read path would run a function outside `pg_catalog`
  is skipped, the session fixes every output setting, each base table is locked `ACCESS SHARE`
  before its columns are read, and a zoned time is read as text or refused (D306, D309, D310).
  After the ninth review, within v0.8.12: `quote_all_identifiers` is fixed too, a multirange is
  followed to its range, a `timetz[]` is read as text, an ordinary table is locked `ONLY`, and a
  lock waited for past half of `reader_seconds` is refused naming its table (D306, D309, D310).
  Round 9's follow-up, within v0.8.12: since a cast lookup ignores the transaction's snapshot, a
  cast a schema owner adds mid-snapshot could still run; every Postgres column of a type outside
  `pg_catalog` is now read through the type's output function, resolving no cast, and only a user
  base type is skipped (D309). After the tenth review, within v0.8.12: an installed extension's
  type (`citext`, `hstore`…) is read through its output function too, and only a base type whose
  I/O is neither `pg_catalog`'s nor an extension's is skipped (D309).
- **v0.8.11** — Database snapshots (D305–D307): named connections as import sources, resolved
  when the import runs, with provenance of host, database and schema only; snapshots read in the
  import's worker process in one transaction, SQLite with SQLite's own defensive reader rather
  than DuckDB's scanner (a deviation from §13.1's "through DuckDB"), DuckDB files hardened, and
  Postgres and MySQL through DuckDB's signed scanners, bundled as wheels, in a session locked
  once it has attached them; declared keys, foreign keys, types and comments `imported`. §5.2
  says what a database's location is, §10.1 what a validator's source is, §12.4 the stack, §13.1
  what a snapshot imports, and §14 how SQLite files and snapshots are read. After the first
  review, a server's rows are read on the server by the catalogue's names (`ONLY` for a Postgres
  parent), decimals wider than 38 digits as their exact text, MySQL's `TINYINT(1)` as an integer
  through one connection; foreign keys to another schema or to a column that is not the
  parent's key are noted; DuckDB's generated columns and control characters in comments are
  refused; and the files beside a SQLite or DuckDB file, and those the reader opens, confined.
- **v0.8.10** — Choices the query tools settle (D296–D304): readbacks from templates, stating every
  step, condition, quantifier, lift rule, unit, coverage, record filter, parent scope and negation
  in the canonical order; the disclosure pass over cohort counts, with `NOT_ESTIMABLE` for an empty
  unit table and the digest after the pass (refines D288); outputs checking their own digests;
  `validate_document`, `count_cohort` and `explain`, with their requests, outputs, refusal paths
  and issuances; where the query tools run (amends D285's last sentence); document translators;
  and documents in tool requests. §7.7 says what a readback is a function of, §8.1 what
  `count_cohort` returns and that digests are checked, §8.4 the linked sets of a cohort count,
  §8.6 where a tool's document refusals point, §10.1 the translator's contract, §11.1 the three
  tools, and §14 where canonicalisation runs. Review round 1 amended D296, D297 and D300–D303,
  D285's last sentence and D289's issuance ids within v0.8.10, adding no numbers: a unit table of
  1 to *k* − 1 units suppresses its size and, under max(*k*, 4) units, every count, and one more
  count is suppressed wherever what is shown would pin one (§8.4); a call's issuances are recorded
  in one transaction only while it can be answered, its request stored once in the log's texts
  (migration 5), the log pruned by `[log]`'s period and by `aibi prune`, and bounded by
  `log_bytes` (§12.2, §14); the queries end by the call's deadline, and the work around them is
  bounded by the document's limits (D301); `explain` gives neither an issuance's request nor
  when a derivation was recorded, the operator router serving the request, and issuance ids are
  not guessable (§7.3, §8.4 Limits); pack summaries go by leaf key, and keys' parts and the
  release's label are data tokens (§7.7); every negation of a translation is flagged (§7.1,
  §10.1); `count_cohort` is not read-only. Review round 2 amended D297, D300, D301 and D302,
  D200's check of two suppressed counts and D289's rules on removing derivations and on ids
  within v0.8.10, adding no numbers: the pass treats `n_true` and `n_false` alike, suppresses
  any count beside a small complement and a suppressed count's partner with it, so that a
  cohort's `not`, `known` and `unknown`, counted with it, pin none of its counts under *k* of 3
  or more, though other cohorts of the call can (§8.4); `log_bytes` counts
  every row pruning can free, issuances' included, and pruning removes a derivation no
  issuance names, a batch at a time, in a thread of the log's own (§12.2, §14); issuance ids
  are drawn as they are recorded and increase across restarts; a run the call's deadline ends
  names `tool_seconds`; an issuance's request is asked for in a request's body; a server without
  `/proc` serves without query workers. Review round 3 amended D297, D300 and D302, and D200's
  check of `lift_differs`, within v0.8.10, adding no numbers: `lift_differs` is suppressed with
  any count of its cohort, which bound it, and a two-valued collapse of a cohort is named among
  what the pass does not stop (§8.4 Limits); `log_bytes` counts the pages rows can take, and
  `keep_count_issuances_days` is at most 36,500 (§12.2); `not_issued` means not in the log, and
  a cohort's status tells whether it was counted within the retention period; `aibi-server check`
  and `serve` say when `count_cohort` is disabled for want of `/proc`; and `count_cohort` records
  nothing once an erasure of a dataset it names is recorded after it began canonicalising (D300,
  as D290 requires). Restacked onto #35's sign-off and #34's round 6, D300 is amended within
  v0.8.10: the datasets a call names are those its issuances name as D290 has it, through their
  derivations or in the document as written and its parameters. Review round 4 amended D297,
  D300 and D200's check of `lift_differs` within v0.8.10, adding no numbers: `lift_differs` is
  suppressed under every *k*, a 0 included, since the units it counts lie on one side of the
  cohort, and `known X`, counted with `X` in one call, shows that side beside counts all large
  (§8.4); the app DB refuses to open with pages of another size than `log_bytes` charges; and a
  test holds that `count_cohort` takes its erasure mark before it pins and canonicalises.
  Restacked onto #34's sign-off, whose vacuum after every redaction leaves no copy of a redacted
  text of the log in the app DB. Review round 5 signed it off, amending D297 and D300 within
  v0.8.10, adding no numbers: complements within a breakdown are not linked, which is
  differencing (§8.4 Limits); a tool call's pins are released without waiting on the store, and
  the sweep, the redactions and the vacuum their release allows run in the store's housekeeping
  thread, never in the call's; `aibi-server serve` exits with the store's message on an app DB
  of another page size; and a test holds the charge for a derivation's releases over
  derivations of 64 releases.
- **v0.8.9** — Choices the three-valued SQL compiler settles (D291–D295): one relation per node and
  table holding each row's truth value, reasons and flags as integers and bits, combinators as
  stacked aggregates, existence questions and coverage listings aggregated per parent row;
  descriptor names only where blobs are read, every value bound, and comparisons exact as the
  evaluator makes them; DuckDB sessions locked and confined to the blobs, in a fresh worker
  process per document with limits on its time, memory and number (`[queries]`), and the server
  never loading DuckDB; integer aggregates only (refines §9.3 with `BIT_OR` and `BOOL_OR`); and the
  differential tests. §9.3 names the new aggregates, §12.2 how the query engine reads blobs and
  what an issuance records, §13.3 that every evaluated document is compiled too, and §14 the
  session's settings and the three new limits (`query_seconds`, `query_memory`,
  `query_workers`). Review round 1 of #35 amended D291, D292, D293 and D295, §12.2 and §14 within
  v0.8.9, adding no numbers: `exclude_self` counts each parent's children once and takes away a
  row's own part, and membership is a semi-join on the bound list; a child that closes its socket
  is waited for and judged by how it ended, its CPU limit sends `SIGXCPU` before `SIGKILL`, it
  asks the OOM killer to pick it first and has an address-space backstop, and no worker runs
  without `/proc` to watch it; its session may open the document's blobs alone and runs one
  `SELECT` per statement; a run ends by its caller's deadline; answers are packed integers of at
  most 64 MiB (a fourth limit, `query_answer_bytes`); faults name the error's class, never
  DuckDB's message; the SQL path verifies blobs as loading does; and the differential tests also
  run on random schemas. Review round 2 of #35 signed it off, amending D293 and D295 within
  v0.8.9: each query states its rows' number of columns, which its answer must hold, the answer is
  parsed under the deadline, and truth values are made as they are read; rows a query cannot give
  are a fault; a caller's deadline ends a run as `CallerDeadline`, and one already past starts no
  child; the blob cache knows a file by its change time too; a process that leaves the child's
  process group is not followed; and the random schemas have second relationships for sibling
  paths.
- **v0.8.8** — Choices the canonical form, ids, digests and the derivation log settle (D281–D290):
  sorting by serialisation as UTF-16 code units, with resolution's removal of duplicates making
  one sort the fixpoint, and the canonical form read back as a document; the objects cohort ids,
  computation ids and leaf keys hash, and the semantics version, 1; digests and rounding; views
  reported unchecked until M3, and a view's ids from their parts, ordered without the disclosure
  setting; pack leaves (their schemas, the shared budget of steps, compiling once per release,
  expansions checked and bounded, refusals placed at the leaf, summaries); pack versions; caveat
  rules; the digested part of a cohort count; the derivation log's tables, what an id resolves
  to, pruning; and erasure of the log. §7.3 says how a pack leaf is checked and expanded, §7.6
  gives the semantics version, the sort, step 1's packs, reading the form back, caveat rules and
  views until M3, §9.3 the rounding, §10.1 the leaf kind's and caveat rule's contracts, §12.2
  pruning and what ids resolve to, and §14 the three new limits (`pack_leaves`,
  `expansion_values`, `pack_leaf_steps`). Review round 1 of #34 amended D281, D283, D285, D287,
  D289 and D290, §7.3, §7.6, §9.3, §10.1 and §12.2 within v0.8.8, adding no numbers: erasure
  matches only a derivation's data, and numbers only where they name the person's rows; a pack's
  exceptions are `PACK_FAILED`, its code gets copies and no release label; the read-back escapes
  `$`; the log's triggers hold against every write; a new *unknown release* status; monotonic
  issuance ids; an overflowing rounding is refused; slow packs are unbounded until the query
  tools. Review round 2 amended D285, D287, D289 and D290 and §12.2 within v0.8.8, adding no
  numbers: erasure decides a constant by its JSON type and its term's datatype, so a text key
  that reads as a number is matched on every column; the issuances it redacts keep what names
  no one; a result's clause parameters are matched as clauses, and concept units' keys as a
  holding table's; a cohort's releases are its object's, and a derivation over a withdrawn
  release takes no issuance; the log's triggers ask for permits and refuse issuances of erased
  derivations; pruning compares instants; packs see copies of descriptors, and a `MemoryError`
  is not a pack's failure. Review round 3 amended D285, D287, D289 and D290 and §12.2 within
  v0.8.8, adding no numbers: erasure matches terms and constants by value under every datatype
  they may be of, so a datetime is erased in every spelling of its instant and a number in every
  spelling of its value, and unit keys given through a parameter as inline ones are; a column
  given as a parameter or a value concept names rows; a derivation's release is listed under
  its manifest's dataset; `prune` takes RFC 3339 only and writes four-digit years; each pack
  leaf's compile and each caveat rule gets a view of its own that copies a descriptor when it
  is read, and a pack without a rule none; redaction runs only within a transaction and reads
  the log a batch at a time. Review round 4 amended D223, D285 and D290 and §12.2 within
  v0.8.8, adding no numbers: erasure matches a value as its place says (a table in §12.2): by
  every reading, read by the engine's constant reader too, where it may name the person, and
  by its text alone, never as a number, on a column known to name none of their rows; free text
  reads more spellings of instants and the person's numbers; each item of a list in an
  identifier column is a term; the M1 redactors' matching by value is D223's; a pack's view
  holds the release's descriptors only in closures, and a redaction leaves no compiled pattern
  of its terms cached. Review round 5 amended D290 and §12.2 within v0.8.8, adding no numbers:
  a coverage scope's column is its table's, and a scope of a table given as a parameter or a
  concept names rows; a cohort as written over another dataset is matched as *other*; a
  result's clause parameters are matched as clauses alone; a `:` or `/` joins a number only to
  a digit, grouped digits are no number, and by text alone a date followed by a time, or a term
  in a range, is no token; a pack leaf's booleans are matched as a naming place's; a name and
  every reference to it are redacted alike; derivations are read a batch at a time. Review round
  6 amended D290 and §12.2 within v0.8.8, adding no numbers: a naming place holds only the terms
  of the tables whose rows it names, text terms too; a view's constants are over the cohorts it
  names; a document's own parameters, and a cohort's `datasets` given as a parameter, may name
  the dataset; a parameter referred to from several places is matched as in each; terms are
  found in text by tokens looked up in sets; and erasure is one pass over the log as it stands.
  Review round 7 amended D223 and D290 and §12.2 within v0.8.8, adding no numbers: erasure's
  matching follows one principle, that a constant goes exactly when it could name one of the
  person's rows, set out as a table keyed by where it sits: a key or foreign key column matches
  the keys of the person's rows in the table it names and nothing else, a unit key as a whole,
  a part of a composite key only through it, an identifier column the values that identify the
  person from any table, and a number in other units as evaluation converts it; text is
  compared in normalisation form C without format characters; a number in free text is parsed
  only when its digits could be a term's; a clause or unit key given as a parameter is matched
  as the cohorts that refer to it are. Review round 8 signed #34 off, with no blocker and no
  major, and amended D223 and D290 and §12.2 within v0.8.8, adding no numbers: a constant goes
  when it could name one of the person's rows or quotes one of their own identifying texts, so a
  key or foreign key column also matches the texts that identify the person, by their text
  alone, while a number there still matches only the keys of the table it names; a number given
  through a parameter is read only as the places that refer to it read it; and a vacuum that
  fails leaves its redaction pending, recorded in the redaction's own transaction and retried
  when the store opens and when a pin is released.
- **v0.8.7** — Choices the catalogue, its statistics and the tools over MCP settle (D270–D280):
  catalogue statistics counted at every build; their disclosure (refines §8.4: a distribution is
  suppressed with its PRESENT count, and a list column's pooled row has no count); statistic
  references' pointers and floors (refines D202); the catalogue index in the app DB; searching
  the catalogue; describing a dataset, with its standing caveats, and a column; the curation queue
  and proposals as tools, a proposal naming its agent; the MCP transport, stateless and behind
  request protection, with an upload's deadlines on bodies, the document loader's rules and a wall-clock
  limit on calls; descriptors as resources; and the tools over HTTP. §8.1 gives the pointers of
  `stat:` references, §8.4 the two refinements, §11.1 the `agent` argument, the standing caveats
  and the transports, §12.2 the statistics and the index, §12.4 the SDK's version, and §14 the
  tools' limits. Review round 1 of #33 amended D271 and D273–D280 within v0.8.7, adding no
  numbers: no curation evidence is served under *k*, the queue's notes are disclosed with the
  statistics and in fixed words, and `n_rows` is suppressed only when it is small (D271, D277); a
  draft is disclosed under the published setting too, and release refusals name `/release` and the
  labels (D275, D279); values an output cannot carry are `unrepresentable` (D271, D276); entries
  are written only while their release is latest (D273); completeness compares rationals (D274);
  proposals have a rate per client and agents a share of the open ones (D277); the transport checks
  the JSON-RPC envelope and refuses secrets before the SDK, filters the root and the SDK's loggers,
  holds a call's place until its thread returns (`tool_calls`), gives public bodies a shorter idle
  time (`tool_body_idle_seconds`) and needs the SDK 1.30 (D278, D280); §8.4, §11.1 and §14 say so.
  Review round 2 amended D270, D271, D277, D278 and D280 within v0.8.7, adding no numbers: a
  release without catalogue statistics is refused, not counted (D270); importers write no counts
  into descriptor text, and declared zeros bound pooled values (D271, §8.4, §10.1); agents' share
  is 5,000, a client's 500, and an operator rejects a proposer's open proposals at once, while the
  public queue drops hidden text before its byte budget (D277, §11.1, §11.2); one client holds at
  most 2 of the tools' places, small messages are read off them, and one 30 s covers a message's
  reading and its call (D278, D280, §14).
- **v0.8.6** — Choices the operator router and request protection settle (D253–D269): server
  configuration; binding and TLS; one protection middleware; the Host allow-list; the Origin
  allow-list and fetch metadata; CORS; rate limits; request sizes and bodies; the curator token;
  operator names; CSRF tokens; the operator router; refusals over HTTP; imports and uploads over
  HTTP; session handles over HTTP and in the CLI; the operator CLI; erasure and proposers on the
  operator router (amends §11.2). §11.2 says where the token and the operator's name travel, which
  requests are a browser's, and adds uploads, erasure and the proposers to the operator's
  operations; §14 gives the order of request protection, the loopback interface, forwarded headers
  and the body limits; §12.4 and §12.5 name uvicorn and httpx, and the `api/` and `operator/`
  packages. The first review's fixes amend D254, D255, D259 and D261–D269 and §14 within this
  version, adding no numbers: the token is verified before any failure limit, and requests
  without it take nothing from the operator's rate; preflights count against the `api` rate;
  tokens are found in URLs once percent-decoded; logged paths, and refusals of a request, blank
  token and handle shapes; operator names have one encoding and no bidi formatting; uploads take
  a `concurrent_imports` place; the CLI takes handles from standard input or `AIBI_HANDLE`, and
  erasure keys from standard input or a prompt, never from arguments. The second review's fixes
  amend D253, D254, D262 and D264–D268 and §14 within this version, again adding no numbers: operator
  names of a token's or a handle's shape are refused; every refusal the server answers blanks
  those shapes; the CLI refuses arguments of those shapes and datasets that are not identifiers
  before any request, and quotes no value in a usage error; a stalled upload is refused after
  `upload_idle_seconds`, freeing its place; a dataset with no label is `UNKNOWN_DATASET` on every
  route that names one; the configuration file's owner and directory are checked; the access
  log blanks those shapes in a record of any shape. The third review's fixes amend D253–D255,
  D259, D260, D262, D264–D266 and D268 and §14 within this version, again adding no numbers: each
  connection has a deadline for its request head, idle connections make room, and each client
  holds at most a share of the connections; an upload has a deadline its length sets; the
  configuration's path is resolved once and the file opened without following a link and
  checked on its descriptor; every log, exceptions included, blanks secret shapes; text a
  request stores, and operator names and CLI arguments once percent-decoded, are refused those
  shapes; bodies are parsed off the event loop; `imports.concurrent` is at most 20; the CLI
  reports any HTTP error as unreachable; IPv6 clients are rate-limited by their /64; D260's
  reason is corrected. The fourth review's fixes amend D253–D255, D259, D261, D262, D265, D266 and
  D268 and §14 within this version, again adding no numbers: a client that takes too little of
  the answers waiting for it is cut off (`send_seconds`), and uvicorn is bound to its tested minor
  version; input is refused only for a token's or a handle's shape standing alone, so ordinary
  long names that hold one inside a word are stored and named, while logs and refusals still
  blank the shape anywhere; uploads declare their length (`LENGTH_REQUIRED`), and JSON bodies
  have an upload's deadlines; D254 says what the per-client cap does behind a proxy and what a
  TLS handshake escapes; link-local and NAT64 clients are keyed by their own address. The fifth
  review's fixes amend D254, D259, D261 and D265 and §14 within this version, again adding no
  numbers: what a client took of the answers waiting for it is what its end acknowledged, so a
  client that pipelines and reads steadily but slowly keeps its connection; D254 says when
  uvicorn answers 503 for its tasks; refusals blank a secret's shape once percent-decoded too;
  the configured token is refused at `/operator` and in stored text wherever it sits; clients of
  the local-use NAT64 prefix are keyed by their whole address.
- **v0.8.5** — Choices the release lifecycle, curation sessions and the proposal queue settle
  (D236–D252): operation slots; imports that publish (amends D235); previous names on re-import;
  carry-forward; tombstones; the re-import gate; re-import notes; descriptor versions; sessions
  and handles; edits; the checks on a change; descriptor-write checks; proposals; curation
  proposers (amends §5.1); the curation queue; draft states and *discarded*; the audit trail.
  §5.1 gives a pack's proposer its `by`, §10.1 says when proposers run, §12.3 adds the operation
  slot, the re-import gate and the recorded draft states, and §13.2 says where proposals are
  dropped. Amended before release: D239–D241 (an absent inference consumes a tombstone; a new
  descriptor's failing proposal goes alone; no tombstone of other inferences is kept for a
  descriptor that stays out); D247 (a step budget for extension schemas that does not grow with
  the schema, capped lower for proposals than for operators' and importers' writes and refused as
  `LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, `extension_steps`; errors that keep no copy of the value; linear
  `uniqueItems`, `unevaluatedItems` and `unevaluatedProperties`; checks of only the descriptors a
  change or a proposal touches, and of every descriptor when a session publishes, as D244 now
  says); D248 (what the proposal cap leaves to operators; an accepted proposal is one the draft
  still holds; it amends D119's evidence); D250 (a byte budget, and the release and the draft
  pinned under the store's lock); and §12.3's sentence on an accepted proposal's evidence.
- **v0.8.4** — Choices the file importers settle (D224–D235): detecting parse settings; sources
  and typed values; table names; the statuses an importer sets; column inference; keys,
  relationships and roles; the validation gate; the import report; confinement; archives and
  limits; the upload area; pack imports. Erasure follows the relationships of every published
  release (amends D223), since the gate drops a proposed relationship a re-import breaks. §12.4 and
  §13.1 note that `.xls` is refused in v1 (D225). Workbooks and Parquet files are read in a
  worker process with memory, CPU, time, decoded-text and concurrency limits, whose answers the
  server reads under the deadline, and a typed cell holds a text field's 131,072 characters at
  most (D225, D233, §14). A worker's peak memory is its `VmPeak`, sampled while an answer is
  awaited and sent with a panic, never its peak resident size on Linux, which holds the
  server's (D225).
- **v0.8.3** — Choices the store settles (D213–D223): release manifests; raw snapshots; parsing
  text; typing cells and list cells; table blobs; derived columns; rebuilds; pins and the sweep;
  the app DB; erasure.
- **v0.8.2** — Choices the reference evaluator settles (D203–D212): unit conversion and big
  integers in doubles; lists that are not PRESENT, unlisted values of ordered categories and
  datetime precision; what a mention is; grouped listings and `covered` flags; parent scopes in
  v1; `lift_differs` and the fields read; units and unit keys; implicit paths and their search;
  `exclude_self` under trailing lookups; caps on the canonical form and on `cohort` leaves.
- **v0.8.1** — Encodings settled by the M0 document, descriptor and result schemas (D188–D202):
  parsing rules for text, numbers and nesting; verbatim notes and parameter values; bounds on
  substitution and on the refusals returned; id lengths and re-import by occurrence; the static
  cross-dataset checks; descriptor refusals, release rules and limits; values compared as JSON
  values; undeclared coverage and entry by absence; typed declared ranges; who sets a status;
  JSON-safe descriptors; whole releases; result invariants on construction, pack API names and
  statistic references (D200–D202).
- **v0.8** — Fourth review round, verifying v0.7 (D176–D187): canonicalisation to a fixpoint;
  conditions that must be TRUE for an intermediate FALSE; flags of dropped children; event-free
  units left out of Cox fits; totals, pooling order, small-count gates and empty bins under
  disclosure; precedence of estimability rows; pins for reused blobs and mutual exclusion of
  imports, withdrawals and sessions; ids kept across re-imports.
- **v0.7** — Third review round (D149–D175): existence questions canonicalised as chains of
  single-step questions instead of merged; intermediate questions, relevance and flags through
  nested questions in §6.5; record filters under `every`; every tuple defined; survival curves,
  histograms and suppression across the whole output under disclosure; Cox estimability,
  degenerate inputs, the median at exactly 0.5, time-zero rows, per-variable exclusions, the
  cross-dataset hazard ratio and covariate coding in §9; withdrawal per manifest, tombstones,
  erasure by re-import, pins during deletion and canonical string forms in §12; database
  snapshots under hardened DuckDB; identifier collisions.
- **v0.6** — Second review round, of v0.4 and v0.5 (D110–D148): request protection on every
  router; raw snapshots of text files as bytes plus parse settings; re-import carry-forward,
  session handles, one deletion rule and labels that are never reused; core time origins reduced
  to domain-neutral ones; coverage per relationship; the pack API contract; model cards and
  server-set attribution; a result cache of digested content only; path steps, per-step
  quantifiers and merged nested existence; exactly specified hashed objects and result ids over
  whole views; segments, the data wrapper, cohort counts and refusals defined; closedness reasons,
  flags and the order of `covered` in §6.5; record filters as conjunctions; an estimability
  table, bootstrap order statistics, the log-rank test with delayed entry and strata, and the PH
  test and median implemented directly; the DuckDB carve-out for determinism; linked-set
  suppression, and distributions and survival curves under disclosure; the caveat table aligned.
- **v0.5** — First review round of v0.4 (D73–D109): operator surface; blob store, raw snapshots,
  withdrawal and erasure; import hardening and resource limits; identity scheme; structured text;
  pack hooks and results versions; derivations and issuances; time origins as concepts; model
  cards, derived columns and the core concepts; closedness caveats, scope rules, lift reasons,
  unknown parent scopes and leaf-level negation in §6.5; aggregates, estimability, stratification,
  overlap, named statistical methods, disclosure rules and determinism in §8–§9; delayed entry;
  exclusion accounting; semantics and results versions in ids; two-phase canonicalisation.
- **v0.4** — Rewritten for consistency, with each rule stated once; third review (D44–D72).
- **v0.3.2** — Second consistency review (D33–D43).
- **v0.3.1** — First consistency review (D20–D32).
- **v0.3** — Decisions from the walkthrough (D1–D19); Cox regression, engine-computed effect
  sizes, observation windows, cohort references, `count_cohort`.
- **v0.2** — Domain-agnostic core; oncology moved into a pack; principle P8.
- **v0.1** — First draft.
